  - `StationChange` has only `set_max_sessions` (at most the station.toml value) and `set_offline_approval_policy`. Allowed roots, provider instances, shells and setup commands stay station-local (doctrine: the station is sovereign over what it may touch; a deck admin key must not widen file access).
  - `KeyRotation { old_key, new_key, signature }`; the signed message is `fleetdeck-rotate-key-v1`, old key, new key, receiver fingerprint, `command_id`, each ended by a zero byte, so a signature is valid for one peer and one command.
  - `CoordinatorCall { caller, token_digest, op }` with `CoordinatorOp` spawn / send / stop / assign. The token stays on the station; the deck matches the SHA-256 digest. Phase 6 may add ops; an unknown op or field fails closed (`deny_unknown_fields`, `invalid_command`).
  - `target_incarnation` is optional only in `CommandEnvelope` (station-local socket, DL-038).
  - The admission check moved to `command::admit` and is shared by every envelope.
- Reason: an untyped payload cannot be checked against its method, and session commands do not fit station settings, keys or fleet intent; typed envelopes keep the receipt rules identical for every holder and let a test compare the ADR table with the code table.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-042: Link base in `fleetdeck-link` (M2 P3-1)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 14)
- Context: P3-1 moves the spike link (`spikes/s4-link`) into `crates/fleetdeck-link` with the Revision 2 rules of ADR-004 §9.1 (G1, G10). The ADR names one `link_epochs(peer_key, epoch)` column for "the dialer's counter and the listener's highest accepted epoch", and it does not say where the link table lives or what a link setup returns before the control and data protocols (P3-2, P3-3) exist.
- Options:
  1. Copy the spike link module whole (setup, control, data, pairing) and change it in place.
  2. Build the setup layer first: `node::dial` / `node::listen` return an `OpenLink` (authenticated control and data WebSockets, `LinkId { epoch, attempt_id, dialer }`, a `closing` watch and a `TableGuard`); the control, data and pairing protocols are added on top in P3-2 to P3-4.
- Choice: 2.
- Details:
  - `epochs::Epochs` keeps two columns per peer: `dial_epoch` (incremented and committed before each dial) and `accepted_epoch` (the listener's highest accepted epoch). One column would mix the two directions of a reciprocal pair. `LINK_EPOCHS_SQL` is the table for the station.db and deck.db migrations; `Epochs::in_memory` serves tests and tools.
  - The dialer dials one pinned key (`PeerPolicy::Fingerprint`), sends the epoch and a fresh 128-bit `attempt_id` on every channel of the attempt, and on `stale_link_epoch { min_epoch }` raises its counter to at least `min_epoch` and dials once more.
  - The listener commits the accepted epoch, then admits the link into the node-wide `LinkTable` and only then sends `Ready`. A data channel binds to the entry with the same peer, epoch and `attempt_id` that the peer dialed; any other gets `no_control_channel`.
  - The dialer admits its link after the control `Ready` and before it opens the data channel. Both nodes apply the same rule (`table::wins`): same dialer → higher epoch; opposite dialers → the link dialed by the lower fingerprint. The loser gets `superseded` (refused before `Ready`, or told through `closing` after it).
  - `TableGuard` is created by the insert and removes the entry on drop only when the generation still matches (G10). Replacing an entry drops its data slot, so a superseded setup wait ends at once.
  - The pair channel answers "pairing is not open on this listener" until P3-4. `dial_with(DialFault)` is a hidden test hook for failure injection at each setup step.
  - `Hello` gains `attempt_id` and `incarnation`; `Close` gains `min_epoch`; version negotiation uses `fleetdeck_proto::version`.
- Reason: the setup layer is where G1 and G10 live, and it can be tested alone over loopback (TCP or the FIFO stand-in, DL-025); the spike's control and data tasks change with G2, G6, G8 and G9 anyway, so they move in their own items.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-043: Control channel of `fleetdeck-link` (M2 P3-2)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 15)
- Context: P3-2 implements the control channel with the Revision 2 rules (ADR-004 §9.4, §9.6, §9.7: G6, G7, G8). The ADR fixes the checks, the limits and the reason codes, but not where scopes live, how the caller treats a request that it may not send, which close reason a refused stream frame gets, or what "injected clock" means for the heartbeat test.
- Options:
  1. Copy the spike task (one `select!` that also awaits socket writes and application delivery) and add the checks.
  2. Three tasks per channel: a reader (decode, check, deliver), a writer (owns the socket sink, every write bounded), and a supervisor (end conditions and heartbeat) that never awaits a queue or the socket.
- Choice: 2.
- Details:
  - `Scope` moves to `fleetdeck_proto::link` (the board shows grants; TS type `link/Scope.ts`). The method table `fleetdeck_link::methods::METHODS` has `Callers` (deck → station, station → deck, either), scope, feature and mutating flag; `tests/method_table.rs` parses the ADR §9.6 table and compares it with the code table (G7). A unit test checks that exactly the mutating methods have a typed envelope (`fleetdeck_proto::mutation::envelope_of`, DL-041).
  - Roles are the ones bound at pairing: this node's role and `node.peer_role()` (setup admits only keys pinned with that role, DL-042). Grants come from a `GrantSource` trait read for every inbound request (in-memory `Grants` now; the station and deck stores implement it in P3-5/P3-6, where the mutating decision re-reads them in its transaction, §9.5).
  - The caller pre-checks its own requests (method, role, feature, `command_id`, open count) and answers a refused request locally with `Outcome::Err` without sending it; only the receiver checks scopes. `send` accepts only stream frames and checks their direction and feature.
  - An inbound stream frame in the wrong direction closes the link with `wrong_role`; a snapshot frame without `snapshots.v1`, a binary message, bad JSON, a response to an unknown id and a repeated open request id close it with `protocol_error`.
  - Limits: 128 open requests on the link, both directions together (corrected in round 16, DL-044; round 15 counted each direction) (own: refused locally with `too_many_requests`; inbound: answered with `too_many_requests`); outbound and application queues of 256; each socket write ≤ 10 s; the final `close` frame and socket close ≤ 1 s (`CLEANUP_LIMIT`), then the writer is aborted. The heartbeat `ping` (every 15 s) uses `try_send`, so a full queue skips it; any received message resets the 45 s idle limit, which closes the link with `timeout`.
  - The application gets `Inbound::{Request, Frame}`; the end of the link is `Control::ended()/closed()` (the inbound queue then returns `None`), so delivery of the end never waits for queue capacity. The supervisor owns the link's `TableGuard`, so the table entry goes with the channel. Dropping every `Control` handle closes the link (`closed`).
  - The injected clock of the G8 tests is tokio's paused test clock over in-memory WebSocket pairs (`tokio::io::duplex`); the channel is generic over the socket for that reason. The data channel stays a plain `Ws` in `LiveLink` until P3-3 ties it to the control channel.
- Reason: a reader that waits for the application, or a writer that waits for a blackholed peer, can then never delay a supersession or a shutdown (G8); the checks match the ADR order exactly and are tested over in-memory pairs and real loopback links.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-044: Request limit per link, and the data channel of `fleetdeck-link` (M2 round 16, P3-3)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 16)
- Context: (a) The round-15 review found that the control channel counted the 128-request limit of G8 per direction, but ADR-004 §9.7 says "at most 128 requests can be outstanding on one link". (b) P3-3 implements the data channel (ADR-004 §2.4, §2.7, §9.2: G2, G6, G9). The ADR fixes the frame format, the budgets and the reason codes, but not the API, how many tokens a node can hold, how a token reaches the opener, or which reason pending writes get when the two connections end in a different order.
- Options for (a): 1. keep a limit per direction (256 on the link); 2. one counter for both directions.
- Options for (b): 1. copy the spike's mux task (one `select!` that awaits socket writes); 2. a sans-IO `mux::Mux` (frames, ids, windows, budgets) driven by one task per channel plus a bounded writer task, like the control channel (DL-043).
- Choice: (a) 2; (b) 2.
- Details:
  - (a) `Pending::open()` = own open requests + inbound open requests. The caller refuses its own request locally and the receiver answers an inbound request with `too_many_requests` when 128 are open on the link. When both sides send at the same moment at 127, at most one of the two crossing requests opens; the other gets `too_many_requests` (regression tests `simultaneous_requests_from_both_sides_never_open_more_than_128`, `at_127_open_requests_only_one_of_two_crossing_requests_opens`).
  - (b) Frames are `[kind u8][stream u32 BE][payload]`: open (token), data (1..=64 KiB; an empty data frame is `protocol_error`), credit (u32), close, reset (reason). The dialer opens odd ids, the listener even ids; ids only grow and are never reused; frames for finished ids and resets of unknown streams are ignored without an event (G9).
  - Budgets per link: 64 open streams (both sides together; a crossing open at the limit is reset with `flow_control_violation`), a 256 KiB window per stream and direction (credit only after the application calls `consumed`), 4 MiB of received bytes not yet consumed, counted also after a reset of their stream. A peer over a budget ends the link with `flow_control_violation`.
  - Tokens (`tokens::StreamTokens`, one per node): 128-bit random hex, bound to the link's `attempt_id` and a purpose, valid 30 s, single use (a redeem removes the token also when it is refused), at most 4096 open. A stream with an unknown, used, foreign or expired token is reset with `bad_stream_token` and the application sees nothing. The token reaches the opener in the response of a control request (`artifact.open`), so the scope check of the method table guards each stream.
  - `Data::write` returns when the last chunk is queued for the socket (it waits for credit); a reset (by either side) or the end of the channel fails the pending writes of the stream at once with the reason.
  - One lifetime (`live::start`, `tie`): the end of the control channel ends the data channel with the same reason. When the data channel ends first, a mux violation closes the control channel with its reason; otherwise the control channel waits up to 500 ms (`DATA_LOSS_GRACE`) for the peer's `close` frame and then closes with `data_channel_lost`. When the peer closes the data socket, the data channel waits up to 500 ms for the control end, so pending writes fail with the link's reason (for example `superseded`), not with `eof`. Dropping the application's data handles keeps the link.
- Reason: a per-direction limit allows 256 open requests, which the ADR does not allow. The sans-IO mux can be tested exhaustively (property tests for `decode` and `receive`), and the task layout keeps G8 (no shutdown waits for a blocked peer) for the data channel too.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-045: Invitation id, pairing admission and reconnect in `fleetdeck-link` (M2 rounds 17–18, P3-4)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 18)
- Context: ADR-004 §2.8 (spike) has an invitation text without an id, and "after 5 wrong proofs, every open invitation closes". GATE-M1 G11 (ADR §9.7) replaces the global cancel: failures count per invitation id, and 5 failures cancel only that invitation. A failure count per invitation needs an id that the joiner sends. The ADR also does not say whether refused attempts count against the rate limit, where pairing results go on the inviter, or what the dialer does when its peer is no longer pinned.
- Options: 1. keep the spike text and count failures per source IP only; 2. add a non-secret invitation id to the text and to `pair_proof`.
- Choice: 2.
- Details:
  - Invitation text: `fdinv1:wss://<ip>:<port>#<fingerprint 64 hex>.<id 16 hex>.<secret 32 hex>.<expiry unix s>`. `PairProof { invitation, proof }` carries the id. An unknown id and a wrong proof both give `invalid_invitation`, so the reason tells nothing about which ids exist. The fifth wrong proof gives `too_many_attempts` and cancels that invitation only.
  - `PairLimiter`: 10 admitted attempts per source IP in a 60 s window, 4 pairing handshakes at a time. A refused attempt does not count, so a lockout ends one window after the tenth admitted attempt (G11 test: a new invitation works after the lockout). `PairLimiter::with_window` exists for loopback tests that cannot wait one minute.
  - The pair channel is open only on a node with `NodeConfig::with_invitations`; otherwise it is refused as before. A failed pairing attempt is reported in the listener's queue like a failed link attempt; a success is reported by `Invitations::next_paired`.
  - Both sides store the new peer through `PairingSink` (`NodeConfig::sink`; the in-memory `Grants` until the stores of P3-5/P3-6 implement it) before they pin the key. An error of the sink fails the pairing with `pairing_failed`.
  - Reconnect (`spawn_dialer`, ADR §2.10): equal jitter in `[c/2, c]`, `c = min(5 min, 500 ms · 2^attempt)`, reset only after a link stayed up 60 s; every attempt commits a new epoch. When the peer key is not pinned any more (revoked), the dialer reports `Stopped { reason: "not_paired" }` and ends instead of retrying forever.
  - The ADR text of §2.8 is superseded by §9.7 for the failure count; the wire contract change (the id) is this entry.
- Reason: G11 needs per-invitation counts; an id is the smallest change that gives them, and it is not secret.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-046: Where G5 and G12 are enforced, and the order of their parts (M2 round 18, P3-4)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 18)
- Context: G5 asks for revocation and key rotation in one transaction with a persistent revocation generation, and for a check of pin and grant in the decision transaction of each command. G12 asks that `station join` / `deck add-station` read the invitation only from a hidden prompt, stdin or a 0600 file. In round 18 no persistent pin store and no command intake exist yet (they are P3-5 and P3-6), and the pairing commands cannot run without them.
- Options: 1. build the stores now inside P3-4; 2. do the link-layer parts now (revoke, rotate, close within 1 s, the input rules of the CLI) and do the store parts in the items that create the stores.
- Choice: 2.
- Details:
  - Now: `revoke::revoke` unpins, calls `PairingSink::revoked` and closes the active link with reason `revoked` (test: closes in < 1 s, the queued request is not answered with `ok`, the next handshake is refused). `revoke::rotate` checks that `old_key` is the link peer, the new key format, that the new key is not already paired and the Ed25519 signature over `KeyRotation::signed_message` (receiver + `command_id`), pins the new key with the old role, moves only the old grants (`PairingSink::rotated`) and revokes the old key. `sign_rotation` makes the envelope payload.
  - CLI: `invite::read` (prompt with echo off through `rustix::termios`, `--stdin`, `--invite-file` that must be a regular file with no group/other bits); input is bounded to 4 KiB and kept in `Secret<String>`; no error message contains it. Without a terminal and without a flag the command fails at once. An argument that contains `fdinv1:` makes the binary print a fixed message instead of clap's error, which would echo the argument. The commands then still report "not implemented yet" until P3-5/P3-6 add pairing to them.
  - P3-5 (station) and P3-6 (deck) add: the `PairingSink`/`GrantSource` of the stores (pin + grants + revocation generation in one transaction), `link.rotate_key` intake with its envelope, the decision-transaction check of pin and grant (`forbidden` for a queued command of a revoked link), and the `forget-deck` / `remove-station` / `join` / `add-station` commands with their end-to-end tests. PROGRESS lists these parts under those items.
- Reason: the stores do not exist before P3-5/P3-6; building them inside P3-4 would mix four items into one round. The link-layer behaviour does not change when the stores arrive: they plug in through `PairingSink` and `GrantSource`.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-047: Station link service surface in this build (M2 round 19, P3-5)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 19)
- Context: ADR-004 §9.6 lists `station.configure` (admin) and `artifact.open` (read) in the method table. PLAN Phase 3 asks for the event stream, command intake and receipts, but no item of M2 defines a station policy that a deck can change at runtime, and artifacts are PLAN Phase 6 (M3). The ADR also does not define the result of `events.replay` / `session.snapshot` or the shape of `station.info`.
- Options: 1. leave both methods out of the table; 2. keep the table as the ADR states and answer them with explicit refusals; 3. build runtime policy changes now.
- Choice: 2.
- Details:
  - `station.configure` goes through the normal mutation path: the envelope is parsed, `target_incarnation` / `retry_until` / pin and grant are checked in the decision transaction, and the decision is a stored rejection with `RejectCode::Unsupported`. A resend returns the same receipt. The station policy stays read-only (it comes from the station config file) until an owner decision adds runtime changes.
  - `artifact.open` answers `Outcome::Err` with code `not_implemented` (`fleetdeck_station::link::NOT_IMPLEMENTED`) until Phase 6. A `coordinator.*` mutation that reaches a station is rejected with `invalid_command` (not stored): coordinator calls belong to the deck.
  - `events.replay { incarnation, after }` and `session.snapshot {}` answer `StreamStart { mode: replay | snapshot, incarnation, from }` and then restart the event stream of the link. A snapshot batch (`snapshot_begin` / `snapshot_session`×n / `snapshot_end`) is sent when the incarnation differs or is absent, when the cursor is ahead of the head, when the cursor is below the retention floor, or on `session.snapshot`; the stream then continues from the watermark. An ack of another incarnation or above the last delivered `station_seq` closes the link with `protocol_error`.
  - `station.info` answers `StationInfo { name, incarnation, head, live_sessions }`; `command.receipt { command_id }` answers the stored receipt or `null`.
  - Over a link, a session command without `target_incarnation` is rejected with `invalid_command` and not stored (the local CLI may omit it, G3).
- Reason: the ADR method table stays the single list of methods (the table-agreement test keeps it true), and a deck gets a typed refusal instead of an unknown method.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-048: Station pairing at run time: one deck, persisted listen addresses, pairing through the local socket (M2 round 20, P3-5c)

- Context: PLAN §8.5 gives `station invite --listen <tailnet-ip:port>`, `station join` and `station forget-deck`, but does not say (a) which process does the pairing, (b) whether a station may pair with more than one deck, and (c) how the `--listen` address of `invite` survives a restart. ADR-004 §2.8 needs the inviter to be listening while the invitation is open.
- Options: (1) the CLI process pairs by itself and writes `station.db` while `serve` runs; (2) the running `station serve` process pairs; the CLI is a client of its 0600 local socket. For (b): allow several decks, or one deck per station. For (c): add the address to `station.toml`, or store it in `station.db`.
- Choice: (2): `serve` owns the link node (`StationNode`: listeners, dialers, `Invitations`), and `invite`/`join`/`forget-deck` are local-socket requests. One deck per station: `invite` and `join` are refused while a deck key is pinned (`forget-deck` first). The bound `--listen` address goes into `station.db` (schema V5 table `listen_addrs`) in the same call that opens the invitation; at start the station listens on `station.toml` `listen` plus the stored addresses. A joined deck's address goes into `pairing.dial_url` in the pin transaction, and the station dials it with jittered backoff until the deck is no longer pinned. The station grants a deck `read`, `drive`, `approve` and `admin` (ADR-004 §9.6). Round 21 (review): the one-deck rule is enforced in the pin transaction (`Store::pin_peer` refuses a deck key while another deck key is pinned, `StoreError::DeckPaired`), so two invitations opened before the first pairing cannot pin two decks; the refused pairing closes with `pairing_failed` and the link layer pins nothing. The CLI-level refusal stays as an early, friendlier message.
- Reason: only the process that holds the link node can listen for the pairing connection and close links within 1 s at revocation (G5); a second writer to the pin tables would race the cache in `StationPeers`. PLAN §1 says one deck controls the fleet; refusing a second deck makes `forget-deck` total and prevents a silent second controller. The CLI must not rewrite the owner's `station.toml`. The invitation crosses the local socket only (mode 0600, same user, peer-credential check) and its type redacts `Debug`; a bad request line is no longer echoed back (G12).
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-049: Deck read model, station ids and the status projection (M2 round 21, P3-6a)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 21)
- Context: PLAN §7.2 names the card states (queued, working, verifying, needs-you, idle, ended, unreachable, lost) but not the exact rules; v0 derived `verifying` from the running shell command. `SessionSummary` (P2) did not record the running tools, so `verifying` could not be derived from the projection. PLAN §10 gives `stations.station_id` and `key` as separate columns without saying what the id is when a station key rotates; `StationId` is documented as "the fingerprint of its identity key". The "condensed timeline" is not defined.
- Options: (a) derive status on the deck from raw events; (b) fold the running tools into `SessionSummary` (station and deck run the same fold) and project the status from the summary plus the link state. For the id: (1) the id follows the key (rows move at rotation); (2) the id is the fingerprint at pairing and stays when the key rotates.
- Choice: (b) and (2).
- Details:
  - `SessionSummary.running_tools` (at most 32, oldest dropped) holds the started and not yet completed tool calls; `run.completed` of the current run and `session.ended` clear it. `card_status(summary, reachable)` (`fleetdeck_proto::status`) applies, in order: `lost` (state lost) > `ended` (activity ended or state exited; stays while unreachable, the end is final) > `unreachable` (link down; the card keeps its last known summary) > `needs_you` > `queued` (state starting and no run yet) > `verifying` (working or background with a running shell tool whose command matches v0's test-runner words: pytest, jest, vitest, go test, cargo test, npm test, npm run test) > `working` (working or background: background work is provider activity, L7) > `idle`.
  - The deck keeps station reachability in memory (link state, not stored state); after a deck restart every station is unreachable until its link is up.
  - `deck.db` V1: `meta` (incarnation, revocation generation), `stations` (pin, grants, name, dial address, direction, last seen, the read-model incarnation and cursor), `sessions` (station id, session id, summary JSON), `timeline`. Events apply only for the incarnation that the read model holds (the first stream starts with a snapshot); an event at or below the cursor is skipped; fold, timeline and cursor commit in one transaction, and the deck acknowledges only the committed cursor. A snapshot batch replaces the station's sessions and sets incarnation and cursor to the watermark; a new incarnation clears the timeline.
  - The condensed timeline keeps every event except `message.delta` and `reasoning.delta` (the completed message holds the text), at most 500 rows per session (newest kept).
  - A station's `station_id` is its fingerprint at pairing; a rotation changes `key` and `fingerprint` and keeps the id, name, cursor and read model, and increments the revocation generation (G5). Station names are unique; without a name from `deck add-station` the name is the short fingerprint. The deck pairs only with stations (a deck key is refused in the pairing sink).
- Reason: one fold for station and deck keeps the card and the station's own view equal; the summary is what a snapshot carries, so a snapshot alone gives the correct status. A stable id keeps commands, receipts and the board's URLs valid across a key rotation.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-050: Deck link runtime: station grants, names, the first sync, and the deck's local socket (M2 round 22, P3-6b)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 22)
- Context: PLAN §8.5 gives the pairing commands (`deck invite`, `deck add-station`, `deck remove-station`) and §8.3 the per-method scopes, but not the scopes that a deck grants to a station, how a station that dials in gets its name, what the first sync of a new station is, or how the CLI talks to a running deck. The board API (P3-9) is not built yet, so the read model needs another view for tests.
- Options: (a) grant the station every scope; (b) grant only the scopes of the station-to-deck methods that this build serves. Name: (1) ask for a name at `deck invite`; (2) store the short fingerprint and adopt the name of `station.info` at the first link. CLI: (i) the board API; (ii) an owner-only local socket `deck.sock`, the same line protocol as `station.sock`.
- Choice: (b), (2), (ii).
- Details:
  - `STATION_SCOPES = [admin]`: the only station-to-deck method that this build serves is `link.rotate_key`. `coordinator.query` / `coordinator.call` (`coordinate`) come with the coordinator API (M3); the deck answers every station request with `not_implemented` until then.
  - A station pinned without a name (the dialing station of `deck invite`, or `deck add-station` without `--name`) is stored under its short fingerprint. At its first link the deck asks `station.info` and renames the station to the station.toml name if that name is free. `deck add-station` waits up to 3 s for that first link so that it can print the name.
  - The deck knows no incarnation of a new station, so the first sync is a snapshot batch (G4). The condensed timeline starts after the snapshot watermark: the events before pairing are in the station's summaries, not in the deck timeline. Later links replay from the stored cursor; the deck acknowledges only the committed cursor.
  - `deck.sock` (0600, owner uid check) serves `invite`, `add_station`, `stations`, `remove_station` and `sessions` (the read model with the projected status). The new CLI commands `fleetdeck deck sessions [--json]` and `deck stations --json` read it; `deck add-station` accepts `--name`. The board listen address of `deck serve --listen` is ignored with a warning until P3-9.
  - `deck invite` needs a link listener (`link_listen` in deck.toml); without one it fails with "the deck has no link listener".
  - The line protocol of the two local sockets moved to `fleetdeck_core::localsock` (bind 0600, peer uid check, 64 KiB line limit, error text without the request body).
- Reason: least privilege for the station key (doctrine: fail closed); the station.toml name is the name that the owner chose, and no prompt is needed on the deck; the local socket keeps the pairing secret off the network and off the command line (G12), as on the station.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-051: A bound for snapshot batches (M2 round 23, review of P3-6b)

- Date: 2026-10-11 (M2 round 23)
- Context: ADR-004 and G4 define the snapshot batch (`snapshot_begin { session_count }`, `snapshot_session` × n, `snapshot_end`) but give no upper limit. The deck buffers the batch until `snapshot_end` because the read model is replaced in one transaction. A station that never sends `snapshot_end`, or sends more sessions than announced, made the deck buffer without limit. The station sent every session it ever had, so an old station could also send a batch without limit.
- Options: (a) apply each session as it arrives (no buffer, but a cut-off batch leaves a mixed read model); (b) a fixed protocol limit on the batch size, checked on both sides; (c) a limit by bytes.
- Choice: (b). `fleetdeck_proto::link::MAX_SNAPSHOT_SESSIONS = 4096`.
  - The deck closes the link with `protocol_error` and drops the open batch when `snapshot_begin` announces more than the limit, or when a `snapshot_session` arrives after the announced count. A new `snapshot_begin` (a restarted stream) replaces an unfinished batch. So an unfinished batch holds at most `min(session_count, 4096)` summaries, each below the 1 MiB frame limit.
  - The station's `Store::snapshot` sends every session that has not exited (state not `exited` / `lost`) and then the newest exited sessions, up to `StoreConfig::snapshot_sessions` (default 4096). Running sessions are bounded by `max_sessions` (≤ 256). The deck therefore drops the cards of the oldest exited sessions when a snapshot replaces a station's read model; the station keeps them.
- Reason: the deck must not trust a peer for its memory use (doctrine: fail closed); one atomic replacement of the read model stays (no mixed state); a session-count limit is simple to check on both sides and the frame limit already bounds each summary.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-052: Command router details that PLAN §8.4 does not state (M2 round 23, P3-7a)

- Context: PLAN §8.4 and ADR-004 §9.3 give the `pending → done | failed | ambiguous` rules for commands that were sent. They do not say what happens to a command that the deck never sent before `retry_until`, to the commands of a removed station, to a request that the link layer refuses or that ends without an answer, or how the CLI addresses the router before the board API (P3-9) exists.
- Options: (a) keep such commands `pending` forever, or redispatch them with a new id; (b) end them with explicit `failed` reasons and keep the router's resend loop for every outcome that leaves the station's decision unknown.
- Choice: (b).
  - The deck counts a send (`attempts`, first `sent_at`) in `deck.db` **before** the bytes leave. A crash between the count and the send can only turn a later outcome into `ambiguous`, never into a wrong `failed`.
  - `retry_until` passed and never sent → `failed` with reason `not_sent`; sent and no receipt → `ambiguous` with `no_receipt`.
  - A rejection with `incarnation_mismatch` / `expired` (no stored receipt at the station) → `failed` (`station_reset` / `expired`) when this was the first send, `ambiguous` with the same reason after an earlier send.
  - A request refused by the link layer (`forbidden`, `wrong_role`, `unknown_method`, ...) → `failed` with the code as the reason on the first send: the station decided nothing. Round 24 (review): after an earlier send the refusal → `ambiguous` with the code, because the earlier send may have reached the station and run (its response was lost). `too_many_requests`, `timeout`, a dropped link and a link that is down keep the command `pending`; the router resends the same stored envelope with the same `command_id` when a link comes up and on each tick (`ROUTER_TICK` = 1 s), until `retry_until`. One send per command is in flight at a time.
  - A receipt whose `command_id` differs from the command's is a `failed` command with `protocol_error`.
  - `remove-station` marks the station's pending commands `failed` (`station_removed`) in the removal transaction.
  - The deck addresses a station only after its first sync: without a known incarnation, `submit` refuses with `NotSynced` (nothing is stored).
  - Until the board API exists, `fleetdeck deck command <station> '<SessionCommand JSON>'` routes one command (origin `board`) and waits up to 15 s for its outcome; `fleetdeck deck commands` lists the router's table.
  - The outbox (A5) and the coordinator receipts (G7) are P3-7b (DL-053).
- Reason: every command ends in a state that tells the owner what is known (doctrine: no silent loss, no double execution); a resend never changes the bytes or the id, so the station's receipt store keeps execution at most once.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-053: Outbox attempts and coordinator receipts before Phase 6 (M2 round 24, P3-7b)

- Context: PLAN A5 asks for an outbox with station receipts for deck-owned intent (mail, task assignment), and G7 / ADR-004 §9.3 ask the deck to dedupe `coordinator.call` with stored receipts. The PLAN does not say how an outbox item relates to the router's commands, when an item may be sent again, or what the deck decides for a coordinator call before the coordinator operations exist (Phase 6).
- Options: (a) the outbox sends its own requests with its own retry loop; (b) each outbox attempt is one routed command, so the router's receipts, resends and G3 states apply unchanged, and the outbox only decides whether a new attempt (new `command_id`) is safe.
- Choice: (b).
  - `deck.db` V4: `outbox` (state `queued | sending | delivered | dead`, `command_id` of the current attempt, `attempts`, `next_at`, `expires_at`, `reason`) and `coordinator_receipts` (command id, station, method, caller `"<station>/<session>"`, payload SHA-256, receipt).
  - One transaction inserts the attempt's `pending` command and moves the item to `sending`. A crash leaves either a queued item or a sending item with its stored command, which the router resends with the same id.
  - The router tick reconciles: command `done` + accepted → `delivered`; `done` + rejected → `dead` (`rejected: <code>`); `failed` with `not_sent`, `station_reset` or `expired` (the station provably did not run it) → `queued` again with a backoff of 5 s · 2^(attempts − 1), at most 5 min; another `failed` reason → `dead`; `ambiguous` → `dead` (`ambiguous: <reason>`), never sent again by the deck; a queued item past `expires_at` → `dead` (`expired`); station removal → `dead` (`station_removed`) in the removal transaction. Attempts start only for reachable stations.
  - `coordinator.call` follows the station's receipt rules: another deck incarnation → `incarnation_mismatch` (not stored); in the decision transaction the station must still be paired with the `coordinate` grant (else `forbidden`, not stored); a stored receipt with the same method, caller and payload hash is returned (also after `retry_until`); the same id with another payload → `command_id_reused` (not stored); a passed `retry_until` → `expired` (not stored). Until Phase 6 a new call is a stored rejection `unsupported` ("coordinator operations come with PLAN Phase 6"). `coordinator.query` and a station's `link.rotate_key` answer `not_implemented`.
  - Stations keep only the `admin` grant (DL-050), so the link layer refuses `coordinator.call` with `forbidden` until the owner grants `coordinate` (Phase 6 adds the command for it).
  - Until Phase 6 adds mail and task assignment, only `DeckLink::enqueue` (tests) writes the outbox.
  - Round 25 (review): an attempt's `retry_until` is `min(now + 15 min, expires_at)`, and `start_attempt` refuses an attempt whose `retry_until` is after the item's `expires_at` or that starts after it. A resend of a `sending` item therefore never carries intent past its expiry; an attempt without a receipt at `expires_at` becomes `ambiguous` → `dead`.
- Reason: one delivery mechanism keeps the at-most-once guarantee in one place (the station's receipt store); a new id is used only when the old one provably did not run, so intent is never lost silently and never executed twice (doctrine).
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-054: Board login, cookies, API tokens and the audit log (M2 round 25, P3-8)

- Context: PLAN §9 and A13 ask for an argon2 owner login that is required also on loopback, an HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Strict cookie, a CSRF token on every mutating request, a WS `Origin` check, one scope per board-API method with short-lived credentials for bearer clients (L15), `fleetdeck open` with a one-time login URL "read from an owner-only local file", and an audit row for every mutation. The PLAN does not say how the board is served (HTTP or HTTPS), how the login link reaches the CLI, what a bearer client authenticates with, or the session and limit values.
- Options: (a) the deck terminates TLS itself; (b) the deck serves plain HTTP on its D13 board addresses and LAN or tailnet browsers reach it through a TLS proxy (`tailscale serve`); the login link file of the PLAN or (c) the owner-only `deck.sock`.
- Choice: (a) and (c). Round 25 chose (b); the round-26 review rejected it because a TLS proxy is a reverse proxy, which D13 excludes, and PLAN §7 draws the board link as "HTTPS + WS".
  - HTTPS: the deck terminates TLS itself on every board listener (rustls, ring provider, TLS 1.2/1.3, ALPN `http/1.1`; hyper HTTP/1.1 with upgrades for the WS route; a handshake has 10 s). There is no plain-HTTP listener: a plain request gets no HTTP answer. The certificate is the owner's `board.crt` + `board.key` (PEM, key mode 0600 or refused) in the deck directory when both exist, for example the output of `tailscale cert` for the tailnet name; else a self-signed certificate (rcgen, ECDSA P-256) that the deck makes once and keeps in `board-selfsigned.{crt,key}` (key 0600). Its names are every bound board address, `localhost` and `tailnet_hostname`; when these names change, the deck makes a new one. `fleetdeck open` prints its SHA-256 fingerprint so that the owner can check the browser warning the first time.
  - The board API is axum 0.8 on the `listen` addresses of deck.toml (`deck serve --listen` replaces them after the same D13 check; public and wildcard addresses are refused). Every route goes through one guard with the method table `fleetdeck_deck::http::methods::API`; a route that is not in the table is refused (fail closed). The WS route of P3-9 goes through the same guard.
  - The guard order: an `Origin` header must be an origin of a bound board address (`https://addr`, `https://localhost:port` for loopback addresses, `https://tailnet_hostname:port`, and `https://tailnet_hostname` when the board listens on port 443); `http://` origins are refused; then a principal is required for every method except `auth.login` and `auth.link`; then the method's scope; then, for a mutating method with a cookie login, the `X-CSRF-Token` of that login (constant-time compare). A bearer client has no ambient credential and needs no CSRF token. A refused mutating request is audited with the refusal code.
  - Owner login: `POST /api/auth/login { password }` (argon2id, default parameters, verified on a blocking thread) or `POST /api/auth/link { token }`. The cookie `fd_session` is `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/`; the board is HTTPS on every address, so browsers keep the `Secure` cookie on loopback, LAN and tailnet. A login expires after 7 days idle or 30 days absolute. `deck.db` stores only SHA-256 hashes of cookies, link tokens and API tokens. Five failed logins in 60 s lock every login (429) until the window passes.
  - `fleetdeck open` asks `deck.sock` (0600, same user only) for a single-use login link (5 min) and prints `https://<first board address>/#login=<token>` (and the certificate fingerprint on stderr); the token is in the fragment, so it reaches no server log and no `Referer`. The socket replaces the owner-only file of the PLAN: it has the same access rule and leaves no secret on disk.
  - `deck password [--stdin]` sets the owner password (at least 12 characters) and ends every login. API tokens (`deck token create <name> --scope … --days N`, at most 90 days, scopes from read/drive/approve/admin, value shown once, prefix `fdtok1_`) are the bearer credential of non-browser clients; the short-lived WS tickets of L15 come with the WS route in P3-9.
  - `deck.db` V5: `owner`, `login_sessions`, `login_links`, `api_tokens`, `audit`. The audit row has actor (`owner`, `token:<name>`, `local`, `anonymous`), action, target, command id, result (`ok | refused | failed`) and detail; it never holds a secret. `deck.sock` mutations (invite, add/remove station, command, password, tokens, login link) are audited with the actor `local`. `deck audit [--json]` and `GET /api/audit` (admin) read it.
- Reason: D13 allows no reverse proxy, so the deck must serve HTTPS itself; a kept self-signed certificate works on every private address with no setup, the owner's certificate (for example `tailscale cert`) removes the browser warning, and the CLI pins the certificate. The guard enforces the same rules on loopback as elsewhere (A13). The local socket is the narrowest owner-only channel the node already has.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-055: Board command calls: client-chosen command ids and a short receipt wait (M2 round 26, P3-9a)

- Context: PLAN §9 says "REST for commands" and the Phase 3 exit asks that link drops and deck restarts "duplicate no commands". The PLAN does not say who chooses the `command_id` of a board command, how a board retry after a lost HTTP response is told apart from a new command, or how long a REST call waits.
- Options: (a) the deck chooses the id and every POST is a new command; (b) the board chooses the id (a ULID) and the deck treats a POST with a known id as a resend; (c) an `Idempotency-Key` header next to a deck id.
- Choice: (b).
  - `POST /api/commands/<method>` with `{ station, command_id, command }` (`CommandRequest`, unknown fields refused). The route names the method; a body with another command is `400 wrong_method`. A `command_id` has 1 to 64 characters from `[0-9A-Za-z_-]`.
  - A known id with the same station, method and command returns the stored command and sends nothing; a known id with another command is `409 command_id_reused`. A station the deck has never synced is `409 not_synced`; an unknown station is `404 unknown_station`.
  - The call waits up to 5 s for the receipt, then answers the command as it is (`pending` when the station is unreachable); the router sends it when the link returns, and the board polls `GET /api/commands/{command_id}`.
  - The scope of each command route is the scope of the link method of the same name (`drive`, `approve` for `approval.answer`); a test checks the table against the link table. Every call is audited: `ok` (detail `duplicate` for a resend) or `refused` with the error code.
- Reason: the board is the only party that knows whether a POST is a retry; a client id is the same rule the station uses for receipts (PLAN §6.4), so one id covers the whole path board → deck → station. A short wait gives a phone an immediate answer in the normal case without holding requests open while a station is away.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-056: The board WebSocket protocol, tickets and the Origin rule (M2 round 27, P3-9b)

- Context: PLAN §9 says "WS for snapshot + deltas", §7.1 asks for a versioned snapshot on connect and per-session timeline subscriptions with a cursor, and §8.5 (L15) says bearer clients get short-lived WS tickets. The PLAN does not give the message shapes, the ticket rules, how a cookie socket is protected against cross-site WebSocket hijacking (a browser sends the login cookie on an upgrade from any page), or what happens when a login ends while a socket is open.
- Options: (a) one socket per session timeline plus a board socket; (b) one socket for the board stream with timeline subscriptions on it; (c) server-sent events for the board and REST polling for timelines.
- Choice: (b), `GET /api/ws` (scope `read`).
  - Server messages (`BoardServerMessage`, TS in `web/src/gen/board/`): `hello { protocol: 1, deck_incarnation }`, one `snapshot { version, stations, sessions }`, then `delta { version, stations?, sessions, removed }` with only the changed cards and the removed `(station_id, session_id)` keys; `stations` is present when a station view changed (also its read-model `cursor`, a freshness signal). The version comes from a deck-wide counter that every read-model or registry change raises; the socket computes the delta against what it sent last, so a burst collapses into one delta.
  - Client messages (`BoardClientMessage`, unknown fields refused): `subscribe { station_id, session_id, incarnation?, after? }` and `unsubscribe`. A subscription answers `timeline_start { incarnation, reset }`, the stored timeline after the cursor, then every folded event of the session live, also the streaming deltas that the stored timeline drops. The client cursor applies only when its incarnation is the station's current one; else `reset: true` and the timeline starts from the beginning. A new station incarnation restarts every subscription of that station with `reset`. A socket that lags behind the live events resends the stored timeline after its cursor. At most 32 subscriptions per socket. Bad messages answer `error { code, message }` and keep the socket.
  - A cookie socket must send an `Origin` of the board (a foreign Origin is refused like every request); a missing Origin is `403 origin`. A bearer header does not open a socket: `POST /api/ws/ticket` (scope `read`, mutating, so a cookie caller needs its CSRF token) returns a single-use ticket valid for 30 s (in memory, at most 256 open; a deck restart ends them), and `GET /api/ws?ticket=…` opens the socket with the ticket's principal. Redemption re-checks that principal in the store before upgrade; logout, password change, expiry or token revocation ends its tickets too. A token principal carries the token hash, not its reusable name or creation timestamp, so a same-name replacement never revives an old ticket or socket (round 28 review fix).
  - Every 30 s a socket checks that its login session or token is still valid and closes with code 4401 when not (logout, password change, token revoke or expiry).
  - The client's 5-minute idle cursor (§7.1) belongs to the Phase 4 web runtime.
- Reason: one socket per phone keeps the connection count low on a mobile network and gives one order for board and timeline messages; the incarnation rule is the same rule the deck uses for station replays (G4), so a replaced station database can never splice two timelines. Tickets keep long-lived bearer tokens out of URLs and logs (L15).
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-057: Browser connection ownership, retry bounds, and timeline cache (M2 round 29, P4-1)

- Context: PLAN §11 and L17 fix one retry owner, separate transport/freshness states, stale readable data, and five idle minutes for cursors. They do not specify timer bounds, React ownership, or login-secret handling.
- Options: (a) a page-owned runtime with injected transport and timers; (b) React effects own separate sockets and retries per screen.
- Choice: (a). `web/src/main.tsx` creates and starts one `Connection` outside React effects. React StrictMode cannot redeem a one-time link twice. `RuntimeApp` reads immutable state with `useSyncExternalStore`.
  - Use the existing same-origin cookie login API. Keep the CSRF token only in memory. Do not store credentials in browser storage. Strip `#login=…` with `history.replaceState` before the link POST or rendering. Clear the password input when submitting. Never retry password or link POSTs.
  - Retry only safe `/api/auth/session` reads before opening the cookie-authenticated `wss:` socket. Use exponential backoff from 500 ms, jitter in `[0.5, 1]`, and a 30 s cap. Reset backoff only after a snapshot connection stays stable for 10 s. Bound authentication, snapshot arrival, and subscription start waits to 10 s. A generation fences stale fetch results, socket callbacks, and timers.
  - Keep stale cards during an outage. A new socket accepts its first snapshot even when a restarted deck has a lower process-local version. Ignore duplicate snapshots and non-increasing deltas. Clear credentials and caches on logout, HTTP 401, or socket close 4401.
  - Reference-count timeline watches. Unsubscribe when the last reader leaves. Keep each idle cursor and timeline for five minutes. Resume with the station incarnation and cursor; reset on a changed incarnation. Cap each cached timeline at 1,000 recent events. Enforce the server's 32-active-subscription limit, including reactivated idle entries. A subscription error changes timeline freshness, not transport health.
  - Validate the wire fields that the runtime reads before reducing them. Reject malformed projections and unsafe sequence numbers. Keep unknown canonical event kinds readable, as L16 requires. This is not a second provider protocol parser.
- Reason: one page-owned runtime prevents duplicate retries and one-time login consumption. Injected fakes prove reconnection without network or wall-clock waits. Timer and cache bounds protect a phone from stalled connections and unbounded streamed text. Generation and incarnation fences prevent old cached data from replacing new live data.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)


## DL-058: Fleet identity, deck status lanes and honest station presentation (M2 round 30, P4-2a)

- Context: the skeleton board accepts bare session summaries. That loses the station ID and the deck status projection. PLAN §7.2 requires queued, working, verifying, needs-you, idle, ended, unreachable and lost states. PLAN §11 requests station details that the current board wire contract does not yet report.
- Options: (a) retain the full `DeckSession` and present only known facts; (b) continue grouping bare summaries and estimate station metadata from sessions.
- Choice: (a). Use `[station_id, session_id]` as the React card key. Group by the deck's `CardStatus`, not the station's summary activity. Put needs-you first, followed by queued, working, verifying, idle, ended, unreachable and lost. Show the last activity on an unreachable card. Keep background task counts as a separate badge, not a replacement status lane.
  - Read fleet and stations from one runtime snapshot. Add in-memory station/provider/search filters and a clear action. Search title, session/station IDs, station name, workspace, model and provider instance, without case sensitivity. Do not mutate runtime projections or create another connection. Keep removed selected station IDs explicit instead of silently selecting all stations.
  - Show cached card and station data during an outage. Label stale station link status separately from its last reported reachability. Show last-seen time in UTC; distinguish no previous link from Unix epoch zero.
  - Show observed provider instances and versions from session summaries under an explicit observed-data heading. Do not call those values the configured provider inventory. Count active process states (`starting`, `running`, `stopping`) separately from configured capacity. Mark missing station version, capacity and branch data as not reported until the station reports them. These placeholders do not satisfy P4-2; metadata and pairing stay unchecked in PROGRESS.md.
  - Use existing tokens, minimum tap sizes, responsive lanes and wrapped paths. Add no dependencies or browser credential storage. Escape untrusted text through React, never inject HTML.
- Reason: station-scoped identity prevents card collisions across a fleet. The deck knows link and process loss; summary activity alone cannot represent those states. Clear stale and unknown labels prevent the board from making false health, inventory or capacity claims while the metadata path is unfinished.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-059: Station report, pairing API and phone browser tests (M2 round 31, P4-2b)

- Context: P4-2 requires real station version, provider inventory, capacity and branch data, plus a pairing dialog. The plan names the screens but not the wire fields, the HTTP methods, the probe timing or the browser test harness.
- Options: (a) extend `station.info` and keep the report in deck memory; (b) store station reports in the deck database; (c) report metadata in every session summary only.
- Choice: (a).
  - `StationInfo` gains optional `version`, `max_sessions` and `providers: [{instance, provider, version, auth}]` (`#[serde(default)]`, L16). The station probes each configured provider instance once per process (`SessionManager::providers`, bounded by `PROBE_TIMEOUT` 10 s) and caches the result. An instance without an adapter (Codex before Phase 5) or with a failed probe reports `version: null` and `auth: "unknown"`. A restart of the station refreshes the report.
  - The deck asks every new link for `station.info` on its own task (it does not delay the replay) and keeps the newest report with its time in memory (`DeckStore::set_station_info`). `StationView` gains `info` and `info_at`. A deck restart loses the report until the next link; the board shows "Not reported" until then. The old rename of a station that paired under its short fingerprint now uses the same report.
  - `SessionSummary.branch` comes from the workspace `.git/HEAD` (also a worktree `gitdir:` file) at session creation; `fleetdeck_core::git::current_branch` reads files only and runs no `git` process. A detached HEAD, a missing repository or an odd name gives `null`. The branch is not refreshed later in M2.
  - Board methods (admin scope, CSRF, audited): `POST /api/stations/invite` → `PairingInvitation {invitation, expires_at, join_command}`; `POST /api/stations` with `AddStationRequest {invitation, name}`; `POST /api/stations/{station}/remove` (POST, not DELETE, so that every mutating board method has one CSRF and audit path). Invitations are at most 4096 characters. A refusal and an audit row never hold the invitation text; the audit detail is the error code. A board without a link node answers 503 `pairing_unavailable`.
  - The dialog keeps an invitation only in component state and drops it on close. Removal needs a second "Confirm remove" tap.
  - Browser tests use Playwright 1.59.1 (exact dev dependency) with headless Chromium at 390×844 (touch, mobile) against the built board from `vite preview`. P4-2 interaction tests script the board API with Playwright request and WebSocket routing (`web/e2e/fake-deck.ts`). The P4-5 MVP flows will run against real deck and station processes with fake agents. `make check` runs `bun run --cwd web e2e`; CI installs `chromium-headless-shell` first.
  - `SessionSummary.branch` moved `ControlFrame::SnapshotSession` over clippy's `large_enum_variant` limit. Boxing that variant moves the limit to `Event`, so `ControlFrame` takes `#[expect(clippy::large_enum_variant)]` with a reason: a frame lives only between decode and dispatch, and boxing would add an allocation per frame.
- Reason: the station is the only process that knows its configuration and provider CLIs; a cached probe keeps `station.info` fast and bounded. Memory-only reports cannot become stale after a deck restart. Scripted browser tests prove the UI interactions without a long process setup, and real-process flows stay in P4-5.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-060: Session view, subagent tree and board commands (M2 round 31, P4-3)

- Context: P4-3 and owner decision D15 require a session timeline, a composer, interrupt, a mode ladder, approvals, a needs-you rail and a subagent tree with child timelines, status and a running-subagent count on the card. The plan does not say how the board binds a backgrounded subagent to its task, how the card counts subagents, how the board picks a model, or how it sends commands.
- Options: (a) build the tree in the board from the canonical event stream and count from the summary; (b) add a new station-side subagent projection to the protocol; (c) show only flat events with a subagent badge.
- Choice: (a).
  - `web/src/session/timeline.ts` folds canonical events into a tree. A child joins its parent by `parent_node_id`; an approval goes into the list of its node. A subagent is `running` from `tool.started` (tool kind `subagent`) until `tool.completed` (`done`, `failed`, `denied`). When the tool completes `ok` while an agent background task (`local_agent`, `remote_agent`) is not yet bound to a subagent, the board binds the oldest such task to that subagent and shows `running in the background` until the task leaves `background.updated`. `session.ended` marks running children `ended` and open approvals expired. Claude reports no task-to-tool link, so this binding is a presentation heuristic; the station data stays unchanged.
  - `SessionSummary` gains `background_agents` (`#[serde(default)]`, L16): the number of agent-kind background tasks, kept by the shared fold. The card count is running `subagent` tools plus `background_agents`. For one frame between a task notice and its tool completion a backgrounded subagent can count twice; the next event corrects it. This avoids a timeline subscription per card.
  - The board sends each action once to `POST /api/commands/<method>` with a new command ID (`b` + 24 base-36 characters from `crypto.getRandomValues`). It never resends. It shows `Done.`, `Refused: <message>`, `Sent. The station has not confirmed it yet.`, `Not delivered: <reason>`, or for `ambiguous` `Delivery is unknown. The deck does not send it again; check the timeline.`
  - Controls follow `Capabilities` (steer, interrupt, `set_mode` with `modes` in the provider order, `set_model`), the scopes (drive, approve) and freshness. Every control is off unless the board and the station are live. While a run is active the composer offers "Steer now" and "Queue follow-up"; else "Send". The model picker takes free text, because providers accept ids and aliases that the board cannot list.
  - The needs-you rail lists the open requests of all sessions, oldest first, and opens the session.
  - The canonical Claude fixtures for the board (`web/src/testing/fixtures/claude-{subagent,tool_call_approve}.events.json`) are generated from the provider replay and a Rust test proves they equal it (`FD_UPDATE_FIXTURES=1` rewrites them). The recorded subagent was a background subagent without child frames, so a synthetic typed fixture (`nestedSubagents`) adds nested foreground subagents with tools, text, a failure and an approval. `web/e2e/fake-deck.ts` serves timelines through the board subscription protocol.
- Reason: the tree needs no protocol change and works for any provider that emits `parent_node_id`. A summary counter keeps the fleet board cheap. One send per user action keeps the "never resend an ambiguous command" rule at the board edge.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-061: Spawn form, directory picker, embedded board and `fleetdeck up` (M2 round 32, P4-4)

- Context: P4-4 requires a spawn form (station, provider, directory picker) and the all-in-one `fleetdeck up` with the board embedded through `rust-embed`. PLAN §11 lists more spawn options (station "auto", branch/worktree, batch, Nx, offline policy), and Phase 6 owns worktrees, batch spawn, Nx and the repo catalog. The plan does not name the picker's wire method, the asset serving rules, how `up` pairs its local station, or where a deck card gets its title.
- Options: (a) a drive-scoped `workspace.list` link method with a deck route, an embedded board with a strict policy, and in-process pairing for `up`; (b) a free-text directory field and a separate board build served from disk; (c) `up` as two child processes paired through the CLI.
- Choice: (a).
  - Link method `workspace.list` (deck → station, scope drive, no feature, not idempotency-keyed). ADR-004 §9.6 table gains the row, because the method-table agreement test compares the code with the ADR (accepted ADR amended under its own extension rule; owner review pending). The station lists the sub-directories of a path inside its allowed roots, or the roots when no path is given. It hides dot directories, non-UTF-8 names and entries whose canonical path leaves the roots; marks entries with `.git` as repositories; caps at 500 entries and 10 000 scanned names (`truncated`); runs on a blocking thread with a 5 s limit.
  - Deck route `GET /api/stations/{station}/workspaces?path=` (drive scope, not mutating) waits at most 8 s and maps errors: 400 `invalid_path` (over 4096 characters or NUL), 404 `unknown_station`, 409 `station_unreachable`, 400 `missing_workspace`/`outside_roots`, 504 `timeout`, 502 `station_error`.
  - The board form (`web/src/spawn/`) offers linked stations that reported providers, the provider instances from `station.info`, the directory picker, a permission mode (station default, or the provider's known modes most restrictive first; P5-3 makes it capability-driven), an optional model (free text, as DL-060), title and first prompt. One button press sends one `session.start` with a fresh command ID; the board never resends. An accepted receipt with a session ID offers "Open the new session". Station "auto", worktrees, batch, Nx and the offline policy field stay in Phase 6 (the station's configured offline policy applies).
  - `Event::SessionStarted` gains optional `title` and `branch` (`#[serde(default)]`, omitted when absent, L16). The provider core leaves them out; the station store fills them from the session row before it stores the event. Reason: a deck that was linked when a session was created folds the event onto an empty summary and had no title or branch (found by the `up` test). The two fields made `Event` 272 bytes and tripped clippy's `large_enum_variant` on `EventBody` and on the provider `Output` enums; `SessionStarted.capabilities` is now `Box<Capabilities>` (the largest field of the largest event; one allocation per session start). The JSON and TypeScript shapes do not change.
  - Board assets: `rust-embed` 8.13 (no default features) embeds `web/dist` at build time (`allow_missing`, so a deck builds without a board build and answers 404 with a build hint). Every page has a strict CSP (`default-src 'none'`, `script-src 'self'`, `connect-src 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, …), `nosniff`, `no-referrer`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`. `/assets/*` is immutable-cached; other paths are `no-cache`. A path without a dot falls back to `index.html` (client routes); `/api/*` never falls back. Only GET and HEAD are served.
  - `fleetdeck up` runs `RunningDeck` and `RunningStation` (new start/stop split of `deck serve` and `station serve`) in one process from the normal config files. At the first start the deck makes an invitation in memory and the station joins it over the deck's link listener; the invitation never leaves the process. A station that is already paired with this deck only reconnects; a station that is paired with another deck is an error with the `fleetdeck station forget-deck` hint (`up` never replaces a pairing). A deck without `link_listen` is an error.
- Reason: the station is the only process that may read its file system, and the drive scope is the right gate for directory names. One embedded binary makes the single-machine mode one command. In-process pairing keeps the secret out of files and terminals.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-062: Real-process MVP browser flows (M2 round 33, P4-5)

- Context: the Phase 4 exit (MVP demo) asks for a phone that spawns Claude sessions on two machines, answers approvals, steers and interrupts, and survives a deck restart and a network drop. The P4-2..P4-4 Playwright tests use a scripted deck (DL-059). The plan does not say how the demo test starts processes, simulates a phone network drop, or treats the self-signed board certificate.
- Options: (a) a second Playwright config that starts the real `fleetdeck` binaries (one deck, two stations with `fake-claude sim`) on temporary nodes from TypeScript; (b) a Rust test that drives the browser; (c) only the scripted deck.
- Choice: (a).
  - `web/playwright.real.config.ts` (`bun run --cwd web e2e:real`, part of `make check` after the scripted e2e): one worker, serial steps that share one fleet and one phone page (390×844, mobile, touch). The global setup runs `cargo build -p fleetdeck-cli -p fleetdeck-testkit --bins` unless `FD_BIN_DIR` names prebuilt binaries.
  - `web/e2e-real/fleet.ts` makes one temporary directory per node under the OS temp directory (home + data dir, mode 0700, config 0600) and removes it after the run (`FD_E2E_KEEP=1` keeps it for debugging). Processes stop with SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 20 s.
  - The deck board listens on a fixed free loopback port, so a restarted deck serves the same origin and the phone keeps its login cookie. The context accepts the self-signed board certificate (`ignoreHTTPSErrors`), as a person does once on first use (A13).
  - Both pairings go through the board dialog: bravo (no listener) joins a board-made invitation; the board pairs alpha (listener) from alpha's invitation.
  - Network drop: Chromium's offline emulation blocks new requests but keeps open WebSockets. The test routes the board socket through Playwright (`routeWebSocket` + `connectToServer`), sets the context offline and closes the routed socket; the board must reconnect after the network returns.
  - Assertions come from the board and from the stations' own `station sessions --json` lists (no session started twice after the deck restart).
  - Round 34 addition (review feedback: session counts alone cannot prove that commands ran once): the deck's own routed-command list (`deck commands --json`) must hold exactly the expected count per method, unique command ids, every row `done` after one attempt; and each station's fake agent logs every stdin frame (`FLEETDECK_FAKE_STDIN_LOG` in `station.toml`), so the tests compare the exact user messages, control requests (`initialize`, `interrupt`) and approval answers that reached each provider process after the deck restart, the phone network drop and the station restart.
- Reason: real processes prove the whole path (board → deck → link → station → provider host → fake agent) with the same binaries that users run, offline and in under 10 s. Closing the routed socket is the closest browser-level model of a phone that loses its network.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-063: Production Codex core (M2 round 34, P5-1)

- Context: P5-1 asks for a sans-IO Codex core from `spikes/s2-codex` with the vendored schema, mapped to proto events, and replays of all Codex fixtures. The spike emits the reduced `spike-canon` events; the production `Event` type needs more (runs, parent nodes, tool input summaries, exit codes, enum expiry reasons, interrupt results, model changes). The plan does not fix where the schema and fixtures live, how runs map to Codex turns, or how D15 child messages reach the timeline.
- Options: (a) port the spike into `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/` with the same structure (rpc, core, map, requests, host_cmd) and adapt it to the production types; (b) make the spike a library dependency of the production crate; (c) copy the 844 KB schema and the fixtures into the production crate.
- Choice: (a), and the tests read the fixtures and the schema in place under `spikes/s2-codex/` (the same as the Claude replay reads `spikes/s1-claude/fixtures`). The spike stays unchanged and keeps its own replay in `make check`.
  - `CoreConfig { provider_instance, cwd, capabilities, resumed, client }`. Production sessions send `clientInfo` `fleetdeck`/crate version; the replay uses the recorder's `fleet-deck-spike`/`0.0.0`, so the recorded host writes stay byte-exact.
  - One run per native turn: `run.started` when the turn starts, with the origin and summary of the input that sent `turn/start` (a provider-only turn gets origin `provider`); `run.completed` follows `turn.completed`, with `background_pending` when a command outlives the turn (trap 6).
  - Tool titles: `commandExecution` → `Shell` + the command; `fileChange` → `Edit` + paths; `mcpToolCall` → `server.tool`; `webSearch` → the query; `collabAgentToolCall` → the tool name (`spawnAgent`, `wait`) + its prompt, kind `subagent`. `tool.completed` carries `exitCode` and the changed files.
  - D15 child timelines: items of a child thread nest under the collab tool node that named the thread (`receiverThreadIds`, also when only the completed item names it). A completed child `agentMessage` becomes `message.completed` with that `parent_node_id`; child deltas, turns and token usage stay out of the parent stream. A thread that no tool call named is ignored.
  - `turn/interrupt` reply → `interrupt.result` (`accepted` from success). An interrupt before `turn/started` is held (trap 3); a second one, or one held past the end of the turn, gets `accepted: false` with a reason, so the station retires the effect and never resends.
  - `set_model` stores the model and sends it as `model` with the next `turn/start`; `model.changed` follows the accepted turn (the same as modes). Codex has no model list, so the core accepts any non-empty model name of at most 200 characters without whitespace and lets Codex refuse unknown names.
  - Errors map to `ProviderError`: no thread → `NotReady`; a mode outside the capability list → `InvalidMode`; an edited approval input → `Unsupported(EditInput)`; an invalid question reply → new `ProviderError::InvalidAnswer`; an expired request → `RequestClosed`; a duplicate answer → no output (idempotent).
  - `codex::capabilities(allow_full_access)`: the §6.2 Codex column; the mode list is `approvalPolicy/sandbox`, ordered most restrictive first, and `danger-full-access` modes appear only when the station allows them.
- Reason: the spike's replay-proven logic stays the same, so the 18 recorded fixtures replay byte-exact through the production core, and new behaviour is limited to the production event fields. Reading the fixtures and schema in place avoids a second copy that could drift.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-064: Codex driver, fake Codex app-server and station integration (M2 round 35, P5-2)

- Context: P5-2 asks for the Codex driver over the P5-1 core, a fake Codex, the background poll of DL-024 and the station integration. The plan does not fix when a start returns, how the probe gets a definite sign-in state (ADR-002 trap 9), the poll interval default, the fake's behaviour, or what a start with a mode that Codex does not offer does.
- Options for the start: (a) return the session as soon as the process runs and let the first prompt wait; (b) return only after `initialize` and the thread open (`thread/start|resume|fork`) completed. Options for an unknown mode: (a) the core's safest-mode fallback (L8) only; (b) refuse the start before any process.
- Choice:
  - `CodexAdapter::start_session` returns after the handshake and the thread open (b), within `open_timeout` 30 s; a failure kills the process and fails the start (`start_failed`, not dispatched). The station sends the first prompt at once after `start`, so it needs an open thread.
  - The shared stdio helpers of both drivers (`Write`, `flush`, `now_ms`, `write_failed`) moved to `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/stdio.rs`.
  - Probe: `codex --version` for the version, then one short `codex app-server` run with `initialize` + `account/read {refreshToken:false}`. An account object → `signed_in` (method = account type); `account: null` with `requiresOpenaiAuth: true` → `signed_out`; any other answer, an error, or a timeout (10 s) → `unknown`.
  - Background poll (DL-024): a poll task per session wakes after each `turn.completed` and after a non-empty `background.updated`, calls `thread/backgroundTerminals/list`, and repeats every `background_poll` (default 5 s) while the list or a background tool is open. The interval is an adapter setting; `station.toml` does not expose it in M2 (the default fits a phone view), and the fixture replay turns it off so that recorded host writes stay exact.
  - A start whose mode is not in the instance's Codex mode list fails with `InvalidMode` before any process (b): the effect retires as not dispatched and the session ends with `start_failed`. The core fallback stays as the last guard.
  - Fake: `fake-codex app-server` scenario `sim` (`crates/fleetdeck-testkit/src/sim_codex.rs`) is a reactive JSON-RPC app-server with the recorded wire shapes: `ask <cmd>` → command item + approval request; `wait <x>` → streams `waiting` until `turn/steer` or `turn/interrupt`; `bg <cmd>` → a background terminal that the first list shows and the second list completes; other text → `echo: <text>`. Scenarios `auth:signed-in` / `auth:signed-out` answer `account/read`. Recorded scenarios replace `<workdir>` in host frames with the fake's working directory.
  - The station builds a Codex adapter for each `driver = "codex"` instance (`adapters_from_config`).
- Reason: (b) for the start keeps the station's start → prompt order valid without a second wait path. (b) for the mode prevents a session that runs in a mode the owner did not choose, and the owner sees the error. The probe follows trap 9: only a definite provider answer is `signed_out`.
- Amendment (M2 round 38, clean-checkout `make check`): an app-server that exits during the handshake ended the start with `SessionEnded` when the reader saw the end first, but with a host write error when the stdin writer saw it first (`a_failed_handshake_fails_the_start` failed once under load). A write error during the handshake now also ends the start with `SessionEnded`. The invariant is that the handshake cannot continue: the stdin writer has stopped (usually because the app-server is gone), so no `initialize` or thread request can reach it. Every error of `CodexSession::open` aborts the start, and `start_session` then kills the process group before it returns the error, so no process or task stays behind. The station treats both errors as a failed start, so the station behaviour does not change; the error is now the same for every timing.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-065: Capability matrix tests and capability-driven UI (M2 round 36, P5-3)

- Context: P5-3 asks for tests of every PLAN §6.2 row for both providers and for controls that appear only when the session's capabilities allow them. The plan does not fix how a capability value is tested against provider behaviour, how the board learns the modes of a provider before a session exists (the spawn form had a hard-coded `MODES` table, DL-061), or what the board shows for the rows that have no control.
- Options for the tests: (a) assert the capability values only; (b) assert each value and the behaviour that the value promises in the recorded fixtures of each provider. Options for the spawn modes: (a) keep a board table per provider kind; (b) the station reports the capabilities of a new session per provider instance in `StationProvider`.
- Choice:
  - (b) for the tests: `crates/fleetdeck-providers/tests/capability_matrix.rs` has one test per §6.2 row. Each test checks the `claude::capabilities(false)` / `codex::capabilities(false)` value and replays the matching Claude and Codex fixtures through the production sans-IO cores (for example: resume keeps the native session id and its memory, fork gets a new id, open requests expire on restart and are asked again, steer joins the running turn, Codex follow-ups are a station queue, every turn ends with one strong terminal status, Codex refuses an edited approval input with `Unsupported(EditInput)`, Codex background work is polled, only Codex has native turn ids, only Claude reports cost). One more test checks every `Operation` against `supports` and its degradation.
  - (b) for the spawn modes: `StationProvider.capabilities: Option<Capabilities>` (`#[serde(default)]`, `None` without an adapter or from an older station); `ProviderAdapter::capabilities()` gives the value under the station's settings (bypass / full access). The spawn form offers exactly these modes, resets the mode when the provider changes, and sends no mode that the list does not contain.
  - Session view: Interrupt, mode ladder, model picker and Steer show only when the session's capabilities allow them (from P4-2); approval option buttons show only with `approvals` ("This provider has no approval controls." otherwise); without `native_follow_up` a follow-up while the turn works shows "The station holds a follow-up until the turn ends."
  - Board timeline fixtures for Codex (`web/src/testing/fixtures/codex-{subagent,tool_call_approve}.events.json`) come from the production core replay (`FD_UPDATE_FIXTURES=1`) and check that the board builds the same tree shapes for both providers. `web/src/testing/capabilities.ts` holds literal Claude, Codex and minimal capabilities for the Playwright fake deck; a unit test compares them with the recorded `session.started` events.
  - Limitation (M2): rows without a board control (fork, resume, edit input, plan text, usage display) are covered by provider tests only; the board has no fork, resume or edit-input control in M2 (resume and fork effects are DL-037). (Corrected in M2 round 39: this entry first said that the board answers a Codex `requestUserInput` question with an option only. That was wrong: the board sent `updated_input: null`, which the Codex adapter rejects, so such a question could not be answered from the board at all. DL-068 adds the question answer form for both providers.)
- Reason: a test that pairs each value with the recorded behaviour prevents a capability from claiming more than the provider does. Station-reported capabilities remove the second copy of the mode lists in the board, so a station setting (bypass, full access) shows in the spawn form.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-066: Codex board flows next to the Claude flows (M2 round 37, P5-4)

- Context: P5-4 asks for the Playwright flows for Claude and Codex, and Phase 5's exit asks that the same board flows work for both providers. The P4-5 flows (`web/e2e-real/mvp.spec.ts`, DL-062) use Claude only. The plan does not fix whether the providers share one fleet, or how a flow handles behaviour that differs by capability (a Codex steer joins the running turn and ends it in the fake; Codex has no native follow-up queue; Codex background work is polled).
- Options: (a) one parameterised spec that runs identical steps and identical expected texts for both providers; (b) a second spec with the same steps for Codex, with expected texts and provider input from the Codex capabilities; (c) Codex sessions added to the Claude fleet.
- Choice: (b). Every real-process station offers both `fake-claude` (`sim`) and `fake-codex` (`app-server`, `sim`) instances (`web/e2e-real/fleet.ts`, with a separate stdin log per provider and `Station.codexInput()`). `web/e2e-real/codex.spec.ts` runs its own deck and two stations (one dialed, one dialing) and the same steps as `mvp.spec.ts` with Codex sessions: pairing, spawn on each station, an approval from the needs-you rail, steer, interrupt, a deck restart with work done meanwhile, a phone network drop and a station link drop, each with the deck's command rows (one row, one delivery per command) and the Codex agents' input (each turn, steer and answer once).
