Codex differences: the steer step expects `steered: <text>` in the running turn, and a second waiting turn takes the interrupt; the composer shows the station follow-up note; the spawn form offers the Codex modes; the mode button of the stale view is `untrusted/read-only`. One more step checks a background command on the card until the station poll (DL-024, 5 s) sees it end. The shared phone steps are in `web/e2e-real/board.ts` (`Phone`); `mvp.spec.ts` stays unchanged except its header.
- Reason: (a) would hide the real capability differences or force the fake to act unlike Codex; (c) would change the verified Claude counts. (b) keeps both flows strict and lets each expected value follow the provider's §6.2 column.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-067: Opt-in live smoke tests (M2 round 37, P5-5)

- Context: P5-5 asks for one live smoke test per provider (real CLI, tiny prompt, temporary directory) behind `FD_LIVE_SMOKE=1`, documented and not in the default run. The plan does not fix the test level, the gate mechanism, the prompt, or the CLI flags.
- Options for the level: (a) a provider-driver test (probe, start, one turn, stop) with the production adapters; (b) a full station or `fleetdeck up` run. Options for the gate: (a) the environment variable only; (b) `#[ignore]` and the environment variable.
- Choice: (a) for the level: `crates/fleetdeck-providers/tests/live_smoke.rs` drives `ClaudeAdapter` and `CodexAdapter` with `ChildEnv::from_process()` in a new temporary directory, in each provider's safest mode (`default`, `untrusted/read-only`), with the prompt `Reply with the single word pong. Do not use any tool.`, and checks the probe (a version, not signed out), `session.started`, one `done` turn with `pong` in the answer, no approval request, and a graceful stop. Claude runs with `--safe-mode` (the same reason as DL-005: the owner's hooks and plugins stay out of the session); Codex uses effort `low`. (b) for the gate: each test is `#[ignore]`d and returns at once without `FD_LIVE_SMOKE=1`. `make smoke-live` runs both (bounded by `timeout 900`); `docs/v2/LIVE-SMOKE.md` documents the run and the last result. The full station path stays covered offline by the fake-agent tests and the real-process Playwright flows.
- Reason: the driver level is the boundary that a CLI change breaks; the station above it is provider-independent and already tested with the fakes. The double gate keeps `cargo test -- --ignored` (for the debug helpers) from spending tokens by accident.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-068: Question requests get an answer form; answers are not input edits (M2 round 39)

- Context: review of round 38 found that the board could not answer a Codex `item/tool/requestUserInput` question. The board sent every answer as `{option_id, updated_input: null}`; the Codex adapter requires `updated_input = {answers: {<question id>: {answers: [..]}}}` for this request (ADR-002 L4) and rejects anything else. Two more gaps were behind it: (1) the station checked any `updated_input` against the `edit_input` capability, which Codex does not have, so the station would refuse the answers too; (2) Codex 0.160.0 starts no item for the question, so the request had no tool node, and the station confirms an answer effect only from the output or completion of the request's node (DL-035), so the answer effect would never be confirmed. Claude `AskUserQuestion` had the same board gap: its answers go in the `allow` option's `updated_input` (`answers` keyed by question text).
- Options for the station: (a) give Codex `edit_input`; (b) treat `updated_input` of a `question` request as the answer, and check `edit_input` only for other request kinds. Options for the node: (a) confirm a question answer from `approval.resolved`; (b) the Codex core opens a `Question` tool node for a question without a provider item and completes it on `serverRequest/resolved`.
- Choice:
  - Station (b): `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/decide.rs` checks `Operation::Answer` for a question request and `Operation::EditInput` only for an approval with `updated_input`. A Codex command approval with `updated_input` stays refused (`unsupported`).
  - Codex core (b): `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/requests.rs` emits `tool.started` (kind `other`, title `Question`, the first question as summary) before the `approval.requested` of a `requestUserInput` without a known item, so the request has a node; the provider's `serverRequest/resolved` after the reply completes the node `ok`, which confirms the answer effect. An unanswered question node fails at turn end like every open tool.
  - Board: `web/src/session/question.ts` builds an answer form from the request input: Codex RPC questions (option `answer`, `{answers: {id: {answers: [..]}}}`), Codex message questions (option `answer`, the text), Claude `AskUserQuestion` (option `allow`, the input plus `answers` by question text, several answers joined by `, `). `QuestionAnswer.tsx` shows one radio (or checkbox for Claude `multiSelect`) per option and a text field for a free answer (password field for a secret Codex question); "Send answer" stays disabled until every question has an answer; the other options (Claude `deny`) stay buttons. Other requests keep the plain option buttons.
  - Tests: the shapes equal the recorded answers of `spikes/s2-codex/fixtures/question_rpc.jsonl` and `spikes/s1-claude/fixtures/rust/ask_user_question.jsonl` (`web/src/session/question.test.ts`, with board fixtures from the production cores); `fake-codex` (`sim`) asks a question for a prompt `question <text>`; station test `a_codex_question_takes_its_answers_and_an_approval_refuses_an_input_edit`; phone flow `web/e2e-real/codex.spec.ts` `a Codex question gets its answer from the phone answer form`.
- Reason: a question's answers are the content of the answer, not an edit of a tool input, so the `edit_input` capability does not apply; giving Codex `edit_input` would wrongly allow edited command approvals. A node from the core keeps one rule for answer confirmation (provider evidence after the write), and `serverRequest/resolved` is that evidence for Codex. Building the shape from the recorded input keeps the board free of provider-specific forms beyond the three recorded shapes.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-069: The real-process spawn helper waits for the chosen listing (M2 round 40)

- Context: the clean-checkout `make check` of commit `b139891` (`/tmp/fd-clean3.lVFXGk/v2`) failed once in `web/e2e-real/codex.spec.ts` `a Codex session on alpha asks for an approval that the needs-you rail answers`: the form showed `Chosen: <home>/projects` instead of `<home>/projects/demo`. The helper clicked "Open demo" and then "Use this directory" at once. The projects listing also has a "Use this directory" button, so the click took the projects path before the demo listing arrived. The board did what the screen showed; the test did not wait.
- Options: (a) disable "Use this directory" while a listing loads; (b) make the test wait until the listing path is the workspace before it chooses.
- Choice: (b). `web/e2e-real/board.ts` (`Phone.spawn`) and `web/e2e-real/mvp.spec.ts` (`spawn`) wait for `.fd-path` to equal the station workspace before they click "Use this directory". The scripted flow `web/e2e/spawn.spec.ts` already waits for "Up one level".
- Reason: the board behavior is correct (the button takes the path on screen), so a product change is not necessary; the defect is a missing wait in the test helper.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-070: M2 gate correction: answer admission before the claim, and session-loss cleanup (M2 round 41)

- Context: the independent adversary CODE REVIEW of `b139891` (plus the round-40 diff) failed M2 with two MAJOR defects, although `make check` passed all 14 commands. (1) `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/decide.rs` claimed the answer of a request (`claim_answer`, the first receipt, `deny_at` cleared) before the provider checked the answer payload. A first Codex question answer without `updated_input` was accepted, the Codex core then refused it (`ProviderError::InvalidAnswer`), and every later answer was `Repeated` and sent nothing: the question could never be answered (reproduction `adv_an_invalid_first_answer_consumes_the_question`). (2) The station restart recovery writes `turn.completed(failed)`, `run.completed(failed)`, `session.state(lost)` and no `session.ended`. `web/src/session/timeline.ts` ignored the loss, so a nested subagent and its command stayed running in the D15 tree (reproduction `/tmp/adv-d15/lost.test.ts`: one running subagent and one running command); the Rust summary fold also kept the running state after a loss.
- Choice (the architect's decisions 1–15 of the round-41 brief):
  - Shared validator: `question_reply` in `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/requests.rs` is now `pub fn validate_question_answer(params, input) -> Result<Value>`, exported from `codex/mod.rs`; `CodexCore::answer` and station admission call the same pure function, so the rules and the native RPC result shape are the same.
  - Admission: in the `Answer` branch of `decide`, a first answer (`state == Open`, no receipt) to an offered option of a `question` request goes through `admit_answer` before `claim_answer`, in the command transaction. The provider comes from the durable session summary; a Claude question passes unchanged. For Codex, the matching `OpenRequest` of `SessionSummary.open_requests` gives the input and the channel: an RPC question needs `validate_question_answer(input, updated_input)`; a message question needs a non-empty `text` for option `answer` (the core's `is_empty` rule), and an offered native option needs no text. A missing summary, provider, open request or input fails closed with `invalid_command`. A refusal writes only its rejection receipt (the decision rolls back), so the request stays open, unclaimed, with its `deny_at`. Repeated or closed answers are not checked as new payloads (first outcome, L3). An unknown option keeps its `invalid_command` from `claim_answer`.
  - Release of a proved unsent answer (defense in depth): `settle` in `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/effects.rs`, in its fenced `Claimed`-only `NotDispatched` branch and the same transaction, calls `requests::release_unsent_answer` for an `Answer` effect. It clears the answer receipt only when the request is open, belongs to the effect's session and process, and its receipt has the effect's `command_id`, option and `by`; anything else changes nothing. `deny_at` returns to the request's own deadline (`Wait` → none; `DenyAfter` → `requested_at + after_ms`, the same `deadline` helper as `observe`). The command receipt and the retired effect stay as they are; a new command id can answer. Worker restart, process loss, station recovery and `OutcomeUnknown` never release.
  - Loss cleanup: `lose_session` in `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/recovery.rs` writes `session.state(lost)` before the final `background.updated([])` (only when the summary had background tasks); the turn and run failures stay first. The summary fold (`crates/fleetdeck-proto/src/fold.rs`) clears `current_run`, `current_turn`, `running_tools`, `background_tasks` and `background_agents` on `session.state` `lost` or `exited`, as on `session.ended`, and keeps the history, the request list (requests close by their own events) and `last_error`. The timeline ends every running tool and running or background subagent (`ended`), completes open messages with their text, expires open requests (`session lost` or `session ended`) and clears the task list, for `session.state` `lost`/`exited` and for `session.ended`; terminal statuses stay. For an old recorded recovery sequence (`background.updated([])` just before the loss), the subagents that this immediately preceding event marked `done` become `ended`; earlier finished subagents stay `done`. `subagentLabel('ended')` is now the neutral `stopped`. `run.completed` and `turn.completed` behave as before: background subagents continue across runs.
  - Unplanned details (recorded here): the timeline also reads the kind `session.state_changed` (the Rust variant name; the wire kind is `session.state`), so the adversary's hand-written reproduction gets the same cleanup and an old client message never leaves a running node. The goal's acceptance filter `cargo test -p fleetdeck-station manager::codex_tests` matches no test, because the module path is `manager::tests::codex` (`manager/tests.rs` declares `#[path = "codex_tests.rs"] mod codex;`, and `manager/mod.rs` is not an owned file); the evidence uses `cargo test -p fleetdeck-station manager::tests::codex` and also records the zero-test result of the literal filter. The store regressions are in the new `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/m2_gate_tests.rs`, a child module of `effects_tests.rs` (which was 775 lines). A message-channel question has no `fake-codex sim` scenario (`sim_codex.rs` is not an owned file), so its station test writes the question events into the store for a Codex session without an adapter process; the worker then proves each answer unsent (`no_process`), which also tests the release end to end.
- Reason: a payload that the provider will refuse must never take the request's only answer slot; checking it with the provider's own pure validator in the command transaction keeps one rule in one place and adds no schema, wire or ambiguity change. The release covers the remaining case (a refusal before any I/O) with proof from the live, fenced worker only, so no ambiguous effect is ever sent again. A lost or exited process runs nothing, so every view must show zero running work; writing the loss before the empty list lets the timeline end background children instead of marking them done.
- Regression evidence: station `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/codex_tests.rs::{a_malformed_first_answer_is_refused_and_a_corrected_one_is_delivered_once, an_empty_free_answer_to_a_message_question_is_refused_before_the_claim}`; store `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/m2_gate_tests.rs` (8: `a_not_dispatched_answer_opens_its_request_again_with_its_own_deadline`, `a_released_wait_request_gets_no_deadline`, `sent_unknown_and_confirmed_answers_keep_their_claim`, `a_fenced_out_worker_releases_nothing`, `a_closed_request_stays_closed_after_a_not_dispatched_answer`, `an_effect_that_does_not_own_the_receipt_releases_nothing`, `a_lost_process_expires_the_answered_request_without_a_release`, `a_lost_session_reports_the_loss_before_it_empties_the_background_list`); provider `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/tests_review.rs::the_station_validator_is_the_core_validator_over_the_event_input`; fold `crates/fleetdeck-proto/src/fold_tests.rs::{a_lost_session_runs_nothing_but_keeps_its_requests_and_error, a_loss_with_only_background_work_clears_it, a_normal_run_completion_keeps_background_work}`; timeline `web/src/session/timeline.test.ts` describe `session loss and exit end the tree (M2 gate correction)` (7) and `a child completion leaves its parent subagent active`. Both adversary reproductions now pass their intent.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)

## DL-071: M2 gate closed; T3 P2 contracts applied to PLAN (Revision 3)

- Context: the first independent M2 gate review (adversary `conv-5b2778fe`, `gpt-6.1-sol`) on `b139891` plus pending work FAILED with two MAJOR defects, although `make check` passed all 14 commands there. The correction `767cc99` (DL-070) and the documentation commit `548d947` followed. The architect's clean-clone `make check` of `548d947` passed all 14 commands (Rust 837 passed, 3 skipped; TS drift 2; web 88 pass, 0 fail; replays 29 Claude, 18 Codex; both browser suites). A fresh adversary CODE REVIEW of `548d947` (subagent `sa-f448d108`, `gpt-6.1-sol`) gave VERDICT: PASS with three open items (P2-a, P2-b, unverified provider survival after a station SIGKILL). No physical two-machine demo, real phone, real LAN or tailnet, or macOS run was done. The P2 lessons L13, L14 and L19 of `t3code-lessons.md` and the `ShimHost` contract were not yet binding PLAN text before M3 (Phases 6–7).
- Options: (a) close the gate and start M3 with the P2 lessons as background reading only; (b) close the gate, record it, write an owner demo checklist, and make L13, L14, L19 and `ShimHost` binding PLAN contracts before M3, with the open items as required M3 hardening; (c) keep the gate open until the owner runs the physical demo.
- Choice: (b). New `docs/v2/GATE-M2.md` records only the recorded gate facts (first review FAIL, correction, independent checks, adversary re-review PASS, open items, not validated). New `docs/v2/DEMO-M2.md` is the owner MVP demo checklist; every step is marked not performed. PLAN Revision 3 (note under the title): L13 in §6.3 (`turn.finalized`), §6.6 and Phase 6 (hidden Git refs at turn start and completion, two events, diffs from checkpoints, revert rejected before any filesystem mutation, no filesystem-only restore called a rollback, negative and effect-boundary tests); L14 in §7.1, §9 and Phase 6 (the coordinator API is an MCP server: station loopback endpoint, deck checks the grant, authority bound to station + app session + provider instance + native thread, idle expiry, revocation at session end, raw credential never stored, receipts/outbox with stable ids, recheck in the decision transaction, ADR-006 before wiring); L19 in A10 and Phase 7 (auto-continue per session, default OFF, identity matches, outbox intent, dispatch rechecks, explicit `lost`/`not_resumable`); `ShimHost` in Phase 7 (ADR-005 before implementation; fencing, exclusive reattachment, bounded buffers, cursor recovery, explicit exit state, socket permissions, failure tests, no `DirectChildHost` drop-kills behavior); cutover deletes v0 code on `v2` only, and the merge into `main` and the `2.0.0` release are owner actions; P2-a, P2-b and the SIGKILL survival test are required M3 hardening items (Phases 6 and 7). D1–D15, D13 (loopback, private LAN, tailnet, never public), PLAN §8 and every M1/M2 contract are unchanged.
- Reason: the automated gate passed after an independent adversary re-review, and the open items are not proven defects of the M2 scope; the physical demo needs the owner and real hardware, so the checklist makes that gap explicit instead of blocking on it. The first gate showed that green checks can miss real defects, so the M3 contracts must be exact and reviewed before the code that builds them. Merge and release change `main` for every user, so only the owner does them.
- Owner review: architect decision; contracts await adversary DESIGN REVIEW before M3 implementation

## DL-072: Revision 3 contracts corrected after the design review

- Context: the adversary DESIGN REVIEW of `4935e26` (subagent `sa-963d0462`, `gpt-6.1-sol`) gave VERDICT: FAIL with five MAJOR contract findings. It claimed no code exploit. It confirmed as correct: the `turn.completed`/`turn.finalized` separation, the grant recheck in the decision transaction, the conservative default-off auto-continue, the descriptions of P2-a, P2-b and the SIGKILL test, the honest GATE-M2 and DEMO-M2 records, and the owner-only merge and release. The architect accepted all five MAJOR findings.
- Options: (a) correct the PLAN contracts now and get a fresh design review before M3; (b) start M3 and fix the contracts in the ADRs during implementation. Option (b) puts unreviewed authority and data-loss rules into code first.
- Choice: (a). PLAN text changes, each marked "(Revision 3, design review)":
  1. Rejected revert vs receipts (§6.6): a rejected revert leaves workspace files, checkpoint refs, session history and execution effects unchanged. Ordinary command rejection still commits the rejection receipt and the required audit record (§6.4); a repeat returns that receipt; admission exceptions stay unchanged. The rollback-at-every-effect-boundary test stays.
  2. Consistent checkpoint boundary (§6.6): the start checkpoint is published before input that can modify the workspace; the completion checkpoint is captured at a fenced workspace boundary; writes of a later turn never enter an earlier checkpoint; native queued work or background writers make the checkpoint unavailable with an explicit reason and disable its revert, without a delay of `turn.completed`. `turn.finalized` is committed only after its Git objects and refs exist, idempotent per app turn; recovery never captures the current workspace as an earlier completion. Required tests: delayed finalization with T2 writes, background writes after the parent turn, crashes at each Git and SQLite publication boundary.
  3. Checkpoint scope and restore ownership (§6.6): ref namespace of station incarnation, worktree, app session and app turn; canonical worktree identity and snapshot scope on each checkpoint; a checkpoint of another worktree or session is rejected. The first implementation snapshots tracked content only (with tracked edits, without a change to the real index), excludes untracked and ignored files, does not clean them or overwrite untracked conflicts, preserves the index, and makes unsupported submodules, formats or sizes unavailable before restore. Broader coverage needs a separate snapshot contract.
  4. Revert concurrency fence and mixed outcomes (§6.6, Phase 6): an exclusive session/worktree execution fence from admission through reconciliation; a running turn, pending input or active writer prevents revert; a recheck of identities, checkpoint and workspace generation before external I/O. The provider conversation rollback needs a correlated confirmation before the filesystem restore; each external step is durable; an uncertain or partial rollback blocks input with an explicit reconciliation-required state; recovery uses evidence and never repeats a claimed rollback blindly; success only when the conversation and the workspace both match. Required tests: queued-input races, concurrent writers, provider uncertainty, restore failure, crashes between the two external operations. The Phase 6 checkpoint bullet points to these rules.
  5. MCP ingress authentication and credential bootstrap (§7.1, §9): mandatory per-session authentication on the loopback MCP endpoint; caller identity from the station's private credential binding, never from loopback, a PID or tool parameters; the deck verifies the binding against the pinned station and the grant. Authority is bound to the station identity, app session, provider instance and provider-native conversation identity (Claude `session_id`, Codex `threadId`) with an explicit generation, and no authority exists before the native identity is known. ADR-006 specifies and tests a provider-supported private credential handoff; raw credentials never enter argv, process-readable configuration, URLs, events, databases or logs; the same-UID boundary is explicit; without the handoff the coordinator capability is disabled. Idle expiry, invalidation on process loss or native identity replacement, revocation at session end; a renewal never authorizes an old queued operation.
- Deferred to the ADRs (not PLAN blockers; each decided in the ADR before implementation):
  - ADR-005 (Phase 7): attachment ownership (database incarnation separate from attachment-owner generation, takeover fencing, stale-writer rejection, authenticated exclusive attachment); output recovery (cursor acknowledgement relative to committed station events, a crash after an output read but before the commit, partial frames, buffer exhaustion, explicit output gaps); platform and upgrades (private socket placement and authentication on Linux and macOS, stale sockets, protocol negotiation, incompatible upgrades, rollback); auto-continue integration (live reattachment is not native resume, the permission mode and offline approval policy stay, an expired approval never becomes authorization, uncertain inputs are reconciled before continuation work).
  - ADR-006 (Phase 6, §7.1): wire and authorization (the G3 envelopes and ambiguity rules, G5 revocation, the G6/G7 direction and scope checks; the tool/class matrix; transactional spawn-cap accounting; duplicate handling; expiry behavior for queries).
- Unchanged: D1–D15, D13 (loopback, private LAN, tailnet, never public), PLAN §8, and every M1/M2 contract. No source, test or tooling file changed.
- Reason: each finding was a gap in which a correct implementation of the old text could lose data, revert the wrong state, or give authority to an unauthenticated local caller. Exact contracts before code are cheaper than fixes after M3 code. The ADR items need design detail that the PLAN does not hold, so they are required ADR decisions, not PLAN text.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12); the fresh adversary DESIGN REVIEW passed (DL-073)

## DL-073: M3 starts after the design review PASS

- Context: DL-072 corrected the PLAN Revision 3 contracts and required a fresh adversary DESIGN REVIEW before M3 implementation. The adversary DESIGN REVIEW of `3fbc4c5` (subagent `sa-f21ff469`, `gpt-6.1-sol`) gave VERDICT: PASS with no findings. It confirmed that DL-072 items 1–5 are closed (PLAN §6.6 rejected revert, checkpoint boundary, checkpoint scope, revert fence and mixed outcomes; §7.1 and §9 MCP ingress authentication) and that the ADR-005/ADR-006 deferrals are acceptable because the PLAN requires each decision before implementation.
- Options: (a) start M3 now with the M3 goal loop; (b) wait for the owner.
- Choice: (a). The owner asked for M2 and M3 to be finished without further questions (2026-10-12). M3 runs as one goal loop of at most 40 rounds (more than the 9 left of the old 50-round M2+M3 budget, because Phases 6–7 need more rounds). Clean-checkout checks run on `/mnt/rust-cow` (btrfs, shared with the kache build cache). Merge to `main` and the `2.0.0` release stay owner steps.
- Reason: the precondition of DL-072 is met; waiting adds no information.
- Owner review: approved (owner, 2026-10-12)
- Owner answers (2026-10-12), recorded by the architect:
  - DL-036 to DL-073: approved.
  - ADR-005 (`ShimHost`) and ADR-006 (coordinator MCP grants): approved in advance. The loop writes each ADR with every required decision of PLAN Phases 6–7 before its implementation and sets its status to "Accepted (owner pre-approval, 2026-10-12)". A later deviation from a written ADR still needs a new DECISIONS-LOG entry.
  - Parity rows that M3 marks "deferred: owner decision needed": the owner approves these deferrals. Each deferral still needs its own DECISIONS-LOG entry with the reason.
  - Merge of `v2` into `main` and the `2.0.0` release: the owner does these after M3 is complete. They stay out of the loop.


## DL-074: M3 hardening P2-a, P2-b and the station SIGKILL test

- Context: GATE-M2 §6 left three required M3 items (PLAN Phase 6 "Required M3 hardening"). P2-a: a start whose process spawned but whose first input failed only logged the failure. P2-b: a Codex message-channel answer goes to the provider as a user message under the input id `answer-<request>`, and no event confirmed its effect. The SIGKILL item: no test showed what a direct-child provider does after an uncatchable station kill. The M3 goal text (2026-10-12) also says that ADR-005 and ADR-006 stay "Proposed" and unwired until the architect records an adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS for each ADR in the gate-state file; DL-073 records an owner pre-approval that would set them "Accepted". The executor follows the stricter goal text and records the difference here for the architect.
- Options (P2-a): (a) fail the start effect when the first input fails; (b) keep the start confirmed (the spawn is the provider's acceptance of the start) and record the input outcome as its own durable evidence; (c) queue the input again as a new prompt effect. Option (a) reports a running session as a failed start; option (c) can send an input twice after an ambiguous write, which breaks the ambiguous-send rule.
- Choice (P2-a): (b). `store::effects::first_input_failed` runs in one transaction with the effect reason. A refusal before any I/O (`Dispatch::NotDispatched`) stores the reason `first_input_not_sent: <why>` and appends `input.updated discarded` and `error first_input_not_sent`. A failure that may have reached the provider (`Dispatch::Unknown`: a stdin write error, a closed sink, undelivered events) stores `first_input_unknown: <why>` and appends `error delivery_unknown`; the input is not marked discarded and is never sent again. The session stays usable. Tests: `manager::first_input_tests::{a_refused_first_input_is_discarded_with_an_error_and_the_session_stays_usable, a_first_input_that_may_have_reached_the_provider_is_unknown_and_never_resent}`.
- Options (P2-b): (a) give the Codex question request a synthetic node id; (b) make the answer input id a shared correlation contract and confirm the answer effect on the provider's `input.updated started` for that id; (c) confirm on `approval.resolved`. Option (a) invents a tool node; option (c) is the adapter's own bookkeeping, not provider evidence that the message started.
- Choice (P2-b): (b). `fleetdeck_providers::adapter::{ANSWER_INPUT_PREFIX, answer_input_id, answered_request}` own the id format; the Codex core uses `answer_input_id`. The station observer confirms an open (`claimed` or `outcome_unknown`) `answer` effect with correlation `<request>` on `input.updated started` of `answer-<request>`, and retires it (`refused_by_provider`) on `input.updated refused`. Station input ids are ULIDs, so no host input has the prefix. Tests: `store::effects_tests::answer_input::{a_message_answer_is_confirmed_when_its_input_starts, an_outcome_unknown_message_answer_is_confirmed_by_a_late_start, a_refused_message_answer_input_retires_its_effect}`; the existing Codex core test `codex::tests` still checks `clientUserMessageId = answer-<request>`.
- Choice (SIGKILL): a new real-process test `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/station_sigkill.rs` kills `fleetdeck station serve` with SIGKILL while one `fake-claude sim` session is idle and one is in a turn. Result on Linux: both providers read the end of stdin and exit; no non-zombie member of either provider process group stays; the restarted station reports both sessions `lost` and starts no replacement process. No code fix was necessary. Limit: a provider that ignores the end of its input survives until its next stdout write (EPIPE); the direct-child host has no identity-checked orphan reaping (a PID alone is not process identity). The durable `ShimHost` (ADR-005) owns process identity. The test is Linux-only (`/proc`); macOS is owner/platform validation.
- Reason: each choice keeps the ambiguous-send rule (no blind resend) and uses provider evidence, not adapter bookkeeping, for confirmation.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 1)

## DL-075: ADR-005 (`ShimHost`) and ADR-006 (coordinator MCP) written as *Proposed* (M3 round 2)

- Context: PLAN Phases 6–7 require both ADRs, with every "Required ADR-00x decision", before any wiring. The M3 goal says that each ADR stays *Proposed* until the architect records an adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS in the gate-state file; the gate-state file says the architect starts that review when the ADR is *Proposed* and committed. Round-2 work stays uncommitted for the goal reviewer, so the round-3 start commit makes both ADRs reviewable.
- Options (ADR-006 credential handoff): (a) credential in an environment variable of the provider child, referenced by name from the provider's MCP configuration (Claude `--mcp-config` `headers` with `${VAR}`, Codex `bearer_token_env_var`); (b) a stdio MCP proxy subcommand started by the provider; (c) `SO_PEERCRED` on a Unix socket. (b) needs the same environment handoff plus one more process; (c) gives a PID and a shared UID, which PLAN §7.1 says is not authentication.
- Choice (ADR-006): (a), with the capability disabled (`handoff_unverified`) until the opt-in live smoke records a passing handoff per provider version. Other executor choices in ADR-006: the M2 `token_digest` field leaves the wire (the binding facts are checked against the deck's projection of `coordinator.bound` events); the binding generation is 0 before the native identity is known, 1 after, +1 per owner renewal; coordinators cannot answer approvals in M3; `spawn` can never carry a grant (no transitive authority); spawn slots are never returned; defaults idle 30 min, expiry 12 h (max 7 days), cap 3 (max 50); link feature `coordinator_v1` for N−1.
- Choice (ADR-005): one `fleetdeck shim` per session (`setsid`, Linux `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` on the provider); station incarnation and attachment-owner generation as separate values; HMAC attach proof with a per-shim key file (0600 in a 0700 directory, never in the database), peer-UID check; process identity = PID + start time + boot id; byte-offset output cursor committed with the events, 16 MiB ring buffer with backpressure, explicit `session.output_gap` only below the shim's floor; stdin `write_id` deduplication and `write_status` to settle uncertain writes; exit state kept until acknowledged (7-day exit file); shim protocol N/N−1; reattach before any L19 path; expired approvals answered deny/cancel, never allow.
- Reason: each choice is the smallest one that meets the PLAN rules and the design-review decision groups while it keeps the M2 ambiguity rules (no blind resend) and fails closed when a provider behavior is not proven.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 2); adversary DESIGN REVIEW of both ADRs pending (architect).

## DL-076: ADR-005 §4.3 corrected: partial and in-flight stdin writes stay `outcome_unknown` (M3 round 3)

- Context: the goal review of round 2 found that ADR-005 §4.3 let `write_status` turn a write into `not_dispatched` with no rule for writes that were partial or in flight, and with no crash-boundary tests. It also used `sent` and moved effects out of `outcome_unknown`, which the M2 effect model (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/effects.rs`: `queued → claimed → confirmed`, or `retired_not_dispatched` / `outcome_unknown`; a claim is never dispatched again) does not allow.
- Options: (a) map every `write_status` answer to `outcome_unknown` (no shim-based retirement at all); (b) allow `retired_not_dispatched` only for proven zero-byte cases (`not_received` with `write_seq > evicted_through` and a tombstone, or `failed_unwritten`), only for effects still `claimed`, and only when the crash-boundary tests pass; (c) keep the round-2 text.
- Choice: (b). The shim creates the write record before the first byte; `received`, `writing`, `written`, `partial`, `forgotten`, no answer and a lost shim all give `outcome_unknown` and no resend; a partial write also sets `input_blocked { stdin_partial_write }` (only interrupt, stop and kill admitted). An effect already `outcome_unknown` never becomes `retired_not_dispatched`. The implementation maps every answer to `outcome_unknown` until tests 26–35 (ADR-005 §9) exist and pass in the same change. Test 12 now covers the full answer table.
- Reason: (b) keeps the M2 ambiguous-send rule exactly (no byte reached the provider is the only path to "not dispatched") and still lets a station that crashed before the send release the input. (a) is safe but leaves every crash-before-send input unknown, so the owner must resend it by hand; the gate makes (b) fall back to (a) until the tests prove it. (c) is unsafe.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 3); the adversary DESIGN REVIEW of ADR-005 should use the corrected text (round-3 work, committed at the round-4 start).

## DL-077: The Git layer of turn checkpoints (M3 round 3, 6-8)

- Context: PLAN §6.6 (Revision 3) fixes the checkpoint contract: hidden refs, a namespace of station incarnation + worktree + app session + app turn with a canonical worktree identity, a tracked-content snapshot without a change to the real index, explicit unavailability for unsupported submodules, formats and sizes, idempotent publication, and a restore that never cleans untracked or ignored files and never overwrites untracked conflicts. It leaves the Git mechanics, the limits and the identity format open. This layer is station-internal (no wire, no event schema); `turn.finalized`, the fence and revert admission come next.
- Options (snapshot): (a) a private copy of the real index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`) plus `git add -u` and `write-tree`; (b) `git stash create`; (c) a read of HEAD into an empty private index plus `add -u`. (b) runs with the real index and includes staged state in a merge commit with two trees; (c) loses files that are staged as new.
- Choice (snapshot): (a). The private index lives in the worktree's Git directory (so a split index finds its shared file) and is removed on drop. Every call removes the variables that redirect Git, sets `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, turns off hooks, fsmonitor and auto-maintenance, and (for checkpoints) reads no system or user configuration. The commit has a fixed author `FleetDeck <checkpoint@fleetdeck.invalid>`, no signature, HEAD as parent when it exists, and a body with `worktree-id`, `incarnation`, `session`, `turn`, `phase` and `scope: tracked`.
- Choice (identity and refs): worktree id = first 16 bytes (hex) of SHA-256 over the canonical worktree root, NUL, and the canonical common Git directory. Ref = `refs/fleetdeck/checkpoints/<incarnation>/<worktree id>/<session>/<turn>/<start|end>`; key parts allow only `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -` (max 64). Publication is `update-ref <ref> <commit> ""` (create only); a second capture of a key returns the first checkpoint (`Existing`). A checkpoint whose body or ref names another worktree id is rejected (`OtherWorktree`).
- Choice (unavailable): gitlink entries (submodules), unmerged entries, skip-worktree or assume-unchanged entries (`S`/`s`), a `filter` or `working-tree-encoding` attribute, a non-UTF-8 path, more than 100,000 tracked files, a tracked file above 50 MiB, or more than 512 MiB in total. Each has a stable code (`Unavailable::code`); nothing is published. The defaults are executor choices.
- Choice (restore): two steps. `plan_restore` makes no change: it checks ownership, the target tree (submodules, sizes, filters), the current snapshot, and every changed path (no untracked or ignored file where the target creates a path, no directory where a tracked file is removed or changed, no symlink ancestor; an ancestor that is a tracked file being removed is allowed). `apply_restore` removes the tracked files that the target does not have and writes the other changed paths with `checkout-index` from a private index of the target tree; it reports `matches` (the target content is in place, checked through a private index, so a restored file that the real index does not track still counts). Restore deletes tracked files that the turn added, because the target state does not have them; it never touches untracked or ignored files and never writes the real index.
- Reason: (a) is the only option that captures exactly "tracked working-tree content, including tracked edits" without touching the real index; the two-step restore gives "rejected before any mutation" by construction.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 3)

## DL-078: `turn.finalized` wire shape and the checkpoint boundary rules (M3 round 4, 6-8)

- Context: PLAN §6.3 lists `turn.finalized { turn_id, checkpoint?, diff_summary? }` and §6.6 requires a consistent boundary (start published before workspace-modifying input; completion at a fenced boundary, unavailable with an explicit reason when native queued work or background writers prevent it; never delaying `turn.completed`). The PLAN does not fix the field types or the boundary algorithm. Also, round-3 review: assume-unchanged entries must make a checkpoint unavailable (Git does not look at their working-tree files, so `add -u` would leave out their edits).
- Options (wire): (a) one `checkpoint` object with a start side and an end side, each `available {commit}` or `unavailable {reason}`; (b) flat optional fields (`start_commit`, `end_commit`, `reason`). (b) cannot say which side is unavailable and why.
- Choice (wire): (a). `TurnCheckpoint { worktree_id?, scope, start: CheckpointSide, end: CheckpointSide }`, `CheckpointSide = available {commit} | unavailable {reason}` (tag `state`), `DiffSummary { files_changed, insertions, deletions }` set only when both sides are available. `checkpoint` stays optional as in the PLAN; a station with the workspace service always sets it (`not_a_git_worktree` when the session directory is not a worktree). An old peer keeps `turn.finalized` as an unknown kind (L16). The fold ignores it: it never changes the turn, the run or the activity (test `a_late_turn_finalized_changes_neither_the_turn_nor_the_activity`).
- Options (boundary): (a) capture the start at `turn.started` and the end at `turn.completed` in the event pump; (b) capture the start in the dispatch of each input under a per-session workspace fence, and run a due completion capture under the same fence, which the next dispatch runs first; (c) pause the provider. (a) captures after the provider can already write; (c) is not supported by the providers.
- Choice (boundary): (b), as pure bookkeeping in `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/boundary.rs` (rules in its module docs). Reason codes: `turn_running`, `queued_input`, `background_writers`, `provider_initiated`, `session_ended`, `station_restart`, `not_a_git_worktree`, `capture_failed`, plus the snapshot codes of DL-077 (now with `assume_unchanged` and `unknown_index_entry`; any `ls-files -v` tag other than `H`/`S` fails closed). A turn takes the start of the oldest waiting input; an input that joins a running turn (`input.updated` started) leaves the queue; `received` keeps it queued.
- Next (not done in this round): a durable `turn_checkpoints` row per app turn (station-minted ULID used as the ref key, because provider turn ids such as `turn-1` repeat across processes), the wiring in the effect dispatch and the pump, recovery of an interrupted publication from that row and the refs (never a new capture), and the boundary tests against the fake providers.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 4)

## DL-079: Invalidation of pending boundary captures by unfenced work (M3 round 5, 6-8)

- Context: the round-4 goal review asked: invalidate pending completion captures when a provider-initiated turn or a background writer starts before the capture, with regression tests. (Revision 1 of this entry also held the ADR review corrections; at the architect's request they are now in one entry per ADR: DL-080 for ADR-005, DL-081 for ADR-006.)
- Options (boundary): (a) invalidate a due or running completion capture (and a running start capture of an unwritten input) when `turn.started` or non-empty `background.updated` arrives before its result is recorded; drop the late result; (b) re-capture later; (c) keep the capture. (b) cannot find a consistent boundary while unfenced work runs; (c) can mix writes of the provider's work into the earlier checkpoint.
- Choice (boundary): (a) in `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/boundary.rs`: `take_due` moves the due turn to `capturing`; `end_captured` returns the finalization only if the capture is still valid; an invalidated completion is finalized at once as unavailable (`provider_initiated`, `queued_input` or `background_writers`). Only inputs marked written (`input_sent`) can start a turn, so a provider turn during a start capture is `provider_initiated` and the input's start becomes `turn_running` (or `background_writers`); a late `record_start` never overwrites an invalidated side. Residual window: a provider event that is still in the stdout pipe when a capture result is recorded cannot be seen; the station cannot fence provider-initiated work, and the PLAN asks for a consistent boundary only where the station can establish one. Tests: `a_provider_initiated_turn_invalidates_a_due_completion_capture`, `a_provider_initiated_turn_during_a_completion_capture_drops_its_result`, `a_background_writer_invalidates_a_due_or_running_completion_capture`, `a_provider_turn_or_background_work_invalidates_a_running_start_capture`.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 5)

## DL-080: ADR-005 revision 2 after DESIGN REVIEW FAIL sa-f717e02d (M3 round 5)

- Context: adversary DESIGN REVIEW sa-f717e02d of ADR-005 at 7965c6e + DL-076: VERDICT FAIL (9 MAJOR, 2 MINOR; findings in the architect's review file). The findings: (1) reattachment does not restore provider adapter state and the host interface is undefined; (2) a historical database restore can repeat executed commands; (3) eviction removes duplicate-write protection and the tombstone conflicts with immutable effects; (4) shim death does not prove that provider execution ended; (5) a stdout line larger than the ring buffer deadlocks; (6) startup and exit-publication crash boundaries are unresolved; (7) approval recovery relies on an undefined provider timestamp; (8) a protocol-incompatible live shim can enter lost → auto-continue; (9) stdin buffering is unbounded and blocked writes lack an independent control path; MINOR: a reference to a nonexistent §3.4, and 12-character socket names can collide.
- Options: (a) apply each required text as binding contract text (only section numbers and wording adapted), remove each contradicted older sentence, add the named tests, and map each finding to its section; (b) argue against findings. No finding conflicts with the PLAN, so (b) has no basis.
- Choice: (a). Tests 36–45 added; §12 maps each finding.

- Context: adversary DESIGN REVIEW sa-7bc9f9f0 of ADR-006 at 7965c6e: VERDICT FAIL (7 MAJOR, 1 MINOR; findings in the architect's review file). - Options: (a) apply each required text as binding contract text, remove each contradicted older sentence, add the named tests, and map each finding to its section; (b) argue against findings. No finding conflicts with the PLAN §7.1 or L14, so (b) has no basis.

Tests 5a–30a added; §13 maps each finding.

## DL-082: Durable turn checkpoint rows and start recovery from refs (M3 round 6, 6-8)

- Context: PLAN §6.6 (DL-072) requires `turn.finalized` only after its Git objects and refs exist, idempotent per app turn, and a ref namespace keyed by an app turn. Provider turn ids are per-process counters (`turn-1`), so they repeat across processes. A station crash can stop between the durable intent, the ref publication and the record of its result. The PLAN does not name the table, the side states or the recovery rule.
- Options: (a) no durable row; derive everything from refs at start; (b) one `turn_checkpoints` row per app turn (station-minted id), written before the ref, each side decided once, and start recovery that settles undecided sides from the refs alone without a new capture; (c) like (b), but recovery captures missing sides from the current workspace.
- Choice: (b). Schema V6 `turn_checkpoints` (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/schema.rs`, `store/turn_checkpoints.rs`): the row is planned (idempotent per session + input) before any ref; the start side is `capturing`, `available {commit}` or `unavailable {reason}` and is never `open`; the end side is `open` until the completion boundary; a decided side never changes, so a late capture result is dropped; the provider turn is bound once; `turn.finalized` is appended once in the transaction that settles the row, with a diff summary only when both sides are available; a row without a provider turn is `abandoned` with no event. Recovery (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/settle.rs`, called from `SessionManager::start` after store recovery): `capturing` + ref → available; `capturing` without ref, `open`, or a gone/moved/other worktree → `unavailable {station_restart}`; a ref that is not ours or a moved commit → `unavailable {capture_failed}`; it never captures.
- Reason: (a) cannot tell a ref of a finished turn from one of a turn that never started, and cannot make `turn.finalized` idempotent; (c) would put writes made after the crash (or by a surviving provider) into the turn's checkpoint, which breaks the §6.6 fenced-boundary rule. With the direct-child host no provider survives a station restart, so every unsettled row is settled at start; ADR-005 reattachment will exclude rows of reattached processes.
- Tests: `store::turn_checkpoints_tests` (6: once with both sides and diff summary; waits for both sides and the provider turn; decided side never changes; plan idempotent and start never open; abandoned without event; rows survive a reopen) and `workspace::settle_tests` (3, real Git: crash after both refs keeps them and finalizes once; crash before a ref makes the side unavailable and never captures; row without a turn abandoned and a vanished worktree unavailable).
- Remaining: wiring of the boundary and the rows into dispatch (start capture before workspace-modifying input) and the pump (bind on `turn.started`, end capture at the boundary, finalize), fake-provider boundary tests, revert.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 6)

## DL-083: Recovery checks recorded `available` checkpoint sides against their refs (M3 round 7, 6-8)

- Context: the round-6 review found that start recovery (DL-082) kept a recorded `available {commit}` side when its ref had disappeared or named another commit, so `turn.finalized` could name a checkpoint that does not exist. The rule "a decided side never changes" also stopped recovery from correcting it.
- Options: (a) trust the recorded side (round 6); (b) check every recorded `available` side against its ref before finalization and withdraw it to `unavailable {reason}` when the ref is gone, moved or not a checkpoint of this worktree; (c) re-publish the ref from the recorded commit.
- Choice: (b). New store operation `withdraw` (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/turn_checkpoints.rs`): it changes `available {commit}` to `unavailable {reason}` only for an open row and only for the expected commit; it is the one exception to "decided once" and runs only before `turn.finalized`. Recovery (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/settle.rs`) keeps the side only when the ref still names the recorded commit; a missing ref or a gone/moved/other worktree gives `checkpoint_missing`; a ref to another commit or to a non-checkpoint commit gives `checkpoint_changed`. The diff summary needs both sides verified.
- Reason: (a) breaks the PLAN §6.6 rule that `turn.finalized` names only existing refs; (c) would restore a ref that someone deleted on purpose and cannot prove the commit object is still complete. A `checkpoint_*` reason tells the board why no diff or revert is offered.
- Tests: `workspace::settle_tests::a_recorded_checkpoint_whose_ref_disappeared_is_finalized_unavailable`, `a_recorded_checkpoint_whose_ref_changed_is_finalized_unavailable` (another checkpoint commit and an ordinary commit), `an_intact_recorded_checkpoint_is_kept_and_a_vanished_worktree_withdraws_it`; `store::turn_checkpoints_tests::a_withdrawal_needs_the_recorded_commit_and_an_open_row`. The three recovery tests fail on the round-6 `settle.rs` and pass with the fix.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 7)

## DL-084: Turn checkpoints wired into dispatch and the event pump (M3 round 8, 6-8)

- Context: rounds 3–7 built the Git checkpoint layer, the pure boundary bookkeeping (DL-078/079), the durable `turn_checkpoints` rows and start recovery (DL-082/083), but no live session used them. PLAN §6.6 requires the start checkpoint before a workspace-modifying input, the completion checkpoint at a fenced boundary that never delays `turn.completed`, and `turn.finalized` only after the refs exist.
- Options: (a) capture inside the effect worker for every effect kind; (b) one `SessionTurns` object per live session that owns the workspace fence, the boundary and the row ids, called by the dispatch of prompt/follow-up/steer and by the pump after each append; (c) capture in the provider adapters.
- Choice: (b), new module `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/turns.rs`, used by `manager/target.rs`.
  - Fence: a per-session async mutex. A prompt or follow-up takes it, runs any due completion capture of an earlier turn first, stores the row (start `capturing` or `unavailable {reason}`), publishes the start ref, records the side, marks the input as sent, and only then writes. A steer takes the fence too (a due capture runs before the steer writes) but has no row. A due capture without a later input runs in its own task under the same fence.
  - Pump: the events are appended first, then fed to `observe` (so `turn.completed` is in the log before any capture starts, L13). `turn.started` binds the provider turn to the row of the oldest written input or stores a provider-initiated row (start `unavailable {provider_initiated}`). An input that leaves the queue without a turn abandons its row (`no_turn`; `session_ended` on process end); an input refused before I/O abandons its row (`not_dispatched`). An `Unknown` write keeps the row until a turn starts or the session ends.
  - Finalization: the row's end side is recorded (`capturing` before the capture starts, so a crash leaves recovery evidence) and `turn.finalized` goes through the shared `workspace::settle::finalize_row`, which checks each `available` side against its ref again before the event is committed (same path as start recovery).
  - A session directory outside a Git worktree is valid: both sides are `unavailable {not_a_git_worktree}` and the turn is still finalized once.
  - Station Git runner: `GitOptions::isolated()` and `Limits::default()` (`SessionTarget::with_checkpoints` for other values).
- Reason: (a) has no event context to decide queued/background boundaries; (c) puts station workspace authority into the provider crate and duplicates it for each provider. With (b) both providers share one boundary and the provider's own diff events stay advisory.
- Tests: `manager::turns::turns_tests` (8, real Git, no provider): start ref before the write and finalization from both refs; a later dispatch captures the earlier completion before its own writes (delayed finalization with T2 writes); background writers after the parent turn; native queued turn; process end with a waiting input; inputs without a turn (refused, discarded); provider-initiated turn; directory outside Git. `manager::tests::checkpoint_wired` (5, `fake-claude sim` and `fake-codex`): a live turn publishes both refs in the station namespace and finalizes after `turn.completed` with a 1-file diff; a follow-up queued behind a running turn; a workspace outside Git; a killed session finalizes its running turn once; a Codex turn finalizes from the station checkpoints.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 8)

## DL-085: ADR-005 revision 3 after DESIGN RE-REVIEW FAIL sa-013bfabf (M3 round 10)

- Context: adversary DESIGN RE-REVIEW sa-013bfabf of ADR-005 revision 2 (frozen 8af7811, DL-080): VERDICT FAIL (BLOCK), 4 MAJOR. Earlier findings 3, 5, 7, 8, 9 and both MINOR are fixed; 1, 2, 4, 6 are partial. The open findings: (F1) output-only checkpoints do not recover outbound adapter transitions (Codex RPC counter and correlations, pending turn before `turn/start`, queued inputs, mode/model change without output), so a crash after a dispatch loses the correlation and can reuse an RPC id; (F2) an activation-trial rollback can discard acknowledged recovery state (events, checkpoint, cursor below the shim floor, attachment generation going backwards); (F3) the session-and-group termination predicate allows a resume beside a `setsid()` descendant (the reviewer reproduced it on Linux); (F4) missing runtime files after a reboot do not prove that a launch never occurred. Note: the status line named DL-079 for revision 2; revision 2 is DL-080.
- Options: (a) apply each required text as binding contract text (only section numbers and wording adapted), remove each contradicted older sentence, add the named tests, map each finding; (b) argue against findings. No finding conflicts with the PLAN Phase 7 rules, so (b) has no basis.
- Choice: (a). Revision 3: every adapter transition is recoverable; command-side transitions are committed with the effect and its dispatch metadata before outbound I/O; an output transaction commits internal effects, adapter state, events and cursor together before the acknowledgment; recovery applies the committed transitions after the checkpoint and never reuses an RPC id; proven unwritten answers keep their request mappings (§2.1, §4.1; F1). A rollback-eligible activation trial performs no attach, no output or exit acknowledgment and no durable reconciliation, checks migrations on a disposable copy, and reads shim versions from rows and journals; after live recovery state advances, the launcher never restores its snapshot (§3.3, §5.3; F2). Session/group absence is a necessary condition only; a verified reboot is the only sufficient automatic evidence; without it the session stays `execution_unknown`/`not_resumable` until an audited owner reconciliation; inspection errors fail closed (§4.6, §6, §7, §10; F3). Launch journal and lock in `<station dir>/shims/` (persistent, independent of the socket directory); the shim durably records `spawn_attempted` before the spawn; only an intact journal without that record retires a launch as `shim_never_started`; missing or invalid evidence gives `outcome_unknown` (§3, §3.2 step 1, §7; F4). Test 40 extended with a `setsid()` descendant; tests 46–50 added; §13 maps the re-review.
- Reason: each required text closes a crash or authority gap that PLAN Phase 7 (fencing, cursor recovery, explicit exit state, "a PID alone is not process identity", L19) requires, and each has a named test.
- Executor choices inside the required texts (owner may change): the journal path `<station dir>/shims/`, the key file moved to that directory, the `created`/`spawn_attempted`/`fenced` journal records, no sufficient termination evidence other than a reboot in this revision (a dedicated cgroup could be added later by a new revision).
- Status: "Proposed, revision 3". Not Accepted, not reviewed; no ShimHost code before the architect records a PASS in "Design approvals".
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 10). DESIGN REVIEW of revision 3: pending (architect).

## DL-086: ADR-006 revision 3 after DESIGN RE-REVIEW FAIL sa-b11bfa3b (M3 round 10)

- Context: adversary DESIGN RE-REVIEW sa-b11bfa3b of ADR-006 revision 2 (frozen 8af7811, DL-081): VERDICT FAIL (BLOCK), 3 MAJOR and 1 MINOR. Earlier findings 1, 4, 6, 7 and the MINOR are fixed; 2, 3, 5 are partial. The open findings: (1) a snapshot acknowledgment does not prove lifecycle synchronization, because snapshots carry no binding state; (2) the rule "apply a lifecycle event only with a higher generation, or an equal one with the same facts" rejects active → expired/revoked at the same generation; (3) renewal and revocation have no link-method contract; (4, MINOR) older wording makes an unsent row `ambiguous` at the deadline.
- Options: (a) apply each required text as binding contract text, remove each contradicted older sentence, add the named tests, map each finding; (b) argue against findings. No finding conflicts with PLAN §7.1 or L14. The architect approved the two link-method rows of finding 3 as written (scope `drive`, deck → station, feature `coordinator_v1`).
- Choice: (a). Revision 3: coordinator-capable snapshots carry a complete binding projection at the watermark, committed with the cursor in one transaction before the acknowledgment; a summary snapshot acknowledgment does not open the lifecycle fence; a snapshot cannot revive a revoked grant or accept an unsolicited renewal generation (§6.1; finding 1). The lifecycle admission rules: equal generations still allow expiry and revocation, a matching `coordinator.ended` moves the current generation, events follow incarnation and sequence order, a repeated `coordinator.bound` is a no-op, activation only for the initial pending binding or the recorded renewal generation, revoked stays terminal (§5.5; finding 2). ADR-004's method table gains exactly `session.coordinator_renew` and `session.coordinator_revoke` (mutating, `drive`, deck → station, `coordinator_v1`, `CommandEnvelope` with station receipts; `SessionCommand::CoordinatorRenew { session_id, grant_id, generation }` and `CoordinatorRevoke { session_id, grant_id }`; the station checks the grant belongs to the session; only owner-authorized deck actions create them) and "rows stay unchanged" is removed (§6.1, §7; finding 3). A never-sent row becomes `failed`/`expired` at the deadline, a sent one `ambiguous`; `sent_at` is committed before the network write; neither is redispatched (§6.5, §9; finding 4). Tests 25d, 25e, 26a–26e, 28b, 28c added and test 28 qualified; §14 maps the re-review.
- Reason: the required texts keep the L14 rules (binding generation, transaction-time checks, revocation terminal, no authority increase on retry or replay) true across snapshot recovery and same-generation transitions, and each has a named negative test.
- Executor choices inside the required texts (owner may change): the `SessionCommand` variant names, a grant/session mismatch as a stored `invalid_command`, the station-local socket answering `forbidden` for both ops, lower-generation lifecycle events ignored.
- Status: "Proposed, revision 3". Not Accepted, not reviewed; no coordinator MCP wiring before the architect records a PASS in "Design approvals".
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 10). DESIGN REVIEW of revision 3: pending (architect).

## DL-087: Fenced `session.revert` with provider rollback before any file change (M3 round 10)

- Context: PLAN §6.6 and L13 require a revert that checks the session, provider, process, checkpoints, policy and readiness before any filesystem mutation; rejects when the provider cannot roll back its conversation; holds an exclusive session/worktree fence through reconciliation; gets a correlated provider rollback before the file restore; makes each external step durable; blocks input in an explicit reconciliation-required state when the rollback is uncertain or partial; never repeats a claimed rollback; and reports success only when conversation and workspace both match. The PLAN does not name the wire shapes, the provider mechanism or the durable steps.
- Options: (a) a file-only restore with a "conversation not rolled back" warning; (b) a new mutating method `session.revert` with a station-side durable revert row, a provider rollback operation per adapter and a fenced executor in the effect worker; (c) roll the conversation back with a new forked session. (a) breaks L13 ("do not claim a filesystem-only restore is a conversation rollback" and "reject before any mutation"); (c) changes the session identity and is not a rollback.
- Choice: (b).
  - Wire: `SessionCommand::Revert { session_id, turn_id }` (`session.revert`, mutating, `drive`, deck → station, feature `revert.v1`; ADR-004 feature, envelope and method rows); `Operation::Revert` (degradation `hide`); `Capabilities.rollback` (`#[serde(default)]`, so old stored `session.started` events decode with `false`); event `revert.updated { revert_id, turn_id, turns, state, reason }` with `RevertState` `admitted | conversation_rolled_back | succeeded | failed | reconciliation_required`; `SessionSummary.revert` (`#[serde(default)]`). The target turn and every later turn are reverted: the workspace returns to the start checkpoint of the target turn.
  - Providers: `LiveSession::rollback(turns)`, default `Unsupported`. Claude stream-json has no conversation rollback: `rollback: false`, so a Claude revert is a stored `unsupported` rejection. Codex uses the app-server `thread/rollback { threadId, numTurns }` (`crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/rollback.rs`): checks before the write are definite refusals, a JSON-RPC error answer is `ProviderError::Refused`, and every failure after the write (process exit, write error, no answer within 30 s) is `ProviderError::Uncertain`.
  - Admission (`manager/revert.rs`, in the command transaction): capability (unknown capabilities fail closed), session `running` and idle (no turn, run, open request or background task), no queued or claimed input effect, no other open revert, the target is a finalized turn of the live provider process with an `available` start checkpoint, every later turn is finalized in the same process and worktree, the latest turn has an `available` completion checkpoint, and the worktree is inside the allowed roots. Every refusal is an ordinary stored rejection receipt with no row, event, effect or file change; a repeat returns that receipt (§6.4). An accepted revert stores the `reverts` row (schema V7), `revert.updated admitted` and one `revert` effect in that transaction.
  - Fence: the unique partial index `reverts_active(session_id)` over the open steps is the durable fence; `summary.revert.blocks_input()` refuses every command except `stop` while a revert is `admitted`, `conversation_rolled_back` or `reconciliation_required`; the executor holds the session's workspace fence (the same lock as prompt dispatch, DL-084) for the whole run.
  - Executor (`manager/revert_run.rs`): recheck process, turn rows, both refs (commit equal to the admitted ones) and the workspace (its tracked tree equals the completion checkpoint of the latest turn), all before any I/O → else `failed` with no rollback; commit `rollback_sent`; rollback; refused → `failed`; uncertain → `reconciliation_required` (`rollback_uncertain`, effect `outcome_unknown`); confirmed → commit `rolled_back` (`conversation_rolled_back`), commit `restoring`, plan the restore again, restore only when the workspace still equals the completion checkpoint (a writer during the rollback → `reconciliation_required` `workspace_changed`, nothing overwritten), `succeeded` only when the restore report matches the target tree; the reverted turns leave the revertable history (`turn_checkpoints.reverted_by`); the refs stay.
  - Start recovery (in `SessionManager::start` after the checkpoint settle): `admitted` → `failed` (`station_restart`, nothing was sent); `rollback_sent` → `reconciliation_required` (never resent); `rolled_back`/`restoring` → finish the file restore from the refs (a workspace that already equals the target → `succeeded`; a workspace that equals neither checkpoint → `reconciliation_required`).
- Reason: (b) is the only option that keeps the L13 order (conversation first, files second, both verified) and gives each external step a durable record that recovery can use without repeating it.
- Executor choices (owner may change): the method and event names, `revert.v1`, reverting the target turn and all later turns, the 30 s rollback deadline, `failed` for an admitted revert interrupted by a restart, a partial restore after a crash treated as `reconciliation_required` (the station cannot tell it from a foreign writer).
- Not done yet: an owner action that clears `reconciliation_required` (today only `stop` is accepted in that state) and the board control; both stay in the 6-8 remaining work.
- Tests: `manager::revert_run::revert_tests` (11, real Git, scripted provider session): confirmed rollback restores the target start checkpoint and a second run changes nothing; refused rollback changes no file or ref; uncertain rollback → reconciliation and blocked input; a workspace change or a changed ref fails before the rollback (no rollback call); a writer during the rollback stops the restore; recovery from `admitted`, `rollback_sent` (no repeat), `rolled_back` and `restoring` (complete and partial); the active-revert index refuses a second open revert. `manager::tests::revert_wired` (5, fake-claude and fake-codex): Claude revert is a stored `unsupported` rejection with files, refs, events and effects unchanged and a repeat returning the same receipt; Codex rolls back the thread, then restores the files (`admitted → conversation_rolled_back → succeeded`, effect `confirmed`, refs kept, prompt accepted after); refused Codex rollback → `failed`, files unchanged, input accepted; provider exit during the rollback → `reconciliation_required`, effect `outcome_unknown`, input blocked; busy session → `invalid_state` with no row. `fleetdeck-deck` `http_commands::a_rejected_revert_returns_the_station_receipt_is_audited_and_a_repeat_sends_nothing` (audit rows, `drive` scope). Updated: method/envelope/ADR table tests, capability matrix (Claude lacks `revert`), event and mutation samples, TS bindings and board fixtures.
- Owner review: pending (executor decision, M3 round 10)

## DL-088: Read-only file service `files.v1` (M3 round 11)

- Context: PLAN §6.6 and M3 deliverable 1 require file browse, search and read, and diffs from the recorded checkpoints, with allowed-root validation and symlink handling. The PLAN names no wire methods, limits or search backend. v0 (`src/daemon/files.ts`, board `FileViewer.tsx`) served per-session relative paths, refused `.git` and credential directories, never opened symlinks, and searched with `git ls-files`/`git grep` or a bounded walk.
- Options: (a) one generic `files.query` method with a kind field; (b) four read-only link methods `files.list`, `files.read`, `files.search`, `files.diff` under a new feature `files.v1`, each with its own typed query and answer; (c) a data stream that sends whole trees. (a) hides four answer shapes behind one method and makes the method table less exact; (c) moves unbounded data and copies file content to the deck.
- Choice: (b).
  - Wire (`crates/fleetdeck-proto/src/files.rs`, TS `web/src/gen/files/`): roots `{"session": id}` (the session's working directory) or `{"workspace": path}`; relative `/` paths without `.`, `..`, `\\`, NUL or a leading `/`; error codes `invalid_path`, `invalid_query`, `not_found`, `wrong_kind`, `unknown_session`, `missing_workspace`, `outside_roots`, `no_diff`, `busy`, `timeout`. ADR-004 gains the feature row and four method rows (deck → station, `drive`, not mutating), with a note.
  - Station (`workspace/files.rs`, `manager/files.rs`, `link/intake.rs`): the root passes `Policy::workspace` on every call (canonical, inside the allowed roots); every component below it is checked with `lstat` and a symlink is `not_found`; files open with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, so a symlink or FIFO swapped in at the last moment is refused and a FIFO never blocks; `.git`, `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.gnupg`, `.netrc`, `.kube`, `.docker` (case-insensitive) are never listed, read or searched. Limits: 1000 listed entries (20 000 scanned), 256 KiB read with a binary sniff of the first 8000 bytes, 200 hits (5 per file, 400 characters per line), search text 2 to 256 characters, at most 4 searches at a time on a station (`busy`), deadlines 5 s (list/read), 10 s (search), 15 s (diff). A Git worktree is searched with `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` and `git grep -I -n -i -F --untracked` (ignored files are not searched; output capped at 4 MiB with the process killed); any other directory with a bounded walk that never follows a symlink and prunes `node_modules`, `target`, `dist`, `build`, `coverage`, `.next`, `.turbo`.
  - Diffs: `files.diff {session_id, turn_id}` takes the newest finalized turn row with that provider turn id, requires both sides `available`, checks the worktree root against the policy, reads both refs and requires the recorded commits, then returns the checkpoint diff summary and patch (256 KiB). A provider diff is never used; a missing or changed ref is `no_diff`.
  - Deck (`crates/fleetdeck-deck/src/http/file_routes.rs`): `GET /api/stations/{station}/files/{list,read,search}` with `session=` or `workspace=` and `GET /api/stations/{station}/sessions/{session}/turns/{turn}/diff`, `drive` scope, station codes kept (400/404/429/504, other codes `station_error` 502), a 20 s wait; the deck stores no file content.
- Reason: (b) keeps each method typed and bounded, keeps the station as the only judge of paths, and uses the checkpoint refs as the only diff source (L13).
- Executor choices (owner may change): the method names, `drive` for reads (same reason as `workspace.list`: file names and content are more sensitive than the session list), the limits and deadlines, case-insensitive literal search only (no regex), ignored files not searched in a Git worktree, the pruned directory list, the newest finalized turn for a repeated provider turn id.
- Not done in this entry: file rename (a mutating command through the effect worker), the file event feed, and the board file viewer and diff view.
- Owner review: pending. Design review: the `files.v1` contract is a new public wire contract; it is recorded here and in ADR-004 for the architect's DESIGN REVIEW and is not marked reviewed.

## DL-089: ADR-006 revision 4 after DESIGN RE-REVIEW FAIL sa-b6db7ba5 (M3 round 12)

- Context: the adversary DESIGN RE-REVIEW of ADR-006 revision 3 (sa-b6db7ba5, frozen 671fbb3) gave VERDICT: FAIL with one MAJOR. Revision-2 findings 2, 3 and 4 are fixed; finding 1 (snapshot fence) is partial. The finding: a snapshot cannot establish the initial generation-1 binding, because §6.1 accepted a higher generation only for a recorded renewal and §6.2 set the `bound_*` fields only from `coordinator.bound` events. A grant whose initial `coordinator.bound` event is covered by a forced snapshot could therefore never bind, and replay cannot repair it (the deck skips events at or below its committed cursor). The review also notes, as no finding, that the ADR-004 rows for `session.coordinator_renew`/`session.coordinator_revoke` are added with the implementation.
- Options: (a) apply both required replacement sentences and extend test 25e; (b) let the station re-emit `coordinator.bound` after a snapshot. Option (b) adds a new event path that the review did not ask for.
- Choice: (a). §6.1 snapshot synchronization: a coordinator-capable snapshot may establish generation 1 for an owner-authorized grant awaiting its initial binding, under the §5.5 initial-binding admission rules; any other increase needs the recorded, matching renewal generation; a snapshot never revives a revoked grant. §6.2 step 3: the deck sets binding fields from admitted `coordinator.bound` events or admitted coordinator-capable snapshot projections; snapshot binding fields, lifecycle state and stream cursor commit in one transaction before acknowledgment. Test 25e keeps its negative cases and adds the skipped initial generation-1 bound event, the skipped renewal bound event, and the revoked grant in a projection. §15 maps the finding.
- Reason: the replacement text is binding (architect instruction in the gate-state file); it closes the counterexample without a new event path.
- Status: "Proposed, revision 4". Not wired; the coordinator MCP wiring stays blocked until a PASS is recorded in "Design approvals". Owner review: not needed beyond DL-073 (contract text required by the review).

## DL-090: ADR-005 Accepted after DESIGN REVIEW PASS sa-34468515 (M3 round 12)

- Context: the gate-state file "Design approvals" records an adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS for ADR-005 revision 3 at 671fbb3 (sa-34468515, gpt-6.1-sol, 2026-10-12), with no findings: F1-F4 fixed, earlier fixes intact, Linux probes of the `setsid()` descendant and the persistent journal confirm the contract. The architect instruction: mark ADR-005 Accepted with that review id in its status line and here. The PASS does not certify the implementation or macOS runtime behavior.
- Choice: ADR-005 status "Accepted (revision 3; adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS sa-34468515 ...; DL-090)". The contract text is unchanged. The ShimHost implementation (7-1) may start and follows revision 3 exactly, including tests 26-50.
- Reason: the approval was recorded by the architect, not by the executor; the executor only applies the recorded result.
- Owner review: covered by DL-073 (owner pre-approval) and the architect's recorded PASS.

## DL-091: The file service walks paths with directory descriptors (M3 round 12)

- Context: the round-11 review: "Fix intermediate-directory symlink races in crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/files.rs with descriptor-relative traversal and swap-race tests; O_NOFOLLOW currently protects only the final component." The DL-088 code checked each component with `lstat` by path and then opened the full path by name; a directory swapped for a symlink between the check and the open redirected the open outside the root.
- Options: (a) descriptor-relative traversal with `openat(parent, name, O_NOFOLLOW | ...)` for every component; (b) Linux `openat2(RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS)`. Option (b) is Linux-only (macOS has no equivalent) and needs a fallback anyway.
- Choice: (a) on Linux and macOS. The root opens once (`O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW`); every component below it opens relative to its parent's descriptor (`O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW` for directories; `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK | O_NOCTTY` for files, then `fstat` must be a regular file). The last component is first checked with `statat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` in the opened parent (a symlink is `not_found`, a FIFO or directory `wrong_kind` without an open). Listings read the directory through its descriptor and `statat` each entry. The walk search reopens each queued directory from the root descriptor component by component. No path is resolved by name after the root.
- Reason: a descriptor that the service holds cannot be redirected by a later rename; `O_NOFOLLOW` on each step refuses a swapped-in symlink at any depth.
- Limit: the root path itself is opened by name once; its ancestors are the canonical policy root chosen by the station (the allowed roots), not a query path.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/files_race_tests.rs` (swap race with `RENAME_EXCHANGE` on Linux; symlink at any depth; symlinked root). They fail on the round-11 code and pass now.
- Owner review: not needed (a security fix inside DL-088; no contract change).

## DL-092: The file service opens its root from `/` without following any symlink (M3 round 13)

- Context: the round-12 review: "Fix root-ancestor symlink races in crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/files.rs::open_root with descriptor-relative root acquisition and ancestor-swap tests; O_NOFOLLOW still protects only the root's final component." DL-091 opened the canonical root by its full path with `O_NOFOLLOW`; a parent of the root swapped for a symlink after the allowed-roots check redirected the open outside the roots. The Git search also ran `git` with the root path as its working directory, so the same swap moved Git into another repository.
- Options: (a) open `/` and then each root component with `openat(parent, name, O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW)`; (b) Linux `openat2(RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS)` (Linux-only); (c) remember a `(dev, inode)` identity of each allowed root at station start and compare it (refuses a legitimately moved root until restart, and does not cover the Git child).
- Choice: (a). `files::open_root` refuses a relative root or a root with `.`/`..` components (`EINVAL`) and walks the canonical root component by component from `/` with `O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC`. For the Git search the station opens the root this way and gives `git` the working directory `/proc/self/fd/<n>` of that descriptor (`files::pinned_dir`), so the child changes into the opened directory itself, never into the root by name; the descriptor stays open until `git` has finished. Where no such path exists (macOS) `pinned_dir` is `None` and the search uses the descriptor walk (`backend: walk`).
