- Reason: (a) closes the race on Linux and macOS with the same code and keeps DL-091 unchanged below the root. The served directory is the one at the allowed path at open time, reached without any symlink; a rename of real directories that puts other content at the allowed path serves that content, because it is then inside the allowed path (the policy is path-based). The diff path (`files.diff`) needs no change: it reads only checkpoint refs and compares both commits with the stored row, so a swapped repository cannot produce a different patch.
- Platform limit: on macOS a Git worktree is searched with the walk (ignored files that are not pruned can appear). This is owner/platform validation, not a pass on this Linux machine.
- Owner review: not required (security fix inside the reviewed `files.v1` contract); the architect's review of the round decides.

## DL-093: The revert fence covers every session of the worktree; one revertable history; the completion commit is checked again after the rollback (M3 round 14)

- Context: adversary DESIGN+CODE REVIEW sa-3491e52e of `session.revert` (DL-087, commit 2cf9c5b): FAIL with one BLOCKER and two MAJOR findings (`.pi/review-revert-sa-3491e52e.md`). (1) BLOCKER: the fence was per session (each `SessionTurns::open` made its own mutex; the active-revert index is keyed by session only), so a revert of session A could run while session B ran a native turn in the same worktree (conflicts with PLAN §6.6: an active workspace writer prevents a revert). (2) MAJOR: admission used the non-reverted history (`reverted_by IS NULL`), the executor recheck used all rows, so a second revert after a successful one failed with `turns_changed`. (3) MAJOR: after the rollback, the restore reread the completion checkpoint without comparing its commit with the admitted one; a writer that changed the workspace and repointed the completion ref made the changed workspace look expected, and the restore overwrote it.
- Options for (1): (a) a durable check over every live session whose directory overlaps the worktree, in the command transaction, plus one in-process fence per canonical worktree root shared by all its sessions; (b) a new persisted `worktree_owners` lease table; (c) refuse a second session in a worktree. Option (b) duplicates state that the sessions, effects, turn rows and reverts already hold durably; option (c) removes parity (several sessions per repository).
- Choice: (a).
  - Durable admission (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/sharing.rs`): sessions overlap when one directory is inside the other (whole path components; the cwd is canonical at start, the revert root is the Git worktree root). A revert is refused (`invalid_state`, stored receipt) while another live session of the worktree is starting or stopping, has a turn or a run, an open request, background work, a queued or claimed input effect, or an open turn checkpoint row (a natively queued input, a turn before `turn.finalized`, a completion capture), and while another revert is open in the worktree.
  - While a revert is open (also `reconciliation_required`), every other session of the worktree is refused prompt, steer, follow-up, answer, input edit and revert, and a new session cannot start there; stop stays allowed.
  - The executor checks the same conflicts again under the fence before any provider or file I/O (`worktree_busy`).
  - `manager::turns::Fences` maps a canonical worktree root to one shared tokio mutex; every `SessionTurns` of that worktree uses it for input writes, checkpoint captures and the revert executor. A directory outside Git keeps its own fence.
- Choice for (2): the executor recheck reads `Store::revertable_turn_checkpoints` (the rows without `reverted_by`), the same history as the admission.
- Choice for (3): `restore_files` compares the reread completion commit with the admitted `current_commit` before planning or writing, in the live path and in start recovery; a mismatch keeps every file and ends in `reconciliation_required` (`checkpoint_changed`).
- Reason: the checks read only durable state in the serialized command transaction, so two commands cannot both pass; the shared fence closes the window between an admitted input and its write; the finding texts are binding.
- Tests: `manager::tests::revert_wired::worktree` (4, fake Claude and fake Codex: refusal while B runs a turn and while its queued input waits, then success; refusal while B has background work; B refused input and a new start refused while A's revert needs reconciliation; revert T2 then T1); `manager::revert_run::revert_tests` +3 (repointed completion ref during the rollback, the same in recovery, second revert after a successful one); `manager::turns::turns_tests::sessions_in_one_worktree_share_one_fence`.
- Owner review: not required (fixes inside the DL-087 contract; no wire change). 6-8 stays unchecked until the architect records a revert re-review PASS.

## DL-094: The private checkpoint index keeps the modification time of the real index (M3 round 15)

- Context: the round-14 `make check` failed once in `workspace::checkpoint_tests::the_diff_between_start_and_completion_comes_from_the_checkpoints`: the completion checkpoint missed a same-size edit (`one` → `two` in `a.txt`). A stress run reproduced it about once in 300 runs. Cause: Git compares file times in whole seconds (it is built without `USE_NSEC`), and its racy-entry protection rechecks the content of an entry only when the entry is not older than the index file. `PrivateIndex::copy_of` copied the index with a new modification time. When the copy was made after a second boundary, an entry that changed in the second of its last stat, without a size change, looked clean to `git add -u`, and the snapshot kept the old content. Such a checkpoint is wrong data: a diff misses an edit, and a revert compares against a wrong completion tree.
- Options: (a) give the copy the modification time of the real index; (b) give the copy a very old time, so Git rechecks the content of every tracked file at each capture; (c) build the snapshot from an empty index (hash every tracked file).
- Choice: (a). The time is read before the copy, so a concurrent rewrite of the real index can only make the copy older (more rechecks), never newer.
- Reason: with (a) the private index has the same protection as `git add -u` on the real index, which is the Git guarantee that `git status` and `git commit` use. (b) and (c) hash the full tracked content on each start and completion capture; the start capture holds the input write, so that cost adds latency to each prompt.
- Tests: `workspace::checkpoint_tests::a_same_size_edit_in_the_second_of_the_index_entry_is_in_the_checkpoint` (deterministic: the entry, the real index and the edit are in one second, `core.trustctime=false`; it failed on 90623d2 and passes now); a stress run of both tests (1000 iterations each) passed.
- Owner review: not required (a correctness fix inside the DL-072 snapshot contract).

## DL-095: Session rename and the board event feed (M3 round 16)

- Context: PLAN §16 row "Workspace tools (files, worktrees, rename, event feed) | Keep, per station | 6". In v0, "rename" is the board title of a session card (`POST /api/sessions/:id/name`, `RenameDialog.tsx`), and the "event feed" is the fleet ticker at the bottom of the board (`Feed.tsx`: filter chips, newest first, `hh:mm:ss`, "quiet so far"). v2 had no way to change `SessionSummary.title` after the start, and no feed.
- Options (rename): (a) a deck-only label in the read model; (b) a station command `session.rename` that appends a station event and folds into the summary; (c) a provider operation (the native session title). Options (feed): (d) a new deck event stream of fleet events; (e) a board feed derived from the summary deltas that the board already receives.
- Choice: (b) and (e).
  - Wire: `SessionCommand::Rename { session_id, title: Option<String> }`, method `session.rename`, deck → station, scope `drive`, mutating (`CommandEnvelope`, station receipt), new link feature `rename.v1` (an old station never gets the method). ADR-004 gains the feature row and the method row; the method/envelope agreement tests cover it.
  - Station: `proto::command::normalize_title` trims the title; an empty title clears it (`None`); more than `MAX_TITLE_CHARS` (80) characters or a control character is a stored `invalid_command` rejection. The decision appends `session.renamed { title }` only when the title changes; it creates no effect and needs no live process, so a rename works on an ended session and while a revert holds the session. An unknown session is `unknown_session`. Old readers skip the new event kind (`EventBody::Unknown`).
  - Board: `RenameForm` under the session heading ("Rename", "Save", "Clear the title", an alert above 80 characters), enabled with `drive` on a live board, also for ended sessions. `board/feed.ts` turns each summary change into feed items (join, new ask, new running tool, turn ended, revert state, new error code, end or lost, rename); `RuntimeState.feed` keeps the newest 200 items since the page opened (the first snapshot adds none; a snapshot after a reconnect adds what changed); `FeedPanel` shows the newest 50 with filter chips and opens the session.
- Reason: (a) would make the title differ between the deck and the station and would be lost on a deck rebuild of the read model; the station owns session state (G1). (c) is not supported by both providers and is not needed for the board label. (d) needs a new stored stream and retention; (e) reuses the summary deltas that already reach the board, shows the same facts, and adds no wire contract. The feed shows changes since the page opened, as v0 did.
- Tests: `manager::tests::rename` (3: set/unchanged/clear, refused titles change nothing and a repeat returns the same receipt, an ended session can be renamed); `fold_tests::a_rename_sets_and_clears_the_title_and_an_old_reader_skips_it`; deck `http_commands::a_rename_reaches_the_station_needs_drive_and_is_audited`; web `board/feed.test.ts` (6), `session/session.test.tsx` (+2); Playwright `e2e/workspace.spec.ts` (2: rename with refusal, save and clear at a phone width; the feed newest first, the Asks filter, open a session).
- Owner review: not required (v0 parity with a station-owned title); `rename.v1` is a new link method and needs the architect's review like the other new contracts.

## DL-096: ADR-006 coordinator MCP Accepted (revision 4)

- Context: the architect recorded adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS sa-8e3c8665 for ADR-006 revision 4 at 92ede4a (DL-089) in "Design approvals" of the gate-state file, with the instruction to mark ADR-006 *Accepted* with that review id and a DECISIONS-LOG entry. The PASS approves the design only; the real-provider credential handoff smoke (ADR-006 §8.5) still gates enabling the capability.
- Choice: ADR-006 status "Accepted (revision 4; adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS sa-8e3c8665, 2026-10-12, at 92ede4a; DL-096)"; the deciders line records the PASS. PROGRESS: the ADR approval boundary and 6-ADR6 are checked; 6-5 is unblocked and follows revision 4 exactly, including the §8 tests.
- Reason: the goal rule (an adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS before implementation, DL-074) is now met for ADR-006.
- Owner review: covered by the owner pre-approval DL-073.

## DL-097: Owner resolution of a revert in `reconciliation_required`; board file viewer and turn diff/revert controls (M3 round 17)

- Context: PLAN §6.6 blocks session input while a revert is `reconciliation_required` ("uncertain or partial rollback blocks session input with an explicit reconciliation-required state; recovery uses evidence, never repeats a claimed rollback blindly"). Before this round, nothing could leave that state: the session stayed blocked for ever, and the worktree fence (DL-093) also blocked every other session of the worktree. The board had no file viewer and no diff or revert controls, although the station served `files.v1` (DL-088) and `session.revert` (DL-087).
- Options (resolution): (a) a station-local repair command only (CLI); (b) a board command with two owner resolutions: retry the file restore, or accept the current state; (c) an automatic timeout that unblocks the session.
- Choice: (b).
  - Wire: `SessionCommand::ResolveRevert { session_id, revert_id, resolution: RevertResolution }`, method `session.resolve_revert`, deck → station, scope `drive`, mutating (`CommandEnvelope`, station receipt), feature `revert.v1` (one more row in ADR-004 §9.6 and in the link and deck tables). `RevertResolution::{Reconciled, RetryRestore}`. New `RevertState::Reconciled` (older readers decode the unknown enum value as an unknown event and skip it, as for other additions).
  - `reconciled`: the owner accepts the current conversation and files. Allowed only from `reconciliation_required`, also on an ended session; no provider effect and no file change. Every not yet reverted turn of the session becomes not revertable (`reverted_by` = the revert id), because the station cannot prove which checkpoint matches the conversation now. Input and the worktree fence open again.
  - `retry_restore`: allowed only when the conversation rollback was confirmed (schema V8 `reverts.rolled_back_at`, set when the step reaches `rolled_back`; V8 back-fills it for older rows only where the evidence shows a confirmed rollback), the same provider process is live, the session and the worktree are quiet (the DL-093 checks) and the worktree is inside the allowed roots. The decision moves the row back to `rolled_back` (event `conversation_rolled_back`) and enqueues one new revert effect. The executor never calls the provider again: it rechecks the process and the worktree, then runs the normal restore (completion commit unchanged, files equal to the completion or the start checkpoint, else `reconciliation_required` with the files kept). A rollback that is not confirmed (`rollback_uncertain`) can only be reconciled.
  - Board: `web/src/session/TurnsPanel.tsx` shows the finalized turns from the timeline (diff summary, "Show the diff" from `files.diff`, "Revert to here" with a confirm step; disabled with the reason as title when the provider cannot roll back, the turn is reverted, the start checkpoint is missing, a revert holds the session or a turn runs; a Claude session shows "This provider cannot roll back its conversation, so the board offers no revert. A file-only restore is not a revert.") and the revert state with the two owner actions. `web/src/session/FilesPanel.tsx` browses, reads and searches the session files through `files.v1` (symlinks and other kinds are listed but never opened, the station's refusal text is shown). Both need `drive` on a live board.
- Reason: (a) leaves the board user without a way out of a blocking state, and the board is the remote (PLAN §16). (c) would unblock input while the conversation and the files can differ, which §6.6 forbids. A retry is safe only after a confirmed rollback, because a second rollback call could remove a turn twice; a reconciliation must end the revertable history because the turn checkpoints may no longer match the conversation.
- Tests: `manager::tests::revert_wired::resolve` (3, fake Codex: a retry with changed files stays `reconciliation_required` and changes nothing, the owner restores the turn-end content, the next retry succeeds; `reconciled` unblocks input and leaves no revertable turn; an unconfirmed rollback cannot be retried but can be reconciled after the session ended; wrong revert id, unknown session and a non-reconciliation state are refused); `manager::revert_run::revert_tests` +1 (a retried restore from another process or with changed files writes nothing; rollback called once); web `session/workspace.test.tsx` (8); Playwright `e2e/revert.spec.ts` (2: file browse/read/search with a symlink not opened; diff, confirmed revert, owner reconciliation at a phone width).
- Owner review: not required for the board controls (PLAN §16 parity). `session.resolve_revert` extends the `revert.v1` contract and needs the architect's review together with the revert re-review; 6-8 stays unchecked until then.

## DL-098 — files.v1 review fixes: descriptor reads for Git search, private roots, private-path-free diffs, bounded diff output, slots held by workers (M3 round 18)

- Context: adversary DESIGN+CODE REVIEW sa-13931f53 of files.v1 at 245f27e, VERDICT FAIL: MAJOR 1 the Git content search (`git grep`) followed a child directory exchanged with a symlink to a directory outside the allowed roots; MAJOR 2 a private directory (`.ssh`, as a workspace root or as a session cwd) served as the root, so its files were readable; MAJOR 3 checkpoint diffs returned tracked private-directory content; MINOR 4 the diff budget applied after the whole patch was in memory; MINOR 5 a timeout released the search slot while the blocking work still ran.
- Options for MAJOR 1: (a) keep `git grep` and filter its paths; (b) use the descriptor walk for every content search; (c) let Git list the candidates and read their content through the root descriptor. (a) cannot repair content that Git already read; (b) loses the Git ignore rules that the board shows for Git worktrees.
- Choice: (c) `git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard` (bounded by `SEARCH_OUTPUT_MAX`) names candidates; `workspace::files::grep_paths` opens each one from the root descriptor, one component at a time with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and skips a candidate that is not a regular file then. Name search filters the same list. MAJOR 2: `workspace::files::private_root(root, allowed)` refuses a canonical root when a segment below the longest allowed root that holds it is a hidden name (case-insensitive; a non-UTF-8 or non-normal segment and a root that no allowed root holds also count as private); `manager::files::root` and `diff` apply it to both root forms with `outside_roots`. MAJOR 3: `restore::visible_pathspec()` excludes every hidden name at any depth (`:(exclude,icase,glob)**/<name>` and `**/<name>/**`) and diffs run with `--no-renames`, so a rename into or out of a private directory shows only its public side; the same pathspec applies to `diff_summary`, so the `turn.finalized` summary also counts public paths only. MINOR 4: `Git::bounded(args, max)` reads at most `max` bytes, then kills `git`; the patch ends with a `[diff truncated]` marker. MINOR 5: the slot moves into the `spawn_blocking` closure and is released when the worker ends; lists and reads have their own station-wide slot limit (`MAX_READS` 16). File-system calls cannot be cancelled, so a timed-out worker can still run until its own bounds stop it; the slot limit bounds how many such workers can pile up (documented at `manager::files::blocking`).
- Reason: Git may decide which paths are candidates, but only descriptor traversal can prove that the content came from inside the root. The private-directory wall must hold for the root itself, not only for paths below it. A diff is a read of file content and follows the same wall.
- Tests: `manager::files::files_review_tests` (6, see PROGRESS round 18), each mutation-proved (not committed). Also the revert re-review sa-ba907845: `revert_tests::repoint` writes a valid replacement checkpoint and three tests (live, `rolled_back`, `restoring`) fail when the completion-commit comparison is removed.
- Owner review: not required (security fixes inside the reviewed contract; no wire change). files.v1 stays unreviewed until the architect records a re-review PASS.


## DL-099 — ShimHost slice 1: the shim core without a process or a socket (M3 round 19)

- Context: ADR-005 revision 3 is Accepted (adversary DESIGN REVIEW PASS sa-34468515, DL-090), so 7-1 may start. The ADR is large: shim process, socket, launch journal, `ShimHost`, adapter checkpoints and recovery. The ADR requires the §9 tests and keeps the host setting at `direct` until the real-process tests pass.
- Options: (a) build the whole shim with real processes in one round; (b) first build the pure core (frames, attach proof, output buffer, write table, effect settlement, process identity) with its unit tests, then the process, socket and journal on top of it; (c) start with the station `shims` table.
- Choice: (b). New module `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/host/shim/` with `wire` (frame = u32 total length, u32 header length, JSON header, raw payload; unknown frames and fields refused; size checked before any allocation; protocol versions N and N−1 negotiated by `pick_protocol`), `proof` (`AttachKey` in `Secret<[u8; 32]>`, HMAC-SHA256 through `ring` over length-prefixed nonce, incarnation and generation; proof checked first, then the incarnation, then a generation above the current one; peer UID check), `output` (absolute offsets, bounded buffer 16 MiB default within 1–256 MiB, ack and replay with an explicit `below_floor` gap, detection of a line as long as the buffer), `writes` (one active write, 256 pending writes or 4 MiB, 4096 terminal records with `evicted_through`, `not_received` tombstones, takeover marks waiting old-generation writes `failed_unwritten {superseded}`, `write_tracked` reports `partial` after the first byte), `settle` (the §4.3 answer table; `RETIRE_ROWS_ENABLED = false` until real-process tests 26–35 exist, so every answer stays `outcome_unknown` in this build) and `identity` (Linux `/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22 after the last `)` and the boot id; other systems return `Unsupported`).
- Reason: these parts decide every safety rule of §3–§4 and can be tested exactly without timing. The process and socket layers then only move frames. The retirement gate stays closed, so this slice cannot retire an effect on evidence that no real-process test has proved.
- Not done in this slice: the `fleetdeck shim` subcommand, socket placement and permissions (§5.1), the launch journal and lock, `ShimHost`/`DurableProcess`, the station `shims` table, adapter checkpoints (§2.1), recovery (§3.2) and the real-process tests in `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_*.rs`. The host setting stays `direct`. macOS identity is owner/platform validation.
- Tests: `host::shim::tests` (24) in `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/host/shim_tests.rs`; they name the ADR test numbers they cover in part (5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 30–33, 35, 38, 41, 42). These are core parts of those tests, not the real-process tests that the ADR requires.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005).

## DL-100 — ShimHost slice 2: the `fleetdeck shim` process, its socket, launch journal and exit file (M3 round 20)

- Context: ADR-005 revision 3 is Accepted (PASS sa-34468515). Slice 1 (DL-099) built the shim core without a process. Reviewer feedback for round 20: implement slice 2 and its real-process tests, and keep the retirement of `retired_not_dispatched` rows disabled until tests 26–35 pass.
- Options: (a) the shim process and the station `ShimHost` in one slice; (b) the shim process with a test client that plays the station, then `ShimHost`, the `shims` table and recovery in slice 3; (c) the station side first against an in-process fake shim.
- Choice: (b). New modules in `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/host/shim/`:
  - `journal` (launch journal records `created`/`spawn_attempted`/`fenced` as fsynced JSON lines, the `flock` launch lock, the atomic 0600 key and exit files, the 0700 directory owner and mode check, and the §5.1 socket path choice with the `sun_path` limit);
  - `launch` (the launch spec and the one-line launch reply);
  - `server`, `server_conn`, `server_proc` (the shim process: `setsid`, Linux child subreaper, the lock, the fenced check, bind with mode 0600, `spawn_attempted` before the spawn, the provider through `fleetdeck shim-exec` in its own session with `PDEATHSIG(SIGKILL)` on a current-thread runtime, stdout buffer with backpressure and the overlong-line gap, stderr tail, one stdin writer, takeover by generation, control frames independent of stdin, the exit report with `descendants_remaining`, `ack_exit` clean-up and the delayed exit file);
  - `client` (the station side of the handshake and frames, used by the tests now and by `ShimHost` in slice 3).
  - The CLI gets two hidden subcommands, `shim --id` and `shim-exec --parent <pid> --`. They run before the normal start-up, so they need no station directories and no environment.
- Reason: the workspace forbids `unsafe`, so no `pre_exec` hook can call `setsid` and set the parent-death signal. A small exec helper does both with safe `rustix` calls, checks that its parent is the shim, and then replaces itself with the provider. A parent-death signal fires when the parent thread ends, so the shim uses a single-thread runtime. With (b), every shim rule is tested against real processes before the station depends on it.
- Test names: a test that covers a whole ADR-005 §9 statement at the shim has the exact §9 name. A test that covers only the shim half of a statement (the station half needs `ShimHost`, the `shims` table and recovery) has the suffix `_at_the_shim`. These tests do not count as the full ADR test yet.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_process.rs` (15, Linux, `/bin/sh` fake providers; rig in `tests/shim_rig/mod.rs`): ADR tests 2, 7, 8, 11, 13, 14, 17, 18, 33, 38 (`_at_the_shim`), 41, 43 (`_at_the_shim`), 49 (`_at_the_shim`), 50, and one extra test of the provider session, group and cleared environment. Test 38 first failed: a complete line in the buffer kept the overlong check from firing until the station acknowledged that line, as ADR §4.2 intends; the test now acknowledges it first. A gap of a discarded line is also reported when the attach comes after the gap.
- Not done: `ShimHost`/`DurableProcess`, the station `shims` table, adapter checkpoints, recovery steps 1–5, ADR tests 1, 3–6, 9, 10, 12, 15, 16, 19–32, 34–37, 39, 40, 42, 44–48, and the station halves of 38, 43 and 49. `RETIRE_ROWS_ENABLED` stays `false`; the host setting stays `direct`. macOS runs are owner/platform validation.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005).

## DL-101 — A reconciliation keeps the worktree fence while a live rollback has no answer (M3 round 21)

- Context: review sa-56b1636a (rename/feed at ec43ea9 and resolve_revert/board at b9412f4): VERDICT FAIL, one MAJOR. `session.resolve_revert` with `reconciled` released the session and worktree fence while the provider process was alive and its `thread/rollback` had no answer. The Codex adapter kept the request open after its deadline (`RollbackState::Sent`), and its core accepted a new prompt. A late rollback answer could then change the conversation after new input, or after the fence was free for other sessions. The review found the rename, the event feed and the board controls correct.
- Options: (a) refuse `reconciled` while the same live process has an unconfirmed rollback, until the owner stops the session; (b) send an interrupt or a second request to cancel the rollback, then reconcile; (c) keep the current behaviour and only document it.
- Choice: (a), with defence in the provider:
  - The station refuses `reconciled` with `invalid_state` ("stop the session, then reconcile") while the session is not ended, its live process is the revert's process, and the rollback was not confirmed (`rolled_back_at` is empty).
  - A reconciliation of a live session also needs a quiet session (no turn, run, open request or background work) and a quiet worktree (the same check as revert admission, DL-093).
  - The Codex core refuses `prompt`, `follow_up` and `steer` with `Refused` while a rollback has no answer. A late confirmation or refusal opens the thread again.
  - The rollback deadline is an adapter setting (`rollback_timeout`, default 30 s), so the station tests can use 500 ms.
  - A reconciliation after the session ended is unchanged.
- Reason: Codex app-server has no cancel for `thread/rollback`, so (b) gives no definitive evidence. A stopped provider process is definitive evidence that no late answer can change the conversation. (c) leaves the MAJOR open.
- Tests: `codex::tests_rollback` (2: a prompt and a follow-up are refused while a rollback has no answer; a late confirmation or refusal opens the thread again); `manager::tests::revert_wired::resolve::a_reconciliation_while_the_rollback_has_no_answer_keeps_the_fence_until_the_session_stops` (the fake Codex never answers; `reconciled` is refused; a prompt and a new start in the worktree are refused; after a stop the reconciliation is accepted, the files are unchanged and a new start works). The earlier test `an_unconfirmed_rollback_cannot_be_retried_but_can_be_reconciled_after_the_session_ended` still passes.
- Owner review: not required (fix inside the reviewed revert contract; the architect's re-review is still needed before 6-8 is checked).

## DL-102 — ShimHost slice 3: the station-side host layer (M3 round 22)

- Context: slices 1–2 (DL-099, DL-100) built the shim core and the `fleetdeck shim` process, tested with a client that plays the station. Reviewer feedback for round 22: continue the station integration with the ADR-005 crash-boundary tests; keep ShimHost and the not-dispatched retirement off until the tests pass.
- Options: (a) the station `shims` table, adapter checkpoints and recovery in one step; (b) first the host layer (`ShimHost` as a `SessionHost`, `ShimProcess` as a `ProcessControl`, explicit reattach with a stored record and a manual acknowledgment), tested with real processes and a real adapter; then the `shims` table, the adapter checkpoint and recovery; (c) wire `ShimHost` into the station now with acknowledgment on delivery.
- Choice: (b). New `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/host/shim/host.rs`:
  - `ShimHostConfig` (binary, station directory, runtime and temporary directories, incarnation, buffer size, step timeout) and `ShimRecord` (the future `shims` row: shim id, socket path, provider identity, generation, cursor, next write sequence).
  - `ShimHost::launch` (journal, key, socket path choice, launch, attach with generation 1) and `ShimHost::reattach` (the next generation, the identity check of the record, replay from the record cursor; a refusal or an identity mismatch is `HostError::Shim`).
  - `AckMode::Manual`: the caller acknowledges a cursor after it committed the events below it (ADR-005 §4.1). `AckMode::OnDelivery`: the `SessionHost::spawn` path acknowledges each complete line once the adapter has it; this is not crash-exact.
  - `ShimProcess` implements `ProcessControl` over frames. Control frames (stop, kill, close, acknowledgment) never wait behind stdout backpressure, because one task reads frames and another feeds the adapter pipe. Dropping every handle never stops the provider.
  - Shim fix: an escalated stop now reports `stop_forced`. Before, the `stopped` cause of the stop frame stayed after the shim escalated to a kill.
- Reason: the adapter and the station see the shim only through `SessionHost`/`ProcessControl`, so this layer must be correct before the station depends on it. (c) loses output that the adapter read but the station did not commit, which ADR-005 §4.1 forbids.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_host.rs` (10, Linux): the host write/read/end path; dropping handles and a reattach (`_at_the_host`); replay from a manually acknowledged cursor after uncommitted lines, exactly once (`_at_the_host`); an exit while detached reported after a reattach, then removed by `ack_exit` (`_at_the_host`); a stale attachment cannot write or stop, and an old generation is refused; an identity mismatch is refused; a blocked stdin write ends with `StdinClosed` after a kill; stop escalation for a provider that ignores stdin and TERM; an overlong line reaches the host as a gap; a full fake-Claude turn through `ClaudeAdapter` with `ShimHost`.
- Not done: the station `shims` table, the adapter checkpoint and transitions (§2.1), recovery steps 1–5, the station halves of the `_at_the_shim`/`_at_the_host` tests, ADR tests 26–35 and the others not yet named in DL-100. The station host setting stays `direct` and `RETIRE_ROWS_ENABLED` stays `false`.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005).

## DL-103 — files.v1: a private allowed root never serves; a large file is searched in its prefix and marks the search truncated (M3 round 23)

- Context: CODE RE-REVIEW sa-fbed2a6d of 73714ae (DL-098): VERDICT FAIL, 2 MAJOR, both reproduced by failing tests. The review closed the revert completion-ref test item and files findings M1, M3, m4 and m5 of sa-13931f53. (1) `private_root` checked only the segments below the allowed root that held the requested root, so an allowed root configured as `<tmp>/.SSH` served its secret through both root forms. (2) The descriptor-based content search (DL-098) skipped every file above 1 MiB without setting `truncated`, so a legitimate match in a large tracked file disappeared silently (a regression from b9412f4).
- Options for (1): (a) check every segment of the whole resolved root path; (b) check the allowed root's final segment and the segments below it; (c) refuse private names in the policy when the station loads its allowed roots. For (2): (a) search a bounded prefix and set `truncated` whenever a size or read budget stopped a complete search; (b) raise the size limit; (c) give large files back to `git grep`.
- Choice: (1)(a): a root is private when any segment of the whole canonical path is a hidden name (case-insensitive), above or below the allowed root, and a root that no allowed root holds stays private. (2)(a): `grep_file` reads the first 1 MiB through the descriptor, searches its complete lines, and sets `truncated` when the file is larger or a read fails; binary files are still skipped (as `git grep -I`). The Git and walk backends share the function.
- Reason: (1)(a) is the simplest rule that no allowed-root entry can mask; it also refuses an allowed root inside a private directory. (1)(c) alone would miss a session cwd inside such a root. (2)(b) only moves the silent limit, and (2)(c) brings back the symlink race that DL-098 removed.
- Tests: `manager::files::files_review2_tests` (3): a configured private allowed root (`.SSH`, `.ssh/work`, `p/.Git`) is refused through the workspace and session forms and by `list`; a Git and a walk content search find a match on line 1 of a file above the limit with `truncated = true`, report `truncated = true` for a match only past the limit, and are complete again without large files. `a_private_root_check_uses_the_segments_below_the_allowed_root` gets the private-allowed-root cases. Mutation proofs (each restored): the old segment-below-the-holder check fails 2 tests; skipping large files without `truncated` fails both search tests.
- Owner review: not required (fix inside the files.v1 contract; the architect's re-review is still needed before 6-8 is checked).

## DL-104 — ShimHost slice 4: station `shims` table, generation before attach, launch and recovery classification (M3 round 24)

- Context: ADR-005 revision 3 is Accepted (PASS sa-34468515). Slices 1–3 (DL-099, DL-100, DL-102) built the shim and the host layer, but the station had no durable shim record: no generation committed before an attach, no committed output cursor, and no recovery of `starting` and `running` rows (ADR-005 §3, §3.2, §4.1). Reviewer feedback for round 19 asked for station recovery with generation, checkpoint and crash-boundary tests.
- Options: (a) one slice with the `shims` table, the launch path and the §3.2 classifier, tested with real shims; the adapter checkpoint (§2.1) and the session-level wiring (lost sessions, effect settlement, the host setting) in the next slice; (b) all of it in one slice, including the adapter checkpoint of both providers; (c) wire `ShimHost` into the session manager first and add recovery later.
- Choice: (a).
  - Schema V9: table `shims` (`shim_id` PK, `session_id`, `incarnation`, `state` in `starting`/`running`/`exited`/`never_started`/`lost`, `owner_generation`, `socket_path`, `provider_identity` JSON, `cursor`, `next_write_seq`, `reason`; a `running` row must have an identity).
  - `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/shims.rs`: `shim_starting` (the row before the shim starts), `shim_running` (the first identity, once; a different identity is refused), `shim_next_generation` (committed before every attach; only open rows), `shim_commit_output` (events and cursor in one transaction, only for the current generation, never backwards), `shim_claim_write_seq`, `shim_end`, `shims_to_recover` (this incarnation, `starting` rows first).
  - `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/host/shim/start.rs`: `ShimHost::prepare` (journal `created`, key file, socket path; no process) and `ShimHost::start`, so the station can commit the `starting` row between them. `host.rs` gains `attach(record, generation, mode, first_identity)` with a typed `AttachFailure` (`attach.rs`).
  - `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/shims.rs`: `launch_shim` (prepare → `starting` row → start → identity → generation → attach; any failure after the row is settled by the recovery classifier, never left open) and `recover_shim`/`recover_shims` (§3.2: a free launch lock is fenced and only an intact `created`-only journal gives `never_started`; a held lock waits up to 30 s for the socket and commits the first identity; a running row attaches with a new generation; an unreachable shim gives `exited` with a valid exit file, else `lost` `execution_unknown`, and the stale socket is removed only after that check; a refused proof gives `attach_refused` and leaves the shim alone; an incompatible shim gives `shim_incompatible`).
  - Shim fix found by the tests: a `kill` or `interrupt` sent before the exec helper called `setsid()` found no process group and was dropped, so the provider ran until its natural exit. `signal_group` now signals the child itself when its group does not exist yet.
  - Test hygiene: a shim outlives its station handles by design, so the shim test rigs kill the shims of their own station directory on drop (`crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_reaper/mod.rs`). Earlier rounds had left 73 test shims running; they were killed.
- Reason: (a) puts every crash boundary of the launch and the attach under real-process tests before any session uses the shim. (b) is too large for one reviewed slice. (c) would run sessions on a host whose crash recovery is not proven, which ADR-005 forbids (the host setting stays `direct` until the §9 tests pass).
- Tests: `store::shims::tests` (6); `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_recovery.rs` (9, Linux, real shims): identity and generation 1 commit at launch; a restart reattaches with generation 2 and replays from the committed cursor exactly once, and generation 1 commits nothing more; a crash after the generation commit skips a generation and the old attachment and an old-generation attach are refused; a killed shim is `exited` with its exit file or `lost` (`execution_unknown`) without one; a wrong key is `attach_refused` with the provider alive; only an intact `created`-only journal gives `never_started`, and the late shim of that launch starts no provider; `spawn_attempted`, missing, truncated and foreign journals give `lost`; a live `starting` shim is attached and its first identity commits; a shim that cannot start is settled as `never_started`. `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_host.rs` gains `a_kill_right_after_the_launch_ends_the_provider` (10 launches; it fails without the `signal_group` fix).
- Not done in this slice: the §2.1 adapter checkpoint and replayable transitions, the session-level effects of recovery (lost sessions, write settlement with the retirement gate, request reconciliation), the station halves of ADR tests 38, 43, 49, ADR tests 26–35, and the host setting. The host setting stays `direct`; the retirement gate stays closed.
- Owner review: not required (implementation of the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-105 — ShimHost slice 5: durable stdin frame identities, §4.3 settlement after a reattach, input block, retirement gate opened (M3 round 25)

- Context: ADR-005 revision 3 is Accepted (PASS sa-34468515). Slice 4 (DL-104) committed the generation and the output cursor, but the shim host assigned `write_seq` in memory, and recovery did not ask the shim about claimed stdin writes. ADR-005 §4.3 requires the frame identity to be claimed in a station transaction before the frame leaves, the `write_status` table to settle every claimed stdin write before the generic M2 recovery, and the two `retired_not_dispatched` rows to stay gated until the crash-boundary tests 26–35 pass. Reviewer feedback for round 20 asked for saved adapter state, stdin settlement and request recovery with crash-boundary tests.
- Options: (a) one durable row per frame (`shim_writes`), claimed in its own transaction before the frame is sent, settled per frame and combined per effect; (b) store only the effect's first `write_seq` on the effect row and derive the other frames; (c) keep in-memory sequence numbers and settle every claimed effect as `outcome_unknown`.
- Choice: (a).
  - Schema V10: table `shim_writes` (`shim_id`, `write_seq` PK pair, unique `write_id`, nullable `effect_id`, `ordinal`, `generation`) and `shims.input_blocked`.
  - `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/shim_writes.rs`: `shim_claim_write` (current generation only, `claimed` effect only, `next_write_seq` rises in the same transaction; `write_id` = `<effect_id>:<ordinal>`, or `internal:<shim_id>:<seq>` for adapter frames), `shim_writes_to_settle`, `shim_settle_write` (`Retired` → `retired_not_dispatched`; `Unknown` → `outcome_unknown` `shim_write_unknown`, or `stdin_partial_write` with `input_blocked`), `shim_input_blocked`.
  - `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/host/shim/stdin.rs`: `WriteTag`, `ShimProcess::write_tagged` (frame above 1 MiB refused before I/O) and `write_status`. `client.rs` gains the fault tool `Attachment::send_torn`.
  - `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/shim_stdin.rs`: `send_input` (claims every frame before it leaves; a stale generation sends nothing), `settle_live`, and `settle_writes` (asks `write_status` per frame, asks again for `received`/`writing` up to the in-flight wait, combines per effect: retired only when every frame is proven unwritten; a partial frame blocks input). `recover_shim` runs it right after a reattach, before the generic recovery.
  - The decision transaction refuses every command except interrupt and stop while `input_blocked` is set (`manager/decide.rs` `live_session`).
  - `RETIRE_ROWS_ENABLED` is now true, together with the real-process tests 26–35.
  - Bug found by the tests: `Shared::finish` dropped the write waiters before it marked the attachment ended, so a write sent on an attachment that a takeover had already ended waited forever. `finish` now marks the end first, and every waiter checks `ended()` after it registered.
- Reason: (a) follows §4.3 word for word (identity claimed before I/O, one answer per frame), and recovery never needs to guess the identities of later frames. (b) breaks when a frame is claimed but the effect crashes between frames. (c) would leave the two retirement rows unused forever and makes test 26 impossible.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_stdin.rs` (10, Linux, real shims): 26 + 35 claim without a frame → retired `shim_not_received`, the tombstone refuses the identity, a stale attachment's frame is dropped; 27 a torn frame → retired, no byte read; 28 a full frame without its answer → `outcome_unknown`, a resubmission is a duplicate, read once; 29 a blocked pipe → `outcome_unknown` after the bounded wait; 30 a partial write → `outcome_unknown` `stdin_partial_write` and input blocked; 31 EPIPE on the first call → retired `shim_write_failed`; 32 an evicted record → `forgotten`, `outcome_unknown`; 33 an old-generation frame after a takeover → retired, no byte, and a stale generation claims no identity; 34 a shim killed during a write → `lost` `execution_unknown`, `outcome_unknown`. `manager::shim_stdin::decide_tests` (input block in the decision transaction; stop admitted; claims refused for a stale generation or a settled effect) and `manager::shim_stdin::tests` (per-effect combination).
- Not done in this slice: the §2.1 adapter checkpoint and replayable transitions, request reconciliation after a reattach (ADR tests 15, 36, 37, 46, 47), the session-level wiring of the adapters' stdin to `send_input`, the station halves of 38, 43, 49, and the host setting. The host setting stays `direct`.
- Owner review: not required (implementation of the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-106 — ShimHost stdin settlement: a later unwritten frame never retires an effect whose earlier frames were written (M3 round 26)

- Context: review feedback on round 25 (DL-105): `send_input` returned only the state of the last frame, and `settle_live` and the recovery `combine` retired an effect when one frame was unwritten. A multi-frame effect (input above `MAX_PAYLOAD`, 1 MiB) whose frame 0 reached the provider and whose frame 1 failed (`failed_unwritten`, `closed_unwritten`, forgotten, mismatch, or never sent after its claim) was retired as `retired_not_dispatched`. The provider had read part of the input, so the retirement was false and a resubmission could duplicate input.
- Options: (a) count the written frames in the live result and classify each frame at recovery; retire only when every frame is proven unwritten; (b) refuse inputs above one frame at admission; (c) keep the per-frame table and accept the risk.
- Choice: (a). `Sent::Ended { frames_written, state }` and `Sent::Refused { frames_written, answer }` (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/shim_stdin.rs`); a claim or transport failure after frame 0 is `Ended` with `frames_written`, never `NotSent`. `settle_live`: any end or refusal after at least one written frame is `outcome_unknown` with `input_blocked { stdin_partial_write }` (part of the input reached the provider). Recovery classifies every frame as written, unwritten (with its reason), partial or unknown; `combine` retires only when every frame is unwritten, keeps a one-frame unknown as plain `outcome_unknown`, and blocks input for a partial frame or any multi-frame mix.
- Reason: (b) removes a supported input size (ADR-005 §4.3 permits chunked inputs); (c) violates the §4.3 rule that only proof of no delivered byte retires a row. Blocking input is the ADR answer for a partial input: the owner interrupts or stops the session.
- Tests: `manager::shim_stdin::tests::a_later_unwritten_frame_never_retires_an_effect_whose_earlier_frames_were_written` (unit, live and recovery combinations) and the rewritten `an_effect_is_retired_only_when_every_frame_is_unwritten`; `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_stdin.rs` `a_later_unwritten_frame_never_retires_an_effect_whose_earlier_frame_was_written` (real shim: the provider reads exactly frame 0 and closes stdin; 1.5 MiB input; live result and recovery give `outcome_unknown` `stdin_partial_write`, input blocked) and `recovery_never_retires_an_effect_whose_first_frame_was_written_and_second_never_sent` (frame 1 claimed, never sent; recovery tombstones it, the effect is `outcome_unknown` `stdin_partial_write`). With the old rules all four tests fail; with the fix they pass.
- Owner review: not required (implementation fix inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-107 — ShimHost stdin: the complete frame count is stored with frame 0 before any byte leaves (M3 round 27)

- Context: review feedback on round 26 (DL-106): the frame rows stored only the claimed frames. If the station crashed after frame 0 of a multi-frame effect was written and before frame 1 was claimed, recovery saw one written frame. It settled a plain `outcome_unknown` and left the session open for input, although the provider held only a prefix of the input.
- Options: (a) store the complete frame count of the effect with every frame row (so with frame 0, in the claim transaction before any byte leaves) and count missing rows as unwritten frames at recovery; (b) claim every frame identity of the effect in one transaction before frame 0 leaves; (c) block input for every `outcome_unknown` stdin effect.
- Choice: (a). Schema V11 adds `shim_writes.frames` (default 1 for V10 rows, which were all one-frame claims in the build that wrote them). `Store::shim_claim_write(shim_id, generation, effect_id, ordinal, frames)` stores the count and refuses, with no row, an ordinal outside the count, an out-of-order ordinal, or a count that differs from the one stored with frame 0 (a caller bug, `StoreError::Corrupt`). `ClaimedWrite.frames` carries the count; `settle_writes` adds one `Unwritten(shim_not_received)` frame for each frame without a row, so a written frame 0 plus a never-claimed frame 1 combines to `outcome_unknown` `stdin_partial_write` and blocks input. `send_input` passes `bytes.len().div_ceil(MAX_PAYLOAD)`.
- Reason: (b) also works but makes the shim answer `not_received` for identities that were never meant to leave yet, and changes the per-frame claim order of ADR-005 §4.3. (c) blocks input after any lost one-frame answer, which the ADR does not require. (a) keeps one claim per frame and makes the count durable before the first byte.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_stdin.rs` `recovery_blocks_input_when_frame_0_was_written_and_frame_1_was_never_claimed` (real shim; frame 0 of 2 written, crash before the claim of frame 1; recovery gives `outcome_unknown` `stdin_partial_write` and the session input is blocked; the provider read frame 0 only). With the missing frames not counted, the test fails. `manager::shim_stdin::decide_tests::the_frame_count_is_stored_with_frame_0_and_claims_stay_in_order` (count stored with frame 0, out-of-range, out-of-order, repeated and changed-count claims refused without a row).
- Owner review: not required (implementation fix inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-108 — ShimHost slice 6: versioned adapter checkpoints, proven request times, expired allows never dispatched (M3 round 28)

- **Context:** ADR-005 §2.1 requires recoverable adapter state committed with every transition (outbound in the frame claim transaction, inbound with the output transaction), restored without spawn, `initialize`, `thread/start` or native resume, with a declared version and `adapter_checkpoint_unsupported` for an unreadable one. §6 "Approvals across a restart" requires that open requests keep `requested_at`/`deny_at`, that a replayed request uses the shim's `read_at`, that a request without a proven time expires with the safe deny, and that a queued allow after expiry is never dispatched (test 15). Review feedback of round 27 named tests 15, 36, 37, 46 and 47.
- **Options:** (a) a transition log replayed onto an older checkpoint; (b) a complete versioned snapshot of the sans-IO core committed in each transaction that changes it (a checkpoint with zero later transitions); (c) keep the state in memory and resume natively.
- **Choice:** (b). `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/checkpoint.rs` defines `AdapterCheckpoint { provider, version, state }`, `CheckpointError` (`adapter_checkpoint_unsupported`, `adapter_checkpoint_corrupt`), `reads(provider)` and `readable(provider, text)`. `CodexCore` and `ClaudeCore` serialize completely except their `CoreConfig` (passed again at restore): RPC id counter and correlations, ordinals, inputs, queued follow-ups, pending mode/model, running turn, tools, open and closed requests (`codex/checkpoint.rs`, `claude/checkpoint.rs`, version 1, reads `[1]`). `restore(config, checkpoint)` writes no frame. Station: schema V12 adds `shims.adapter_checkpoint` and `runtime_requests.time_proven`; `shim_claim_write_with(.., checkpoint)` stores the checkpoint in the claim transaction only when the claim is accepted; `shim_commit_output_with(.., OutputMeta { read_at, checkpoint })` stores it with the events and the cursor, for the current generation only. `RequestTime { Now, Read(t), Unprovable }`: a replayed request takes `min(read_at, now)`; an unprovable time under `deny_after` is due at once. `claim_answer` refuses a non-deny option past its deadline (`closed: expired`); `claim_next` retires an allow that became overdue while queued (`request_expired`) and releases the receipt so the deny sweep sends the safe deny. `recover_shim` ends a reattached row `lost` with the checkpoint reason when the stored checkpoint is unreadable, without a fresh adapter and without stopping the shim.
- **Reason:** (b) gives the same recovery guarantee as (a) with one code path, and every committed state already contains every RPC id it used, so a restored counter is never below a used id. (c) contradicts §2.1.
- **Tests:** `codex::tests_checkpoint` (5: ADR tests 46, 47, 36 and 37 at the adapter, held interrupt round trip), `claude::checkpoint` (1), `store::effects_tests::request_time` (5: ADR test 15 rules and checkpoint storage only on accepted claims/commits), `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_recovery.rs` test 37 station half with a real shim. Mutation proofs: without the claim-time retirement the queued-allow test fails; with replay time as request time the read-time test fails.
- **Not done:** the live drivers do not yet commit their core checkpoint through `shim_claim_write_with`/`shim_commit_output_with` and restore it on reattach (that is the ShimHost session wiring with adapter stdin through `send_input`); tests 36, 46, 47 therefore cover the adapter and the store halves, not one end-to-end real-shim run. The host setting stays `direct`.
- **Owner review:** not required (implements accepted ADR-005 revision 3); architect code review pending.

## DL-109 — files.v1 search: the per-file hit budget marks the search truncated; a file of exactly the size budget keeps its final line (M3 round 29)

- Context: CODE RE-REVIEW sa-713c2ae4 of 4d00f4c: VERDICT FAIL. It closed the `resolve_revert` fence finding (DL-101) and the private allowed root (DL-103). Two search findings stay open: (MAJOR) `grep_file` in `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/files.rs` stopped at the per-file hit budget (5) without setting `truncated`, so a sixth match disappeared silently; (MINOR) a file of exactly `WALK_FILE_BYTES` (1,048,576 bytes), read completely, counted as cut, so its final line without a newline was dropped and the search was marked `truncated`. Both apply to the Git and the walk backends, which share `grep_file`.
- Options for the MAJOR: (a) set `truncated` when a further match exists after the budget; (b) set `truncated` as soon as the budget is reached; (c) remove the per-file budget. For the MINOR: (a) read one byte past the budget and call the file cut only when that byte exists; (b) compare the `fstat` size with the budget; (c) keep the rule.
- Choice: MAJOR (a): the loop sets `truncated` and stops only when a sixth matching line is found, so exactly five matches are a complete result. MINOR (a): `grep_file` reads up to `WALK_FILE_BYTES + 1` bytes through the descriptor; only a read longer than the budget is cut back to the budget and to its last complete line (a cut prefix without a newline searches nothing).
- Reason: (a)/(a) report `truncated` exactly when a budget hid a match or text. MAJOR (b) marks complete results as truncated; (c) lets one file fill the global hit budget. MINOR (b) trusts a size that can change between `fstat` and the read; the extra byte is the read itself.
- Tests: `manager::files::files_review3_tests` (4): Git and walk searches of a file with `"needle\n".repeat(6)` give 5 hits and `truncated = true` (5 matches: complete); Git and walk searches of an exactly 1,048,576-byte file (newline at byte 0, `needle` in the final 6 bytes, no trailing newline) give 1 hit on line 2 and `truncated = false`, and the same file one byte larger gives no hit and `truncated = true`. Mutation proofs (each restored): without the `truncated` at the budget both budget tests fail; with the old `>=` cut rule both exact-size tests fail.
- Owner review: not required (fix inside the files.v1 contract; the architect's re-review is still needed before 6-8 is checked).

## DL-110 — Durable Codex driver over the shim (ADR-005 §2.1, §4.1, §4.3; M3 round 30)

- Context: DL-108 made the Codex and Claude cores checkpointable, but the live drivers (`crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/driver.rs`) emit events into an `EventSink` and write frames from a separate task. They cannot commit the events, the adapter state and the cursor of one output line in one transaction, and they cannot commit an outbound transition before its frame leaves. ADR-005 tests 36, 46 and 47 need that end to end. Review feedback (round 25 of the goal): "continue live ShimHost integration with real-shim recovery tests".
- Options: (a) change the provider drivers to call a station callback per line; (b) a station-side durable driver that runs the sans-IO `CodexCore` directly over a `ShimProcess` and the station store; (c) keep the shim path unit-only and wire later.
- Choice: (b), `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/shim_codex.rs` `DurableCodex`:
  - Output: per complete stdout line, one transaction (`Store::shim_commit_output_with`) commits the events (request time = the shim's `read_at` of the chunk that holds the line start, `ShimProcess::read_at`; `Unprovable` when no mark exists), the new checkpoint, the frames that the line generated (schema V13 `shim_frames`, state `pending`), and the cursor after the line. The acknowledgment follows the commit; the frames are sent after it.
  - Commands (`DurableCodex::apply`): the command runs on the core, then a transition row (schema V13 `shim_transitions`: effect id, frame count) and its events are committed before any frame claim; the frames leave through `send_input` (DL-105/107 claims). A proven-unwritten result restores the pre-command core and drops the transition (`retired_not_dispatched`); an uncertain result keeps it (`outcome_unknown`, partial → input blocked).
  - Generated frames: claimed (`shim_claim_frame`, write id `internal:<session>:frame:<seq>:<write_seq>`) before they leave and marked `done` after `written`; an unprovable result blocks input (`internal_frame_unknown`).
  - Restore (`DurableCodex::restore`, after `recover_shims`): `CodexCore::restore` from the latest checkpoint, then the transitions after it in order, except a retired effect or a frame-bearing transition without any claim row (proven unsent, dropped); claimed frames are settled with `write_status` (`written` → done; never received or proven unwritten → pending, sent again; in flight → asked again until the wait; else input blocked); pending frames are flushed; the pump replays from the committed cursor. No `initialize`, `thread/start` or native resume is sent.
  - A committed checkpoint clears the transitions it contains.
- Reason: (b) needs no change to the provider drivers that M2 tested and keeps the transaction boundaries in the station store. (a) moves store transactions into the provider crate's tasks. (c) leaves tests 36/46/47 unit-only.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_codex.rs` (3, real shim + fake Codex app-server, its stdin log proves every frame the provider read): ADR test 46 (turn/start sent, the pump held before the response, crash; the replayed response commits one `turn.started`; the next request id is higher; one `initialize`, `thread/start`, `turn/start`), test 47 (busy follow-up, mode and model change without output, crash; after the restore one queued input and, when the turn ends, one second `turn/start` with `in-2`, `on-request`, `gpt-next`), test 36 (approval opened before the crash answered after the restore with the native id; the turn completes; no second handshake). Mutation proof: restore without the transitions fails 2 of 3.
- Not done: the session manager still uses the direct host (wiring the durable driver into `SessionTarget`, the host setting, a Claude durable driver, and the station halves of ADR tests 38, 43, 49 remain).
- Owner review: not required (implementation of the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-111 — Generated-frame state changes are fenced by owner generation and claim (ADR-005 §4.3; M3 round 31)

- Context: review feedback on DL-110: `Store::shim_frame_state` updated a frame by `(shim_id, frame_seq)` only. A delayed old owner, whose attachment was taken over, could move a frame that the new owner had already settled and written back to `pending`, so the new owner's next flush sent the completed frame again (a duplicate stdin write to the provider).
- Options: (a) fence by the owner generation only; (b) fence by the owner generation and the exact current claim (`write_id`, `write_seq`) of a `claimed` frame; (c) drop the state update and settle every frame from the shim's records at each flush.
- Choice: (b). `shim_frame_state(shim_id, generation, frame_seq, tag, state) -> bool` changes a frame only when `generation` is the current owner generation of an open row, the frame is `claimed`, and its stored claim equals `tag`; target `Claimed` is a `Corrupt` error (only `shim_claim_frame` claims). A stale call returns `false` and changes nothing; the driver (`settle_frames`, `flush` in `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/manager/shim_codex.rs`) then stops, because a newer owner settles. A `done` frame never returns to `pending`.
- Reason: the generation alone does not cover a superseded claim of the same owner (a frame settled `pending` and claimed again), and (c) adds a status round trip per frame on every flush. The claim tag is already stored with the frame, so the fence is one `UPDATE … WHERE` in the same transaction.
- Tests: `store::shims::tests::frame_state_changes_are_fenced_by_the_owner_generation_and_the_current_claim` (stale generation, superseded claim, done never pending, ended row, `Claimed` refused) and the real-shim regression `a_delayed_old_owner_cannot_make_the_new_owner_resend_a_completed_frame` in `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/shim_codex.rs` (the old owner commits and claims a frame and is delayed; the new owner proves it unwritten and writes it; the old owner's `pending` and `done` reports are refused; after more output the fake app-server read the frame once). With the unfenced update both tests fail.
- Owner review: not required (a correctness fix inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-112 — One durable driver for both providers, and the station start order with shims (ADR-005 §2.1, §3.2, §4.3; M3 round 32)

- Context: round-31 feedback asked for the durable Claude driver and the session-manager integration with the station recovery tests. DL-110 wrote the durable driver for Codex only, and nothing ordered the shim recovery against the generic store recovery at a station start.
- Options: (a) copy the Codex driver for Claude; (b) one generic driver over a trait that each sans-IO core implements; (c) a dynamic `Box<dyn>` core.
- Choice: (b). `DurableCore` (create, save/load checkpoint, line, exit, command, ended) is implemented by `CodexCore` and `ClaudeCore`; `DurableDriver<C>` holds every durability rule once. Claude prompt, steer and follow-up are one user message (ADR-001 §2.3), the same as the direct driver. `recover_station` fixes the start order: shim recovery and stdin settlement first, driver restore second, `Store::recover` with the restored processes as live last, so commands are admitted only after every row settled (§3.2) and the §4.3 settlement runs before claims become `outcome_unknown`.
- Reason: (a) would duplicate the subtle rules that DL-110 and DL-111 fixed; (c) loses the typed configs and adds dynamic dispatch on every line for no gain. The start order is the one ADR-005 states; a test fails when it is reversed.
- A row whose checkpoint cannot be restored ends `lost` with the reason (`adapter_checkpoint_unsupported` from the readability check, `adapter_checkpoint_corrupt: ...` from the restore), and its session is lost by the generic recovery. The shim is not killed and no fresh adapter replaces it.
- Not done: `SessionManager`/`SessionTarget` do not launch or dispatch through the drivers yet; turn checkpoints for shim sessions; the station halves of tests 38, 43, 49.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-113 — Sessions under shims in the session manager, output gaps, and launch boundaries at the station (ADR-005 §3.2, §4.2; M3 round 33)

- Context: round-32 feedback asked to integrate the durable drivers into `SessionManager`/`SessionTarget` with turn checkpoints and the station halves of ADR-005 tests 38, 43 and 49. The durable driver commits provider events itself, so the direct target's event pump (which feeds `SessionTurns`) does not see them.
- Options: (a) route durable events through the `EventSink` path; (b) an observer task per session that reads the committed log and feeds `SessionTurns`; (c) call `SessionTurns` inside the driver's output transaction.
- Choice: (b). The observer reads `read_after` from the store head taken before the launch, feeds `turns.observe`, spawns the completion capture, and on a terminal event removes the live session, ends the process and marks the shim row `exited`. Inputs are written under the same workspace fence and `begin_input`/`input_sent` calls as with the direct host. The first input of a launch leaves as adapter-generated frames (`Durable::first_input`), because `session.started` confirms the start effect before it. A station stop does not stop shim sessions; `start_with_shims` restores and adopts them. Revert on a shim session fails before any I/O with `revert_not_supported_on_the_shim_host` (the rollback still runs only through the direct driver).
- Output gaps: `session.output_gap { from, to, reason }` (`output_frame_too_large`, `unterminated_line`, `below_floor`) is a new event kind, decoded as unknown by older peers (ADR-005 §4.2). The pump commits each shim gap and an `error { code: output_frame_too_large }` with the cursor past the stream, and an unterminated last line as a gap with the final cursor, before the exit.
- Launch boundaries: claimed start effects are never settled by stdin settlement (the spawn happened); recovery retires them `shim_never_started` only on `NeverStarted` from an intact journal, otherwise they become `outcome_unknown`. A session lost through its shim row names the reason in its `lost` detail (for example `execution_unknown`).
- Reason: (a) would bypass the output transaction that makes the driver durable; (c) would put Git work inside the commit path and extend it. The observer uses only committed events, so it sees the same order after a restart.
- Not done: the station binary's host setting (`serve.rs`), revert on the shim host, the other ADR-005 §9 tests.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3; the new event kind follows the §4.2 contract).

## DL-114 — Restored shim sessions are observed from the head before the restoration (ADR-005 §3.2; M3 round 34)

- Context: round-33 review feedback: `shim_target::adopt` started each observer at the current head, so turn and exit events that restored drivers committed while they replayed buffered output (between `recover_station` and the adoption) were never given to the turn checkpoints, and an exit replayed in that window never ended the session.
- Options: (a) start the restored pumps only after the adoption; (b) pass the head read before the restoration into `adopt`; (c) make the observer read the whole session history.
- Choice: (b), plus two ordering rules. `SessionManager` settles open turn rows, reads the head, runs `recover_station`, and adopts with that head. `adopt` ends a session whose stored state is already `exited` or `lost`. A station stop detaches the durable pump and observer of each shim session.
- Reason: (a) would delay the stdin settlement and the frame flush that ADR-005 §3.2 and §4.3 order before commands; (c) re-observes turns that settled rows already finalized. Settling the rows first makes the replayed events the only source for the turns that run across the restart, so each turn is finalized once. The detach is needed because a stopped station in the same process otherwise kept committing output that the next station also reads.
- Not done: revert on the shim host; the `fleetdeck station` host setting; the other ADR-005 §9 tests.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-115 — A session that ended during its restoration is observed before its cleanup (ADR-005 §3.2, PLAN §6.6; M3 round 35)

- Context: round-34 feedback: `SessionTarget::adopt` (DL-114) ended a restored session at once when its summary was already `exited` or `lost`. A restored driver can replay buffered output that completes a turn and then the provider's exit before the adoption; that turn's events were then never given to the turn checkpoints, so it was never finalized.
- Options: (a) skip the cleanup and insert the session as live, so the normal observer ends it at the terminal event; (b) a drain-only observer that reads from the pre-restoration head up to the current head, then ends the session; (c) finalize the remaining turn rows directly from the stored events in `adopt`.
- Choice: (b). The observer gets a `Drain` mode: `ToTerminal` for a live session (stops at the terminal event, as before), `ToHead` for a session that ended before the adoption (stops when it has read up to the head, because its terminal event can lie before the adoption). Only then does it end the session: process ended, shim rows `exited`. The session is never inserted as live, so it takes no commands.
- Reason: (a) admits commands to an ended session for a short time; (c) duplicates the turn-checkpoint logic of `SessionTurns::observe`. (b) uses the same observation path as live sessions, so turns are finalized once, by the same code.
- Owner review: not required (implementation inside the accepted ADR-005 revision 3).

## DL-116 — The shim host as a station setting, station stop with shim sessions, and Codex revert under the shim (ADR-005 §1, §3; PLAN §6.6; M3 round 36)

- Context: round-35 feedback asked to wire the shim host into `fleetdeck station serve` and implement revert on the shim host. DL-113 refused revert under a shim (`revert_not_supported_on_the_shim_host`), and serve always used the direct host.
- Options for the setting: (a) the ADR-005 station setting `host`, default `direct`; (b) default `shim` now. Options for revert: (c) a second executor for shim sessions; (d) the same executor over a small `Rollback` trait.
- Choice: (a) and (d). `host = "shim"` selects the shim host; the default stays `direct` because ADR-005 makes `shim` the default only after 7-1 passes, and 7-1 still needs the architect's acceptance and the ShimHost code review. `shim_buffer_mib` is the §4.2 buffer B. A station stop detaches shim sessions instead of stopping them (they must survive a station update, ADR-005 §1), and removes them from the live map so the stop does not wait for them. `revert_run::run` is generic over `Rollback`; the durable Codex driver's `rollback` commits `thread/rollback` with its checkpoint (the frame is claimed before it leaves, as every generated frame) and waits for the correlated answer with the configured timeout (`ROLLBACK_TIMEOUT`, 30 s).
- Reason: one executor keeps every PLAN §6.6 rule (fence, rechecks, durable steps, recovery from evidence) in one place; the trait adds no behavior. Without the answer wait a revert would restore files after an unconfirmed rollback; the tests fail in that case.
- Crash between the rollback and the restore: the station recovery turns `rollback_sent` into `reconciliation_required` (DL-087), the shim session is restored `running`, its input stays blocked, and the rollback is never sent again (test).
- Test harness: `FakeAgent::locate` (crates/fleetdeck-testkit/src/fake.rs) now waits up to 2 s for the fake binary when it is missing, because another test process's `cargo build` of the fakes can be replacing it (a `make check` run failed once with `NotFound fake-codex` in `shim_manager`); a `shim_revert` crash test waited for the `thread/rollback` log line instead of reading it once (`rollback_sent` is committed before the frame leaves).
- Not done: the default host is still `direct`; macOS runs of the shim tests are owner/platform validation.
- Owner review: the default host change to `shim` needs the owner/architect after 7-1 is accepted.

## DL-117 — Real station kills inside a shim-host revert (PLAN §6.6, L13; ADR-005; M3 round 37)

- Context: the round-36 review rejected the `shim_revert` crash test, which simulated a station crash by dropping the station objects inside the test process (destructors ran; the process lived). It asked for a real station SIGKILL regression that covers a confirmed rollback before the file restore.
- Options: (a) SIGKILL a `station serve` process at a timed moment; (b) a fault point that aborts the process right after a revert step commits; (c) keep the in-process simulation.
- Choice: (b). `FaultPoint::AfterStep` (`after_step@<step>`) is hit in `revert_run::step` after `advance_revert` committed the step, before the next external action. The abort is uncatchable for the station (no destructors, no graceful stop), and the step names the exact durable boundary: `rollback_sent` (claimed, frame not yet sent), `rolled_back` (provider confirmed, files untouched), `restoring`.
- Reason: (a) cannot hit the narrow window between the rollback answer and the restore reliably; (c) is not a crash. The fault hook already serves the M2 crash tests and is compiled only with the `fault-injection` feature.
- Results: after `rolled_back` and `restoring` the restarted station restores the files and the revert succeeds without a second `thread/rollback`; after `rollback_sent` the revert needs reconciliation and the rollback is never sent. The shim session is reattached in each case (one `initialize`).
- Withdrawn: an observer catch-up wait before shim-host inputs; its regression passed without it, so it was not kept.
- Round 38 correction (review of round 37): the post-restart check of the two success tests waited only for an idle summary, which an undispatched prompt also gives. It now waits for the `turn/start` frame with the input's id in the provider's stdin log, checks that exactly one new `turn/start` went out, and waits for the stored `turn.completed` (`done`) of the turn that the provider opened for that input, with its answer in `message.completed`. With the prompt reported `Sent` but never written, both tests fail.
- Owner review: not required (tests and a test-only fault point inside accepted contracts).

## DL-118 — `session.resume` first, and an ADR for auto-continue (L19; M3 round 39)

- Context: round-38 feedback asked to continue 7-2 auto-continuation. L19 continues a lost session through a native resume, but the station had no resume at all: `session.resume` was refused as `unsupported` (DL-037), although both adapters implement the native resume and ADR-004 already lists the method. L19 also adds a preference, a new method, new events and dispatch rules: a new public contract.
- Options: (a) wire auto-continue directly; (b) build the owner-requested `session.resume` now and write the auto-continue contract as an ADR for design review first; (c) wait for 7-3.
- Choice: (b). `session.resume { session_id }` is admitted only for an ended session (`exited` or `lost`, no live process) whose provider instance is still configured, whose provider reported `resume` and a native resume identity, whose earlier executions are proven ended (no open shim row; no shim row lost with `execution_unknown`, `attach_refused`, `shim_incompatible` or `identity_mismatch`), with no revert that blocks input or holds the worktree, a workspace inside the allowed roots and a free session slot. The effect carries the stored spec (instance, workspace, mode, model, title), so the resumed process never gets a more permissive mode. The decision appends `session.state_changed { starting, "resume requested" }`, so the slot is taken at the commit and inputs wait for the live process; a resume that gets no process ends the session with `start_failed`. On the shim host a resume is refused at dispatch (`resume_not_supported_on_the_shim_host`) until ADR-005 §6 termination evidence and a durable resume launch exist.
- ADR-007 (`docs/v2/adr/ADR-007-auto-continue.md`, Proposed) states the preference (default off, owner-only enable), the eleven eligibility rules, the stable outbox intent per lost turn, the recheck before dispatch, the two-step dispatch with durable steps, and 17 negative tests.
- Reason: (a) would wire a new public contract without design review and on a resume that did not exist; the owner resume is the shared step of L19 and of the deliverable-3 driven import, and its admission rules are the L19 safety rules that do not depend on the preference.
- Owner review: `session.resume` is inside the PLAN contract (A10, ADR-004 method table); ADR-007 needs the architect's adversary DESIGN REVIEW before any auto-continue code.

## DL-119 — Resume termination checks fail closed (ADR-005 §4.6, §6; M3 round 40)

- Context: the round-39 review found that `Decision::unended_execution` (DL-118) refused a resume only for four listed `lost` reasons. A shim row that recovery ended `lost` because its adapter checkpoint was unsupported or corrupt, or because no driver or live process existed, left the shim and its provider running, yet the session was resumable: a resume could start a second execution of one native conversation.
- Options: (a) add the missing reasons to the list; (b) list the outcomes that prove the end and refuse everything else.
- Choice: (b). Only `never_started` (an intact launch journal proves no spawn) and `exited` with the reason `exited` (the shim reported the provider's exit with no remaining member of its process group or session) prove the end. Recovery now records an exit with surviving members as `exited_descendants_remaining`. Every other row, including reasons that a later build may add, refuses `session.resume` with `not_resumable` and the row's state and reason.
- Reason: a deny list fails open for every reason it forgets, and ADR-005 §6 requires complete termination evidence before any resume. An allow list fails closed for new reasons by construction.
- Not done: the audited owner reconciliation of ADR-005 §4.6 (the way to make such a session resumable after the owner stops the remaining processes); a reboot check (`boot_id`) for shim rows.
- Owner review: not required (a stricter reading of the accepted ADR-005).

## DL-120 — A live shim exit keeps the descendant evidence (ADR-005 §4.6; M3 round 41)

- Context: the round-40 review found that DL-119 applied the descendant rule only at recovery (exit file). When the station itself observed a session's end, `end_observed` wrote the shim row `exited`/`exited` unconditionally, so a provider exit that left a live child in its process group became a proven end and `session.resume` was accepted.
- Options: (a) read `/proc` from the station at the end; (b) use the shim's own exit report (`descendants_remaining`, counted by the shim before it signals the group) for both paths.
- Choice: (b). The shim host keeps the exit report it receives (`ShimProcess::exit_state`). `end_observed` waits up to 10 s for the attachment's end and maps the report with `shims::exit_reason`: `exited` only with zero survivors, `exited_descendants_remaining` otherwise, and `exit_not_reported` when no report arrived. Recovery uses the same function.
