This does not reopen the ADR-005 §8 alternative ("KillMode=process instead of shims"): the shims still own the output and the fencing; the unit setting only stops systemd from killing them (ADR-005 §7 "Station graceful stop").
  - The container file `packaging/container/Containerfile` has three stages (bun 1.4.2 board build, Rust 1.96 build with `--locked`, Debian bookworm-slim runtime), a non-root user `fleetdeck` (UID 10001), `tini` as PID 1 (it reaps ended shims), the data volume `/data` (`FLEETDECK_DATA_DIR`), no provider CLI (a derived image adds it). A container stop ends every agent (like a reboot).
- Reason: (a) the generated workflow must come from a `dist` run that the owner can repeat; a hand-written workflow would drift. (d) the station runs the provider CLIs with the owner's login (PLAN §6.8); a system unit needs a second login per user.
- Evidence: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/packaging.rs` (5 tests; `make package-check`; also part of the workspace tests of `make check`): every command that a unit or the image starts is run against the built binary with `--help`, so a renamed command fails the check.
- Owner steps: `dist init`/`dist generate` with network access (writes `.github/workflows/release.yml`), the tap repository, the image build (`docker build -f packaging/container/Containerfile .`), launchd on a macOS host.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-401: performance harness (M3 item 7-4)

- Context: PLAN §13 bars: approval round trip board→agent < 300 ms on a LAN; deck read-model lag p95 < 500 ms; 50 sessions per station; 10 stations per deck; station overhead < 30 MB + < 5 MB per session (agent process excluded). The build machine has one host (WSL2) and no LAN peer.
- Options: (a) a separate benchmark binary; (b) an `#[ignore]` integration test on the existing real-process harness (`crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/common`), run by `make perf` in the release profile.
- Choice: (b), `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/perf.rs::performance_bars_of_plan_section_13`. One deck and ten stations as separate processes on loopback (TLS links, separate data directories), `fake-claude sim` agents. Method: station memory is `VmRSS` of the station process (agents are other processes) idle and with 50 live idle sessions; the read-model lag is the time from the receipt of `station run` to the card in `GET /api/sessions` (2 ms polling, a new TLS connection per poll, so it is an upper bound); the approval round trip is the time from `POST /api/commands/approval.answer` (new TLS connection) to the answer line on the agent's stdin (1 ms polling). The test writes `target/perf/report.md` and fails when a bar fails.
- Reason: the harness reuses the tested process control, so it stays reproducible with one command; an ignored test keeps `make check` fast and offline.
- Limits: loopback is not a LAN; the LAN approval round trip is an owner step (two hosts). The report records the host, the profile, the transport and the sample counts.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-402: request ids collide between sessions of one station (defect found by 7-4)

- Context: the perf harness answered approvals on fresh sessions from the board and got `unknown_request` for the second session. Cause: the Claude adapter numbers request ids per session (`core.ids.next("req")` in `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/claude/requests.rs:87`, also `req-{n}` in `codex/requests.rs:194`), but the station table `runtime_requests` has `request_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY` (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/store/schema.rs:128`) with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (request_id) DO NOTHING` (`store/requests.rs:264-268`), and the answer lookup is by `request_id` alone (`store/requests.rs:173`, `manager/decide.rs:101`). When two sessions of one station reach the same ordinal, the second request is not stored, and the board cannot answer it.
- Options: (a) fix it in lane D now; (b) record it, work around it in the harness, and give it to the lane that owns the station store.
- Choice: (b). The fix changes the station store key (a schema version and every request query) or the adapter id format (replay fixtures and snapshots); both are outside lane D and touch the durable code of lane A. The harness measures every approval on one session with board follow-ups, which has unique ids.
- Reason: lane rules (do only lane items; a fix must not break another lane's work). The defect is real for any owner with two sessions that ask for approval at the same time.
- Follow-up: key `runtime_requests` by `(session_id, request_id)` (or make request ids unique per station) with a regression test "two sessions with the same native request ordinal can each be answered".
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-403: owner decision, fast rounds and parallel lanes (approved)

- The owner (2026-10-12) asked for a usable M3 soon: each item gets one review, findings go to the "Review follow-ups (after M3)" list of the lane log, normal rounds run only the format and lint checks, and the full `make check` runs once at the end in lane A. Four lanes build M3 in parallel; lane D owns 7-5 Packaging, 7-4 Performance and 6-11 Human shell, in this order.
- Owner review: approved (owner decision).

## DL-404: human shell (M3 item 6-11)

- Context: PLAN §6.8 and D10: the station owns a PTY with the owner's login shell; policy checks (`allow_shell`, `max_shells`, allowed roots); a scrubbed environment; open, resize and close are audited commands with a `command_id`; keystrokes and output go only on the link data channel (A7) and are not audited; at most 5,000 lines and 8 MiB of scrollback; a reconnecting board gets the scrollback, then the live bytes; the shell survives a deck, board or network failure; a station restart ends it (A10). The plan does not name the link methods, the board API or the board scope.
- Choice:
  - PTY: `portable-pty` 0.9 (the crate of PLAN §12 for PTYs). Station module `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/shell/` (`Shells` registry, reader thread per shell, `Scrollback` ring with both limits). The environment is `ChildEnv` (the same scrub as agents) plus `TERM=xterm-256color`. The program is `$SHELL -l`, else `/bin/sh -l`.
  - Link (ADR-004 extension rule, feature `shell.v1`): `shell.list` and `shell.attach` (non-mutating; `shell.attach` returns a single-use `stream_token` for a data stream with the purpose `shell:<shell_id>`), and `shell.open`, `shell.resize`, `shell.close` as mutating methods with the `ShellEnvelope` (command id, target incarnation, retry window, `ShellChange`). They go through the station receipt table like every mutation, so a retry with the same command id gets the same receipt. Rejects: `unsupported` (`allow_shell = false`), `invalid_command` (a bad field, a cwd outside the allowed roots), `invalid_state` (`max_shells`, a used id, an unknown shell).
  - Deck: the deck keeps the data channel of each live link (`link/streams.rs`, `DataHub`); board API `GET`/`POST /api/stations/{station}/shells` and the WebSocket `GET /api/stations/{station}/shells/{shell}/attach` (binary frames both ways). The deck writes one audit row for each open, resize and close (accepted or refused, with the command id); it writes no row for keystrokes.
  - Scope: the board shell routes need the `admin` scope (the shell is owner-only and can do everything that the owner can do on the station host), although the link methods use the `drive` scope (the link has no admin role for a deck; the station keeps its own `allow_shell` gate).
  - Board: a "Shells" panel on each station card of the stations screen (admin only) with an xterm.js 6 terminal (`@xterm/xterm` 6.0.0, `@xterm/addon-fit` 0.11.0, loaded on demand). Detach keeps the shell; Attach shows the scrollback again.
  - Lifetime: shells belong to the station process, not to a link: a link drop or a deck restart does not end them; `RunningStation::stop` and a process exit end them.
- Reason: the smallest design that meets §6.8 with the existing receipt, audit and data-channel machinery; no new table and no new schema version.
- Limits (follow-ups): the deck holds no shell state, so the scrollback lives only on the station; a shell that exits stays in the list (state `exited` and the exit code) until a close; the board terminal resizes the PTY with an audited command on each fit.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-405: shell commands are durable effects; bounded shell queues; shell sockets end with the login (review of 6-11)

- Context: the review of 6-11 (round 2) found three defects. (1) `shell.open`, `shell.resize` and `shell.close` did the PTY I/O inside the receipt transaction (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/link/intake.rs`), so a crash could commit a receipt for I/O that never happened, or do I/O for a receipt that rolled back; ADR-001 wants side effects as durable effects after the commit. (2) Each attached reader had an unbounded queue, so a slow board could grow the station memory without a limit. (3) An attached shell WebSocket stayed open after a logout, a token revocation or an expiry.
- Options for (1): (a) a new `shell_effects` table with the same lifecycle as the session effects (queued, claimed, then confirmed or failed; start recovery retires queued rows and marks claimed rows `outcome_unknown`); (b) reuse the session `effects` table (it is keyed to sessions and provider incarnations, and its worker targets provider processes); (c) do the I/O first and record the receipt after it (a crash between the two gives I/O without a receipt).
- Choice: (a). The receipt transaction only checks the change (pending opens and closes count for the id, the cap and unknown shells) and inserts one `shell_effects` row (schema V15, the end of the station migration list). After the commit, `shell::dispatch` claims the rows in order and does the PTY I/O on a blocking thread; the result goes to the row (`confirmed`, or `failed` with the reason). The station answers the link request after the dispatch, so the board sees the shell in the list at once. At station start, `recover` sets queued rows to `retired_not_dispatched` and claimed rows to `outcome_unknown` (detail `station_restart`); nothing runs after a restart, which matches A10 (a restart ends every shell). A retry with the same command id gets the stored receipt and runs nothing.
- Choice for (2): each reader gets a bounded queue of 256 chunks (`SUBSCRIBER_QUEUE`). A full queue ends that reader (its data stream closes); the shell keeps running and the scrollback keeps everything within 5,000 lines and 8 MiB, so the board can attach again.
- Choice for (3): the relay checks `principal_alive` every `ws_recheck` (30 s in production, as the board WebSocket), and closes the socket with code 4401 ("login ended") and closes the data stream. A database error counts as an ended login (fail closed). The shell keeps running.
- Reason: the same durable-effect rule as every other station side effect; bounded memory per reader; the same login-end rule as the board WebSocket.
- Merge note: lane A also adds a station schema V15; renumber at the merge (lane rule).
- Tests: `shell::effects_tests::{a_shell_command_committed_before_a_crash_never_runs_after_the_restart, shell_effects_run_after_the_commit_in_order_and_a_pty_failure_is_recorded, a_slow_reader_is_cut_off_and_the_shell_keeps_running_with_its_scrollback}` (station lib), `an_attached_shell_socket_closes_after_a_logout_and_a_token_revocation` (`crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/board_shell.rs`).
- Supersedes the "no new table and no new schema version" line of DL-404.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-135 — Cutover clean-up: v1 documents and the v1 changelog leave `v2` (owner request, after M3)

- Context: after the 7-7 cutover (DL-132), branch `v2` still held v1 documents: `docs/v1/`, the v1 reviews and lessons in `docs/*.md`, `docs/assets/` (v1 screenshots), the v1 README in `docs/v0/README.md`, the v1 changelog and a v1 threat model in `SECURITY.md`. The owner asked to remove every file that v2 does not need.
- Options: (a) keep the v1 documents as a record on `v2`; (b) remove them from `v2` and point to branch `main` and the `v0.*` tags, where they stay.
- Choice: (b). Removed `docs/v1/`, `docs/assets/`, `docs/AUDIT-2026-07.md`, `docs/CODER.md`, `docs/fleetdeck-future.md`, `docs/fleetdeck-v1.0.md`, `docs/fleetdeck-v1.0-review.md`, `docs/orca-lessons.md`, `docs/t3code-lessons.md` and `docs/ux-feedback-plan.md`. `docs/v0/README.md` is now a short pointer to `main` and the tags (the cutover test keeps it). `CHANGELOG.md` starts again for 2.0 and points to the v1 changelog on `main`. `SECURITY.md` keeps the reporting process and now states the v2 threat model. PLAN §4 and `docs/v2/t3code-lessons.md` say where the v1 sources now are. `spikes/` stays, because the production replay and schema tests read its fixtures. `.claude/` stays unchanged (agent tooling configuration).
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/cutover.rs` (3 passed).
- Reason: a `v2` checkout should contain only what v2 builds, tests, documents or releases; git history, `main` and the tags keep v1.
- Owner review: requested by the owner (2026-10-12).

## DL-136 — PRE-PR review fixes: owner-only coordinator starts, quiet config errors, the shell off by default, CI on `main` (after M3)

- Context: the adversary PRE-PR review of `41d6bb1` (verdict FAIL) found: (1) a bearer token with the `drive` scope could create a coordinator grant through `POST /api/commands/session.start`, although ADR-006 §4 says that only the owner creates a grant; (2) a TOML syntax error in `station.toml` printed the source line, which can hold a provider secret in `env`; (3) `.github/workflows/v2.yml` ran only for pushes and pull requests to `v2`, so the `v2` → `main` pull request ran no check; (4) `deny_after(é)` panicked in `parse_duration`; (5) `allow_shell` defaulted to `true` in the code but `false` in `docs/v2/operations.md`; (6) the README quick start used `fleetdeck` without putting `target/release` on `PATH`.
- Options for (5): (a) keep `true` and change the documentation; (b) make the code fail closed with `false`, as documented. Options for (2): (a) keep the parser text; (b) print only the line, the column and the parser message with every double-quoted value replaced by `"…"`.
- Choice: (1) the command route gets the principal; a coordinator start from a bearer token is refused with 403 `owner_only` before any grant is stored or any command is sent, and the refusal is audited. (2) (b) for `station.toml` and `deck.toml`. (3) the workflow runs for `main` and `v2`. (4) the duration parser matches the ASCII unit suffix and parses ASCII digits only. (5) (b): the human shell is off unless `allow_shell = true`. (6) the README exports `PATH` after the build, as `docs/v2/TESTING.md` does.
- Tests: `a_bearer_token_cannot_start_a_coordinator_or_create_a_grant` (`crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/http_commands.rs`); `config::tests::{parse_errors_never_echo_config_values, durations, an_empty_file_gives_the_defaults}` (`crates/fleetdeck-core`).
- Reason: authorization, secret handling and shell access must fail closed (PLAN §9, A-decisions); a check that does not run on the merge target is not a check.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-137 — Second PRE-PR review fixes: owner-only grant revoke and renew, value-free config errors (after M3)

- Context: the adversary re-review of `781f2f8` found: (B1) the DL-136 filter hid only double-quoted values, so a TOML type error still printed a numeric or floating-point `env` value, an escaped quote let a string through, and an unknown variant showed between backticks; (B2) `POST /api/coordinator/grants/{id}/revoke` and `.../renew` needed only the `drive` scope, although ADR-006 §3 makes both owner actions; (N1) `docs/v2/operations.md` said that `allowed_roots` is required when `station.toml` exists, but the code uses `["~"]`.
- Options for B1: (a) a better value filter on the parser message; (b) no parser text at all: the line, the column and a fixed category. Options for N1: (a) make the key required; (b) document the default.
- Choice: B1 (b): `toml_category` maps the parser message to a fixed text (`unknown field`, `unknown variant`, `invalid type`, `invalid value`, `invalid length`, `duplicate key`, else `invalid TOML syntax`); only `missing field` keeps its name, because the name comes from this crate's schema. B2: both handlers call `owner_only` first and refuse a bearer token with 403 `owner_only`, whatever its scopes, before any read or write; the refusal is audited. N1 (b): the documentation states the `["~"]` default (no behaviour change).
- Tests: `config::tests::parse_errors_never_echo_config_values` (numeric, floating-point, escaped-quote, unknown-variant and unknown-key canaries in Display and Debug), `a_bearer_token_cannot_revoke_or_renew_a_coordinator_grant` (`crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/board_fleet.rs`: drive, read+drive and all-scope tokens; the grant is unchanged; the owner still revokes).
- Reason: secrets and owner authority must fail closed; a value filter cannot know every format of the parser text.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-138 — Third PRE-PR review fixes: owner login for coordinator lifecycle, shells and pairing (after M3)

- Context: the adversary re-review of `c6717d9` confirmed the DL-137 fixes and found three more routes where a bearer token acted for the owner: (B3) the generic `POST /api/commands/session.coordinator_renew` and `.../session.coordinator_revoke` took a `drive` token, reached the station with the Board origin and skipped the deck's grant transitions; (B4) the shell routes (list, open/resize/close, attach) took an `admin` token, although PLAN §9 says "Shells are owner-only" (DL-404 had chosen the `admin` scope); (B5) `POST /api/stations`, `/api/stations/invite` and `/api/stations/{station}/remove` took an `admin` token, although PLAN §9 says "Only the owner configures targets".
- Options: (a) keep the `admin` scope as the owner stand-in and document it; (b) require the owner's board login (`Principal::Owner`) in the handlers, with the scope check of the API table kept as the first gate.
- Choice: (b), because PLAN §9 and ADR-006 §3 are the source of truth and a token can leave the owner's devices. A shared `guard::owner_only` refuses a token principal with 403 `owner_only` before any read, body parse, station I/O or write; WebSocket tickets keep their token identity, so a token ticket cannot attach a shell. The generic coordinator renew/revoke routes are removed from the HTTP command table (the link methods stay; only the owner-checked grant routes queue them; the board already used those routes). Refusals are audited (`shell.open` Refused, `stations.*` Failed, detail `owner_only`). The `fleetdeck deck` CLI pairs through the local owner socket and is unchanged.
- Tests: `board_shell::an_attached_shell_socket_closes_after_a_logout_and_a_token_revocation` (an admin token's ticket attach gets 403, list and open get 403 `owner_only`, the shell list is unchanged), `board_pairing::pairing_needs_admin_csrf_a_listener_and_a_node` (an all-scope token gets 403 `owner_only` on invite, add and remove; the station list is unchanged; audit rows), `http::methods::tests::every_command_method_has_the_scope_of_its_link_method` (16 generic command routes).
- Not changed (adversary note N2): semantic config errors still quote the rejected value of non-secret keys (policy text, durations, instance ids, paths, hostnames); provider `env` values never appear. DL-137's "value-free" means TOML parser errors.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-139 — macOS build: the FIFO test transport is Linux-only (owner testing)

- Context: `cargo build` failed on the owner's macOS machine. `crates/fleetdeck-link/src/transport.rs` (and its spike copy `spikes/s4-link/src/transport.rs`) used `rustix::fs::mkfifoat`, which rustix does not offer on macOS, and tokio's `pipe::OpenOptions::read_write`, which exists only on Linux. A files test fixture also used `mkfifoat`. CI and `make check` run only on Linux, so nothing caught it.
- Options: (a) a macOS FIFO implementation (`mkfifo` through libc, and a second open for the accept FIFO); (b) keep the FIFO transport Linux-only, because it is only a test fallback for Linux sandboxes that deny IP sockets (`FD_LINK_TRANSPORT=fifo:<dir>` or `auto`), and refuse it elsewhere.
- Choice: (b). `make_fifo` and the new `open_accept` are `cfg(target_os = "linux")`; on other systems they return `io::ErrorKind::Unsupported` ("the fifo link transport needs Linux; use FD_LINK_TRANSPORT=tcp"). The two FIFO I/O tests run on Linux only. The files fixture makes its FIFO with `mkfifo(1)`, which exists on both systems, so its tests still run on macOS.
- Verification: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin` and the same with `--release` give no errors (cross-check on Linux with a stub C compiler; no linking). Linux: clippy `-D warnings`, transport and files tests 43/43.
- Not done: a macOS CI job (owner step; operations §12 lists the macOS host). Test-only warnings for macOS remain in Linux-only tests.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-140 — Board UI rebuild and TUI handover (owner request, 2026-10-12)

- Context: The owner found the v2 board unusable: the session screen is a form, the timeline has no markdown and no auto-scroll, Stop and Resume are missing, and the shell is hidden in the Stations screen. The owner wants terminals into each station, a chat way to drive the agent, a phone UI that is mostly chat (like T3 Code), and the TUI too. The owner asked for all of it now, on `v2`, with no phases.
- Options for the agent view: (a) the provider TUI as the main view; (b) our own chat view of the canonical events, with an explicit TUI handover. (a) is not possible under D3: a second process on a driven native session corrupts its history.
- Choice: (b). The full design is `docs/v2/UI-PLAN.md`. Summary: chat view first; desktop, tablet and phone layouts; hash routes; `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` without raw HTML; one session controller; a board-level terminal dock; slash commands from `session.started`. The TUI handover is station-authoritative: new shell changes `shell.handover` and `shell.release` (feature `shell.handover.v1`), a durable `session.terminal` event folded into `SessionSummary.terminal`, the hold taken at admission in the same transaction, and released only when the TUI process is gone. Resume, auto-continue, import and a second handover refuse while a hold exists.
- Advisor review (gpt-6.1-sol): adopted in full. The hold starts at admission, covers other sessions with the same native id, is durable, and is not released on PTY EOF or a close request alone. After a station restart, a hold whose process still runs stays until the owner releases it.
- Owner review: approved the direction (all features, no phases).

## DL-141 — Board visual rebuild and owner-test fixes (owner request, 2026-10-13)

- Context: the owner found the DL-140 board ugly and hard to use: a fallback system font, 44 px controls on the desktop, data-dump cards, eight mostly empty lane boxes, "Needs you" three times on the home, no icons, diffs without colors. Owner testing then found: "Open in terminal" always failed with "still stopping"; mode changes seemed to do nothing; shell actions failed with `allow_shell = false`; a new session did not open; the lanes were gone in a narrow window; the file explorer was hidden behind a tab and a "Browse" click.
- Choice (visual): keep the DL-140 structure and rebuild the look in the v1 "1a Console" language. The design record is `docs/v2/UI-DESIGN.md`: bundled IBM Plex (`@fontsource`, offline), `lucide-react` icons through `web/src/ui/icons.tsx`, one primitives sheet (`base.css`, 30 px controls, 44 px only with a coarse pointer), v1 columns and cards on the fleet home with an answerable Needs-you rail, a session header with one overflow menu, one approval control surface (the dock), code blocks with a head row, colored diffs, and a terminal dock without an empty bar.
- Choice (fixes):
  - Stop: after a model turn a user's async Claude hook (here the v1 Fleet Deck plugin's `asyncRewake` Stop hook) keeps `claude -p` alive about 30 s after stdin EOF, so a graceful stop always waited the full 30 s grace. An idle Claude session now gets `idle_grace` (3 s, `ClaudeAdapterConfig`), then SIGTERM; a busy one keeps the full grace; a core locked by the reader counts as busy. The board's handover waits 60 s and shows a progress strip.
  - Mode: the core held the answers to the host's own control requests until the first `system/init`, which Claude sends only after the first prompt. Control-response events are now released at once (ADR-001 §2.4 exception).
  - `StationInfo.allow_shell` (optional, L16): the board hides shell actions on a station that refuses them and says why.
  - A single successful start opens the new session; the tablet layout (lane board on the home) starts at 600 px; the session shows the permission mode; a desktop ≥ 1280 px has a Files side panel that lists the root at once.
- Choice (v1 parity, owner request 2026-10-13 "parity with all the features in v1"): a 139-row audit of the v1 board against v2 (`docs/v2/PARITY.md` § Board parity). Built on the board: the answerable rail with keys (`[` `]` `a` `d` `1`–`9`), conflict marks on cards and in the fleet line, repo grouping and filter, Resume all, Clear and an Archived view (per-browser `localStorage`, not synced across devices), stars, the bypass mark, the last error, mail to a station/repo/everyone (one `POST /api/mail` per target), compact density, light/dark/system theme, help with concepts and first-run help, the `c` key, plan assign to a live session, the batch preview, recent and pinned directories, the Files side panel and a global Files page (`#/files`, `?workspace=` file routes), Markdown view and copy path, TUI Shift+Enter (ESC CR), OSC 52 copy (write only; a read request gets no answer), card mail badges and file chips (`GET /api/fleet/extras`, read scope), an activity sparkline (counted on the board while it is open), and the deck version (`deck_version` on the auth views).
- Not changed (open items): the shim host's graceful stop still uses the full grace; a stop during a turn still waits for the CLI's own exit after the turn; the archive does not sync across devices; parity gaps that need more design (PARITY.md § Board parity, last sentence).
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-142 — Worktree inventory, read only (v1 parity row 113)

- Context: worktree sessions get `<repo>--fd-<callsign>` on `fd/<callsign>`; nothing listed or removed them (v1 had a Worktrees modal with Remove).
- Options: (a) inventory and safe remove from the board; (b) a read-only inventory with a copy-able remove command.
- Adversary DESIGN REVIEW of (a) (gpt-6.1-sol, report `/tmp/fd-briefs/adversary-worktrees-design-r1.raw`): FAIL with 6 BLOCKERs — a recorded path does not prove that the station created its current occupant; session checks do not give custody of the worktree; repository config can hide untracked or edited files; `git worktree remove` has a race between its clean check and the deletion; remote-tracking refs do not prove that commits are on a server; `branch -d` is not an atomic checked-tip deletion; plus a MAJOR on receipt semantics for an effect that deletes.
- Choice: (b). Link method `workspace.worktrees` (feature `workspace.worktrees.v1`, Drive, non-mutating; only read-only Git with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, `status.showUntrackedFiles=all`, a 10 s limit per command and 45 s per call), deck route `GET /api/stations/{station}/worktrees` (Admin + owner only), and a Worktrees section on each Stations card with the verdicts clean / has work / live / missing / unknown and a "Copy the remove command" button (Git itself refuses a worktree with changes). A repository with Git filters is not inspected (verdict unknown). The word "safe" is not used.
- Adversary PRE-PR round 2 (report `/tmp/adversary-report-4aab615.md`) kept one BLOCKER open: a filter could be added to the repository config between the filter probe and `git status`. Not followed, by the architect's decision: writing `.git/config` (or a file it includes) needs local write access as the station user, and the driven agents already run commands as that user; a cloned repository cannot define filter drivers (clone copies no config). The probe closes the real gap (a filter configured before the inventory ran every time); the race gives no new capability. Owner review: please confirm or ask for status-free inspection (every worktree would then show `unknown` for changes).
- Not done: removal from the board; it needs a quarantine/retention design, stable command ids with phases, remote verification and an expected-old-value branch deletion, then a new adversary round.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-143 — Worktree inspection without `git status` (closes the DL-142 filter-race BLOCKER)

- Context: the DL-142 inventory probed `git config --get-regexp '^filter\.'` and then ran `git status`. A filter that the repository configured after the probe could run inside `status` (adversary BLOCKER on DL-142).
- Choice: the inventory runs no `git status` and no command that can run a repository-configured program. Each command has `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and the existing safe configuration (no hooks, no fsmonitor, no external diff). The scan (`crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/worktree_scan.rs`) counts as work: unmerged paths (`ls-files --unmerged`); index against HEAD (`diff-index --cached --raw --no-ext-diff --no-textconv`; an unborn branch, `rev-parse --verify HEAD` exit 1, counts every index entry); work tree against index (`ls-files --stage --debug` plus the station's own `lstat`: type, executable bit, size, mtime, ctime and inode; when the metadata differs or the entry is racily clean, the station hashes the file or link target itself as a Git blob and compares the object id, so a touched but unchanged file stays clean; a repository with a clean filter or eol conversion over-reports); submodules (HEAD against the gitlink, then the same scan, depth ≤ 3); untracked files (`ls-files --others --exclude-standard`). Ignored files are counted for information only. A sparse checkout, a too-deep submodule or any Git failure gives the verdict `unknown`. An unborn branch has `unpushed = 0` and no `compared_with`.
- Adversary: design rounds 1–2 (`/tmp/fd-briefs/night2-design-review-r1.md`, `-r2.md`): lazy fetch, staged changes and metadata-only checks were found in round 1 and fixed in round 2.
- Tests: `worktrees_tests.rs` (filters and fsmonitor never run; a staged edit, a staged deletion, an equal-size rewrite with the old mtime, a `chmod +x`, an unborn branch with a staged file and a submodule commit are work; a touched clean file is clean), parser and blob-hash unit tests.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-144 — Spawn parity: Git access check, branch choice, ticket callsigns

- Context: v1 checked Git access before a clone, let the owner choose the branch of a worktree, and named sessions after the ticket in the branch. v2 had none of these.
- Choice: link method `workspace.preflight { url }` (Drive, read-only, feature `workspace.preflight.v1`): the clone URL rules, then `git ls-remote --exit-code --heads` with the owner's credentials, `BatchMode=yes`, no prompt and a 20 s limit; the answer `{ ok, message, hint }` carries the scrubbed end of the Git error and a hint for the common causes (SSH key, https credentials, missing repository, unknown host, timeout). Deck route `POST /api/stations/{station}/workspace/preflight` (30 s wait). `WorkspaceRequest::Worktree { branch?, base? }`: an existing branch is checked out (a base is then refused); a new branch starts at `base` or HEAD (`rev-parse --verify --end-of-options <base>^{commit}`); names are checked by syntax in the decision and by Git in the effect. A ticket key `[A-Z][A-Z0-9]{1,9}-[0-9]{1,6}` in the chosen branch (or the current branch of a start in place) makes the callsign candidates `<animal>-<KEY>` first. `SessionCommand::Start.workspace` is boxed in Rust (clippy `large_enum_variant`); the wire shape is unchanged. The spawn form shows Branch and Base for a worktree start and a "Check access" button for a clone URL.
- Tests: station unit tests (branch rules, ticket keys, callsign order, preflight against local repositories, refused URLs, hint table), a deck relay test, board tests for the form pieces.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-145 — Conflict whisper (v1 parity, DL-306 follow-up)

- Context: the conflict radar (DL-306) shows the files that two sessions changed, but only on the board. The two agents do not know. v1 told both sessions with a "whisper".
- Choice: when an event records a file touch, and another session has a touch on the same `(group_key, file)` inside the conflict window (30 min, measured from the event time), the deck mails both sessions once. The work runs in the `apply_events` transaction, so a mail and its outbox item commit with the touch. The mail id is `conflict-` and the first 24 hex characters of the SHA-256 of `group_key|file|a|b|recipient`, where `a`, `b` and `recipient` are `station_id/session_id` and `a <= b` in string order. A stored mail with that id is the dedup record: one warning for each pair, file and recipient, also across a deck restart. The mail is a follow-up from `fleetdeck`, with a TTL of 2 hours, and names the other session (its title, else its id) and its station. A recipient whose summary state is `exited` or `lost`, or who has no stored summary, gets no mail; the other session still gets its mail. `deck.toml` `conflict_mail = false` turns it off (default true). No migration, no proto change, no board change.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/conflict_mail.rs` (two sessions get one mail each with the text and the stable ids; a third touch by either adds none; an exited session gets none and the live one does; `conflict_mail = false` sends none; a deck restart does not warn again); `crates/fleetdeck-core/src/config_tests.rs` (default and parse).
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-146 — Device link (owner login link for a new device)

- Context: a new phone or laptop needs a login. `fleetdeck open` makes a one-time link on the machine of the deck only, so the owner could not add a device from the board.
- Choice: `POST /api/auth/device-link` (no body; scope `admin`, mutating, CSRF as every owner POST). Only the owner's board login may call it: a token gets 403 `owner_only` (audited `auth.device_link` refused), whatever its scopes. One `create_login_link` for each request (single use, 5 minutes). The answer is `DeviceLink { links: [{ label, url }], expires_at, certificate_sha256, self_signed }`. `links` has one entry for each origin of the board (`Origins::list()`): the tailnet host names (`Tailnet <host>`), then the non-loopback addresses (`Network <ip>`), then loopback and `localhost` (`This computer`). Every URL is `<origin>/#login=<token>` and carries the same token; the first use makes the others fail. A limit of 10 links in a rolling 60 s for each deck process (`DeviceLinkLimiter`); the 11th gets 429 `too_many_requests`. The audit row `auth.device_link` (ok) has no target and holds no token and no URL; no log line names the URL. The deck `Board` state gets `certificate_sha256` and `self_signed` (from `BoardTls`). No board code in this change.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/board_device_link.rs` (the owner's token logs in once and a second use fails; the order, labels and the shared token; a token gets 403 `owner_only` and no link is made; no CSRF header is refused; the 11th call is 429 and makes no link); `http/guard.rs` (the order and labels with a LAN address); `http/auth_routes.rs` (the limiter window).
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-147 — Image paste on a station (`files.write_temp`)

- Context: the board could not hand a pasted screenshot to a driven agent that works on a station: the agent needs a file path on the station.
- Choice: link method `files.write_temp` (feature `files.write_temp.v1`, deck to station, scope `admin`, non-mutating on the link). Params `PasteImage { upload_id, ext, data_base64 }`, answer `PastedImage { path }`. The station (`workspace/paste.rs`) serializes all pastes with one mutex and checks: `upload_id` 8 to 64 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`; `ext` is `png`, `jpg`, `gif` or `webp`; the decoded size is 1 byte to 700 KiB (`too_large`); the magic bytes match `ext`. The file is `<station dir>/paste/<first 32 hex of sha256(upload_id)>.<ext>`, mode 0600, in a real directory (not a link) with mode 0700. The same id with the same bytes returns the same path; the same id with other bytes is `conflict`. Before a write the station deletes files older than 24 h, then the oldest files until 49 files and 24 MiB remain, then answers `no_space` when less than 512 MiB would stay free (`rustix::fs::statvfs`). The write is a `create_new` temp file (mode 0600), `sync_all` and `rename`. A station without a paste directory answers `unsupported`. The deck route `POST /api/stations/{station}/paste` (scope `admin`, owner only, CSRF, body limit 1 MiB, wait 15 s) maps `invalid_params` to 400, `too_large` to 413, `conflict` to 409 and `no_space` to 507. The audit row `files.paste` names the station and never the data. The station reads `base64` 0.22.1 (already in `Cargo.lock`; now a direct station dependency).
- `files.write_temp` is not a receipt mutation. A receipt mutation needs a `command_id`, a typed envelope and a stored receipt so that a retry does not repeat an effect. Here the effect is idempotent by construction: the file name is a function of the `upload_id`, so a retry (also after a lost answer) writes nothing new and returns the same path, and a stored file is not session state that a receipt must protect. A receipt would add an event-log row for a temporary file.
- Tests: `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/workspace/paste_tests.rs` (modes, retry, conflict, magic bytes, size limits, bad parameters, the file and byte quotas, the 24 h age, a link in place of the directory); `crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/board_paste.rs` (the owner pastes through a real station and the file exists; the error mapping; a token gets 403; no CSRF; a body above 1 MiB gets 413; a station without a directory).
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-148 — Setup command of a session start (v1 parity)

- Context: v1 could run an owner-given setup command in the workspace before the agent started (for example `npm ci`). v2 had the `allow_setup_cmd` switch in `station.toml` but no command. A setup command is shell code on the station, so who can send it, and what happens after a crash, need an exact rule.
- Adversary DESIGN REVIEW, part D4 (gpt-6.1-sol, reports `/tmp/fd-briefs/night2-design-review-r{1,2,3}.md`): round 1 — BLOCKER: the station opt-in let tokens and coordinators submit arbitrary shell commands; MAJOR: setup descendants can outlive a failure or a timeout. Round 2 — MAJOR: the launch could not give a crash-safe process-group record. Round 3 — MAJOR: the restart cleanup mistook an absent group leader for an absent setup group, so a setup process could survive a station crash.
- Choice:
  - Wire: `SessionCommand::Start.setup_cmd: Option<String>` (serde default, skipped when absent, optional in TypeScript) and the same field on the station's `EffectPayload::Start`; `StationInfo.allow_setup_cmd: Option<bool>` (optional, L16). `SessionCommand` does not refuse unknown fields, so an older station would ignore `setup_cmd` and run no setup: the deck therefore refuses to send one to a station whose `station.info` is not `allow_setup_cmd = true` (409 `setup_not_supported`, "this station does not run setup commands").
  - Who: only the owner's board login. A token gets 403 `owner_only` ("only the owner's board login starts a session with a setup command") before anything is stored or sent. A coordinator spawn always builds `setup_cmd: None`. Resume, import and fork never carry one.
  - Station decision (start only), in this order: policy off → `Forbidden` "setup commands are off on this station (allow_setup_cmd = false)"; command origin Coordinator → `Forbidden` "a coordinator start cannot carry a setup command"; empty after trim, more than 4096 characters, or a NUL → `InvalidCommand` "the setup command must be 1 to 4096 characters without NUL". The original text is stored.
  - Run (`workspace/setup.rs`): in the start effect, after the Git work and before the provider starts. `/bin/sh` runs a wrapper that waits for a `go` line on stdin and then runs the command with `exec /bin/sh -c`; the process has its own group, the cwd of the session, the scrubbed environment of the provider instance plus `FLEETDECK_SETUP_ID=<effect id>`, and no stdin. The row `setup_runs(effect_id, session_id, pgid, started_at)` (station migration V19) is committed before the `go` line, so the command cannot run without a row. A 10-minute limit. In every case the group gets TERM, then KILL, each with up to 5 s to disappear; the row is deleted only when the group is gone. Stdout and stderr go to one buffer (the last 64 KiB). A failure ends the start with `start_failed` and the text "the setup command failed (exit N)", "was killed by signal N", "timed out after 10 min" or "a setup process did not stop", followed by the output tail (URL credentials removed). The environment comes from `Policy.setup_envs` (one per provider instance, built with the same call as the adapter environment); an instance without one fails the start (fail closed).
  - Restart cleanup (`workspace/setup_cleanup.rs`, in `SessionManager::start_inner` after the revert recovery): for each remaining row, kill every process of the station's user whose environment holds `FLEETDECK_SETUP_ID=<effect id>` (Linux: `/proc/<pid>/environ`; macOS: `ps -E`), and the whole group when all its processes carry the marker (Linux only); scan again for up to 5 s. No match → delete the row. A match that stays → keep the row, log an error and append a `session.error` event with code `setup_process_left`. This closes the round 3 MAJOR: the cleanup no longer relies on the group leader.
  - Not kept: workspace custody. The setup command is the owner's own code, so a crash leaves at most the owner's own process. Documented limits: a process that clears its own environment is not found; on macOS the group check is skipped.
  - Config: `allow_setup_cmd` already existed in `station.toml` (default false); it now reaches the station policy and `station.info`.
- Tests: station — `manager/setup_tests.rs` (policy off, coordinator origin, empty / 4097 characters / NUL, the command runs before `session.started`, a failing command ends the start with its exit and output), `workspace/setup.rs` (cwd and marker, the row exists while the command runs and ends with it, exit and signal texts, the 64 KiB tail, a timeout with a background child leaves no process, a background child dies at the end, a missing cwd), `workspace/setup_cleanup.rs` (a marked process is killed and its row deleted, an unmarked process is left, a row without a process, the `/proc` stat and `ps` parsers); deck — `tests/board_setup_cmd.rs` (a token gets 403, no or old or off station info gets 409, the owner's command reaches an on station), `store/coordinator_tests.rs` (a coordinator spawn has no `setup_cmd`).
- PRE-PR fixes (adversary PRE-PR REVIEW, `/tmp/fd-briefs/adv-prepr-night2-r1.md`): two BLOCKERs. (B1) A setup such as `setsid sleep 60 &` leaves a marked child in another process group; the group check saw the group gone, the row was deleted and the child survived. (B2) The awaited setup blocked the one effect worker, so every other session's effects waited for up to 10 minutes, and the setup session could not be stopped.
  - B1: `setup::run` now calls `setup_cleanup::kill_marked(effect_id, wait)` after the group stop in every case (success, failure, timeout, cancel). It SIGKILLs every own-uid process with the marker, in any group, and scans again until none is left or 5 s pass; the restart cleanup uses the same function. A process that is left keeps the row and fails the run with "a setup process did not stop". The row is deleted only when the group is gone and no marked process is left.
  - B2: new `Dispatch::Deferred` ("the target settles the effect itself later"); the worker does not settle it, so the effect stays `claimed` (the restart rule for claimed effects applies). A start with a setup command does its Git work, then registers a task per session in `SessionTarget` (`SetupTasks`: a cancel sender and the join handle) and returns `Deferred`. The task runs the setup, then the provider start, then `store.settle` with the fence of the claim (an error such as `Fenced` is logged and ignored). A failure ends the start with `start_failed` and settles `NotDispatched("setup_failed")`; a cancel does the same with the text "the session was stopped during its setup command" and `NotDispatched("setup_stopped")`. `SessionTarget::shared()` builds the `Arc` with a `Weak` to itself, which the task needs.
  - Stop: the Stop decision skips the live-process check when a `setup_runs` row exists for the session, and the Stop effect is stored without a process incarnation. Its dispatch sends the cancel to the task and returns `Confirmed`; the task ends the session. A stop before the row exists (during the Git work) is rejected as before ("the session has no running provider process yet"). A task that left its setup takes itself out of the registry under the same lock that a stop uses, so a stop is either seen by the task or finds no task (then `NotDispatched("no_process")`).
  - Shutdown: `SessionTarget::stop_all` first cancels every setup task and waits for them for up to 15 s.
  - Round 2 (adversary RE-REVIEW, `/tmp/fd-briefs/adv-prepr-night2-r2.md`, one BLOCKER: the deferred start left the registry before the provider was registered, so a queued Stop or a shutdown could miss it): the task code moved to `manager/setup_task.rs`. The task now stays in `SetupTasks` through the setup, the provider start and the settle, and leaves last. A cancel records its mode (Kill replaces Graceful, Graceful never replaces Kill; `stop_all(how)` records its `how` the same way). At its end the task stops the new session with that mode when a cancel came after the setup ended. The check "a stop is pending" before the provider start runs under the lock that a stop takes. A Stop effect with no incarnation and no task stops the process that is live now (`operate` skips the incarnation check for exactly this case). Shutdown cancels the entries without removing them and awaits the handles for up to 15 s. Tests: a stop during the provider start, a stop dispatched after the start, a shutdown during the provider start (a `#[cfg(test)]` hook holds the task before the provider start). The setup tests use random effect ids, because the effect id is the process marker and the deterministic ids of parallel test processes collide.
  - Round 3 (adversary RE-REVIEW, `/tmp/fd-briefs/adv-prepr-night2-r3.md`, one BLOCKER: a cancel still let the first prompt go, and the shutdown wait of 15 s was shorter than the 30 s Codex handshake): the cancel receiver goes into `start` and `start_durable`. A cancel before the provider spawn spawns nothing and settles `NotDispatched("setup_stopped")`; a cancel before the first input sends no input (the input gets `first_input_not_sent` evidence) and the start completes, then the task stops the provider itself and waits for the end of that stop (no spawned stop). Shutdown waits up to 60 s for each task, then aborts it; a direct provider session dies with its dropped handle, and the shutdown also stops the session that the aborted task registered (a shim session outlives the station by design, ADR-005, and the next start restores it). Tests: a stop before the spawn leaves no provider pid in the sim stdin log; a stop after the spawn sends no first prompt (`hello` is absent from the log) and ends the session; a shutdown during the held start returns only when the provider process is gone; a start without a stop still sends the first prompt once.
  - Round 4 (adversary RE-REVIEW, `/tmp/fd-briefs/adv-prepr-night2-r4.md`, two BLOCKERs): (1) the cancel check came before the fence and the checkpoint work, which can wait for long. Both start paths now check again after `fence()` and `begin_input()`, right before the delivery with no await between; a cancel there abandons the prepared checkpoint (`input_not_sent`) and records `first_input_failed(NotDispatched("setup_stopped"))`. (2) The end of the task read the recorded stop and removed the entry under two locks, so a Stop could be confirmed and then lost. `finish_or_take_stop` now decides under one lock: with a recorded stop it takes the mode out and keeps the entry as `stopping`, otherwise it removes the entry. The task loops `while let Some(how) = finish_or_take_stop() { stop_started(how).await }`. A Stop during `stopping` records a mode only when it is stronger (Kill over Graceful) and is `Confirmed`. Tests (hooks in one `Hooks` struct): a cancel held after the checkpoint work sends no prompt (the test fails without the final check); a Stop between the provider registration and the task end stops the provider.
  - Tests: `workspace/setup.rs` (a detached `setsid` child is killed and the row ends; a cancel stops the run and its children), `manager/setup_tests.rs` (session A with `sleep 30` does not block session B, whose first prompt finishes; a stop of A ends it with the stopped text, its row is gone and no marked process is left), `worker_tests.rs` (a `Deferred` effect is not settled).

## DL-149 — Archived sessions on the deck (sync across devices)

- Context: the board's cleared (archived) sessions lived in per-browser `localStorage` (`fd-archived`, DL-141), so each device showed another board.
- Choice: the deck stores the set. Migration V12 adds `archived_sessions(station_id, session_id, archived, updated_at, PRIMARY KEY(station_id, session_id))` and `archive_mutations(mutation_id PRIMARY KEY, applied_at)`; the mutation ids are never pruned. `GET /api/archive` (scope `read`) answers `ArchiveList { archived: [{station_id, session_id}] }`, ordered by station and session. `POST /api/archive` (scope `drive`, mutating: CSRF for a cookie login) takes `ArchiveChange { mutation_id, changes: [{station_id, session_id, archived}] }` and answers the list. `mutation_id` is 8 to 128 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]`; 1 to 500 changes; each id is 1 to 256 characters; a bad body is 400 `invalid_body`. A bearer token with `drive` may call it, because a change only hides cards. A new `mutation_id` upserts every item and records the id in one transaction; a known id changes nothing and returns the current list, so a lost-response retry cannot undo a newer action of another device. The order between devices is the order in which the deck applies the mutations. The audit row is `archive.change` with the detail `<n> changes` (`<n> changes, replayed` for a known id), and `Failed` with `invalid_body` for a bad body.
- Board: `web/src/ui/archive.ts` keeps the same API as the old set (`has`, `toggle`, `add`, `remove`, `use`) and adds `attach(api)`. The first paint uses the cache `fd-archived-cache`; every deck answer replaces the set and the cache. `attach` loads the list at once, on window focus and every 60 s. A change is optimistic, then one `POST` with a new `mutation_id`; a failed `POST` reloads the list (the change is lost) and logs a warning. The old key `fd-archived` moves to the deck at the first successful load: the refs that the deck does not list are posted in one change with the id `migrate-<device id>-<first 16 hex of the SHA-256 of the sorted old refs>` (the device id is a random id in `fd-device-id`). The key is removed only after the post succeeded; after a failure the next load sends the same id. The rule "only ended or lost sessions hide" does not change.
- Adversary design review (gpt-6.1-sol, `/tmp/fd-briefs/night2-design*.md`): round 1 MAJOR (a retry of an old mutation can undo a newer action of another device) fixed with the stable `mutation_id`; round 2 MAJOR (a 7-day pruning of the ids reopens the same defect for a migration retry) fixed by never pruning the ids; round 3 found no open blocker or major for D6.
- Open items: a migration or a call with more than 500 refs, or with a ref part longer than 256 characters, is refused by the deck (400), so the migration then fails and retries at each load; the board does not split a change.
- Tests: `store/archive.rs` (a new id applies and the list is ordered; the same id again changes nothing; an archive then an unarchive with two ids; a stale retry stays unarchived); `fleetdeck-proto` `board.rs` (the validation rules, strict bodies); `crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/board_archive.rs` (GET and POST, a stale resend, the audit details, a token with `read` only gets 403, a bad body is 400, CSRF is needed for a cookie login); `web/src/ui/archive.test.ts` (an optimistic toggle then the deck list wins, a failed post reloads, the migration posts the missing refs once with a stable id and clears `fd-archived` only after success, a second attach after a failed migration reuses the id).
- - PRE-PR review after round 3 (the owner asked to finish the setup blocker; rounds 4 and 5, `/tmp/fd-briefs/adv-prepr-night2-r{4,5}.md`): round 4 found a cancel check before the fence and checkpoint waits and a two-lock race at the task end; both are fixed (a final check right before the first-input delivery; `finish_or_take_stop` under one lock). Round 5 reports that a stop which arrives while the first prompt is already inside the provider's delivery call (its core lock, durable frame admission) still lets that prompt start. Not followed: the station stops the session right after the delivery, as a stop of any running session does; a stop never takes back input that is already in transit. Known limit, recorded here.
Owner review: pending.

## DL-150 — Worktree trash: move, restore, delete for good, release (v1 "Remove worktree" without data loss)

- Context: v1 could remove a worktree. A direct delete loses unsaved work, and a crash between two file steps can leave Git and the disk in different states. v2 needs the same action with a way back, and with a delete that only the owner can start, only on a folder that the owner already moved to the trash.
- Choice:
  - Wire: `SessionCommand::{WorktreeTrash { session_id }, WorktreeRestore { session_id }, WorktreePurge { session_id, confirm_name }, WorktreeRelease { session_id }}`, link methods `session.worktree_trash|restore|purge|release` (deck to station, scope `admin`, mutating, feature `workspace.trash.v1`). They are not in the deck's generic command table: only the owner routes `POST /api/stations/{station}/worktrees/{trash|restore|purge|release}` (body `{ session_id, command_id, confirm_name? }`, `deny_unknown_fields`, owner login only, CSRF, audited `worktree.trash|restore|purge|release` with the target `<station>/<session>`) send them, with origin Board. The station accepts them only with origin Board (else `forbidden` "only the owner's board changes worktrees"); the coordinator never builds them. The answer of a route is the command, as the generic command route answers; the receipt only tells that the station admitted it.
  - State: station migration V20 `worktree_ops(op_id = the trash command id, session_id, main, admin_dir, original_path, trash_path, dev, ino, branch, state, phase, message, trashed_at, updated_at)` and the partial unique index `worktree_ops_open` (one open row per session; open = not `restored`, `purged`, `released`). States: `trashing`, `trashed`, `restoring`, `restored`, `purging`, `purged`, `failed` (with `phase` `trash`, `restore` or `purge` and a `message`), `released`.
  - Custody hold: an open row holds the original folder and the trash folder and everything below them. `session.start` (also a worktree or clone start whose path overlaps), `session.resume`, `session.import`, the auto-continue recheck, `shell.open` and `shell.handover` refuse with `invalid_state` "the worktree is in the trash or being moved; restore it first". The checks come before the existence checks, because the original folder is missing while it is held.
  - Trash decision (one transaction, no Git command): the board origin; the session exists with a worktree, has ended (`exited` or `lost`), has no run, turn, terminal hold, or queued or claimed effect; no open row or revert over the path; no other live or held session overlaps; no live shell has a start folder that overlaps (the manager holds the station's `Shells`, set by the link node; the decision reads them in memory); the path is inside the allowed roots, a folder and not a symbolic link, and has a `.git` file (not the main checkout). It inserts the row in `trashing` (`dev` and `ino` from `symlink_metadata`, `trash_path = <parent>/.fleetdeck-trash/<name>--<first 12 characters of the command id, lower case>`) and queues one `EffectPayload::Worktree { op_id, action }`.
  - Effects (`workspace/trash.rs`; the effect needs no provider process and the manager target routes it before the target lookup): trash reads the Git facts once (common dir, `git worktree list --porcelain`: listed, not locked, not the main checkout; the `.git` file names an admin dir inside `<common dir>/worktrees/`) and stores `main`, `admin_dir` and `branch`; creates the trash folder (mode 0700, a real folder); renames by identity (a folder with another identity, or both or neither present, fails the row with `phase trash` and moves nothing; EXDEV is an error too); runs `git worktree repair -- <trash_path>`. Restore reconciles by identity (repair only, or rename back and then repair). Purge deletes the trash folder only when its `dev` and `ino` are the recorded ones, then the admin dir only when it is inside the common dir and its `gitdir` file names the trash folder. Branches are never deleted. A restore is accepted for `trashed` and for `failed` with phase `trash` or `restore`; a purge for `trashed` and `failed` with phase `purge`, with `confirm_name` equal to the trash folder name (else `invalid_command` "type the folder name to confirm"); a release only for `failed`. Trash and purge check the open shells again in the effect.
  - Claim guard: the first step of every run reads the row in a transaction and goes on only when its state is the one that its action expects (`trashing`, `restoring`, `purging`); else the effect ends `NotDispatched("superseded")` and changes nothing. Every later state change of a run is a compare-and-set on that state. Every settled run ends `Confirmed`, also when it set the row to `failed`; the effect adds no session event (`transition_events` is empty for a worktree effect), because the row and the inventory carry the outcome.
  - Restart: the existing rule retires a queued effect and marks a claim unknown; `SessionManager::start` then queues one new effect for each row left in `trashing`, `restoring` or `purging` (`Store::requeue_worktree_ops`).
  - Inventory: `WorktreeInfo.trash: Option<WorktreeTrash { state, phase, message, trash_path, trashed_at }>` for each open row; a session whose row is `trashing`, `trashed`, `restoring` or `purging` gets the new verdict `trashed` and is not scanned; a `failed` row keeps the normal scan and adds the facts; a `purged` or `released` row hides nothing. The doc comments of `dirty` and `ignored` no longer name `git status`.
- Adversary record (DESIGN REVIEW of part D2, gpt-6.1-sol, reports `/tmp/fd-briefs/night2-design-review-r{1,2,3}.md`): round 1 — findings on the delete (a delete outside the custody hold, a replaced folder), on stranded failed rows, and on a delegate that does not exist (a station pairs with one deck, the owner's); round 2 — BLOCKER 1 and 2 (the purge moved the folder out of custody, and a retry could delete a replacement folder) fixed in the r3 design by deleting in place under the hold and checking the recorded identity before every delete; MAJOR (a `failed` state with no allowed action) fixed by phase-aware restore and purge and by release. Round 3 — one BLOCKER: a release ended the custody without cancelling a queued purge, so a shell could open in the trash folder, write files, and the purge would then delete them (the folder still has the recorded identity). The closure in this build has two parts. The claim guard stops any stale effect: the purge effect reads the row first and goes on only in `purging`, so a row that was forced to `failed` and released makes it end `superseded`. And a release is accepted only for a `failed` row, and a `failed` row never has a queued or claimed effect (a run sets `failed` as its last step, so its own effect is already past all file work, and the worker settles it at once; a restore or purge from `failed` moves the row to a new state in the same transaction that queues its effect). The design review stopped at its 3-round limit with this BLOCKER open on paper: the owner decides on the merge.
- PRE-PR review round 1 (`/tmp/fd-briefs/adv-prepr-trash-r1.md`): two BLOCKERs, both fixed. (1) The identity check ran before an `await`, so a folder replaced in between could be deleted or moved: each identity check now sits in the same blocking call as its `rename` or `remove_dir_all` (`move_checked`, `delete_checked`, `delete_admin_checked`), and the move also needs the destination to be absent in that call. (2) A queued or claimed shell open was not part of the custody: the trash and purge decisions and effect step 0 now refuse while a queued or claimed shell effect has an overlapping start folder ("a terminal is opening in this worktree; wait"), and the shell effect that opens a PTY checks the custody rows again right before it spawns and fails with the custody text.
- Open items: the Git `worktree repair` step needs Git 2.30 or newer; the trash folder must be on the same disk as the worktree (EXDEV fails the row, nothing moves).
- Tests: station — `workspace/trash_tests.rs` (trash moves the folder under `.fleetdeck-trash` and Git lists the new path; restore; a purge with a wrong name is refused and a right one deletes the folder and the admin dir and keeps the branch; start, resume, shell open and handover into the original or the trash path refused while a row is open; a live session in the folder, a non-ended session, no worktree, a non-board origin and the main checkout refuse a trash; a folder replaced after the claim is neither deleted nor moved (`workspace/trash_race_tests.rs`), a pending shell open refuses a trash and a purge, a queued shell open fails when a row holds its folder; a changed folder identity fails the row with `phase trash` and moves nothing; release only from `failed`; the r3 blocker case, a queued purge after a release ends `superseded` and deletes nothing; a replaced trash folder is not deleted; a restart re-run after a crash between the rename and the repair; a restore of a trash that failed before Git was read; an open shell refuses the decision and fails the effect; the inventory shows `trashed` with the trash facts), `manager/trash_wired_tests.rs` (the manager and the worker end to end, origins, the restart queue), `store/schema.rs` (V20 index); deck — `crates/fleetdeck-deck/tests/board_worktree_trash.rs` (the owner gets the command and the whole cycle runs over a real link; a wrong purge name; every call audited; a token gets 403 `owner_only` and nothing is sent; CSRF is needed; strict body); link `tests/method_table.rs` (47 rows); proto `adr_revision2.rs`, `mutation_tests.rs`.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-151 — Session models: the provider reports the models it offers (`session.models`)

- Context: the owner asked for a model selector on the board. The board needs the list of models that a session can switch to, from the provider harness.
- Choice:
  - Event: `Event::SessionModels { models: Vec<ModelChoice> }`, kind `session.models`. `ModelChoice { value, label, description? }`: `value` is what `set_model` takes. The fold ignores the event (as `session.slash_commands` and `session.notice`). The TypeScript binding `ModelChoice` is generated.
  - Claude: each model of the `initialize` answer maps to `{ value, label: displayName (else value), description }` in the CLI order; an entry without `value` is dropped; an empty list emits nothing. The event follows `session.started` (after the `v1_plugin` notice, before `session.slash_commands`) when the answer came before the first `init` (the core keeps the list in `pending_models`); an answer after the first `init` emits it at once. The flat list that checks `set_model` is unchanged.
  - Codex: after the `thread/start`, `resume` or `fork` reply, the core sends one `model/list` with `{}` (`HostRequest::ModelList { pages, models }`). It keeps entries with `hidden != true` as `{ value: model (else id), label: displayName (else value), description }`, follows `nextCursor` for at most 4 more pages, and emits one `session.models` with all pages. A failed or empty `model/list` emits nothing and no error.
  - Replays: the recorded Codex fixtures hold no `model/list` and the host ids are part of the recording, so `CoreConfig.request_models` (false in every replay and checkpoint placeholder) and `CodexAdapterConfig.request_models` (true from `new`) switch the request. The adapter passes the flag to the core and to the durable launch config. The sim Codex answers `model/list` with one model. The three Claude board fixtures (`web/src/testing/fixtures/claude-*.events.json`) are regenerated, because the recorded `initialize` answers now yield `session.models`.
- Tests: proto (`event_tests.rs` round trip and a decode without `description`, `fold_tests.rs` the summary does not change); Claude (`claude/tests.rs`: answer before `init`, answer after `init`, empty or invalid lists); Codex (`codex/tests.rs`: the request after the thread opens, two pages with a hidden model and the id fallbacks, the 4-page limit, an error and an empty list; `tests/codex_driver_sim.rs`: the event arrives after `session.started`).
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-152 — GitHub, GitLab, Jira and Linear integration is in scope (owner decision, 2026-10-13)

- Context: the v1 plan (`docs/v1/p3-issue-pr-spawning.md` on `fd/typescript-migration`) cut Jira and Linear because a tracker token is a stored credential, and `docs/v2/FLEETDECK-V2.md` (first version) repeated that cut. The owner wants all four systems in the board.
- Choice: GitHub, GitLab, Jira and Linear are in scope (`FLEETDECK-V2.md` R3). The credential rule changes, but it keeps its intent. The station does all forge and tracker I/O with its own user's credentials. GitHub and GitLab go through the user's `gh` and `glab`. Jira (Cloud REST v3) and Linear (GraphQL) use a token that the station reads from its user's environment (`token_env`) or a mode-0600 file (`token_file`) that `station.toml` names. The token never reaches the deck, the link, a read model, an argv, a log or the board. Every issue, PR and tracker text is fenced untrusted data. A spawn from it is supervised, with no path to a bypass mode.
- Not decided yet:
  - the order of the four systems;
  - the write verbs (create a PR, comment, move an issue) and their milestone;
  - the exact `station.toml` keys.
  These come with the design of M5. That design is high risk (credentials, untrusted text that starts sessions), so it gets an adversary DESIGN REVIEW before it is frozen.
- Invalidates: the "Jira and Linear: cut" line of `FLEETDECK-V2.md` §4, which is corrected in the same commit.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-153 — Model help through the owner's subscription (owner decision, 2026-10-13)

- Context: v1 and v2 kept "no model calls in the core" (the deck and the station never call an LLM). The owner now allows Fleet Deck to use the Claude Code or Codex subscription to generate useful text, with few tokens, using small models: Haiku 5.5 and Luna.
- Choice: rule 3 of `FLEETDECK-V2.md` changes from "no model calls" to "model help through the owner's subscription, small and optional". No crate calls a model API directly; the test `no_crate_calls_a_model_api_directly` stays. A station runs one short headless call of the user's own provider CLI with the smallest fitting model. Each call is opt-in per feature, has a token budget and a daily cap, is cached by its input hash, and shows its use in the usage meters. It is off the critical path, so the feature works without it, and the board labels its text "generated". Generated text never approves, picks a winner, chooses a mode or routes work. The candidate uses are in `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R10.
- Not decided yet:
  - the exact CLI invocation and model ids per provider ("Luna" is the owner's name for the small Codex model);
  - the default budgets;
  - which features turn it on by default.
  These come with the M5.2 design.
- Invalidates: the "LLM-written titles: not built" line and the old rule 3 of `FLEETDECK-V2.md`, which are corrected in the same commit. `PLAN.md` doctrine text that says "no model calls" means "no direct model API calls" from now on.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-154 — A shared fleet: other people see, follow and steer sessions (owner decision, 2026-10-13)

- Context: v2 has one human principal, the owner, plus scoped bearer tokens (DL-138). The owner wants team work: share a URI, and the people with that invite see the session, follow it, steer it and work on the same thing.
- Choice: collaboration is in scope (`FLEETDECK-V2.md` rule 11 and R11).
  - The owner shares a session, a space (a named set of sessions) or the board, with a role: viewer, collaborator or approver. Each share has an expiry, and the owner can revoke it.
  - An invite URI (`#join=<token>`, single-use or N uses) makes a member login.
  - Members are new principals. Every input, approval and action carries its author, and the audit log records it.
  - Owner-only surfaces stay owner-only: shells and the TUI, the setup command, worktree delete, pairing, device links, grants, credentials, and the creation of shares. No member can raise the permission mode that the owner set.
  - The invite says in plain words that steering makes the agent run commands on the owner's machine.
- Not decided yet: the open owner questions (a)–(d) of R11, and the member identity beyond local members.
- Review: this changes the threat model (new principals, remote humans on a deck that runs code). It needs an adversary DESIGN REVIEW before freeze and a PRE-PR review before merge.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-155 — Artifacts are first-class: native viewing and sharing (owner decision, 2026-10-13)

- Context: the owner works with HTML artifacts a lot (for example the `/explain-html` pages) and wants to see them inside Fleet Deck and to share them with anyone they choose.
- Choice: artifacts are in scope (`FLEETDECK-V2.md` rule 12 and R12).
  - An artifact is an immutable, versioned file (HTML, Markdown, image, SVG or PDF) from a session or a person.
  - It is made by a `publish_artifact` MCP tool for every session, by a suggestion when a session writes a page that is meant to be opened, or by "Publish" in the Files panel.
  - The station stores the versions, and the deck keeps a copy of shared versions.
  - The board shows an Artifacts tab per session, a global page, and chat chips.
  - HTML renders only in a sandboxed iframe with an opaque origin, under a strict CSP and a CDN allowlist.
  - Sharing: revocable share links with an expiry, downloads, and the shares of R11.
- Not decided yet: the open owner questions (e)–(g) of R12.
- Review: high risk (untrusted HTML and JavaScript next to the board, links to outsiders). It needs an adversary DESIGN REVIEW before freeze.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-156 — Owner answers: integration projects, PR/MR views, sharing and artifacts (2026-10-13)

- Integrations (adds to DL-152; `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R3):
  - A Fleet Deck project is a station repository with bindings to its forge (GitHub or GitLab) and to Jira or Linear projects. The references are Jira Cloud REST API v3 and the Linear GraphQL API.
  - People browse issues with rich previews and start one or a batch of sessions from tickets. A session is prefixed with its ticket id in its title, callsign and branch, and it keeps an `issue` link.
  - The PR/MR view shows "review requested from you", "yours", "pending" and "merged or closed". It links PRs to sessions by branch or ticket key, and it offers "Start review session" in one click.
- Sharing (DL-154; R11):
  - (a) the invite level sets the role, and only an approver answers tool approvals;
  - (b) members are local accounts;
  - (c) the member file browser shows only the session's workspace, and the station hides sensitive files (a default deny list for `.env*`, keys and credential files, which the owner extends per project) from the browser, search, reads and diffs;
  - (d) a shared session can run in mob or pairing mode, with one driver and "request to drive".
- Artifacts (DL-155; R12):
  - (e) a public share link may reach people outside the team;
  - (f) there is no CDN allowlist: an HTML artifact may load anything, the team owns that risk, and the opaque-origin sandbox stays only to protect the board's login and API;
  - (g) there is no size cap; the board shows the total size, and an HTML page is published with the local files that it names.
- Review: the high-risk parts (member principals, the file deny list, untrusted text from trackers, the HTML sandbox, public links) get an adversary DESIGN REVIEW before freeze.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-157 — Owner answers: all of the roadmap, cost, automations, and how to watch many sessions (2026-10-13)

- All of the work in `FLEETDECK-V2.md` is planned, including the R3 writes. The milestones set only the order of the work, and nothing is cut.
- Watching many sessions: no terminal wall. Explore another form (compact live chat tiles, a focus mode, split panes) with mockups first (R6.2).
- Codex cost from a versioned local price table, shown as "inferred" (R4.6).
- Automations: all kinds (R5).
- Open: when to cut the 2.0.0 release, early (hardening first) or late (after all features). The architect recommends early.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-158 — Local CI, a provider version check, the owner's security review, and the release on the owner's word (2026-10-13)

- CI: GitHub Actions is expensive, so `.github/workflows/v2.yml` runs only on demand (`workflow_dispatch`). The owner runs `make check` on their own machines, also on macOS before a release. Every worker and the architect still run `make check` (or its parts) locally before a merge.
- Minimum provider version (the owner left it to the architect):
  - each adapter names the oldest CLI version that its fixtures and live smoke cover;
  - an older CLI gets a warning (station info, the spawn form, `fleetdeck doctor`), not a refusal;
  - only a version that is known to break the protocol is refused (a deny list);
  - a local `make` target diffs a newer Codex app-server schema against the pinned one.
- Security review: the owner does one at the end. The architect keeps a checklist of the new surfaces for it. The per-feature adversary reviews stay.
- Release: the owner cuts 2.0.0 when they decide. Until then, all work is on the `v2` branch.
- Invalidates: the GATE-M1 G13 note that CI runs on every push (the workflow header is updated), and the "macOS CI lane" and "security delta" rows of `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R8 and R9, which are corrected in the same commit.
- Owner review: decided by the owner.

## DL-163 — Honest feedback: spawn failures, plan atomicity, refused controls, overlays, palette start row (R7, R6.3)

- Context: a clone, worktree or setup failure ended the session with a `start_failed` error that only the session page showed (R7.1). The plan library marked a plan `executed` in a second call (R7.2). The refused-control rule and the overlay order were not written, and the hotkeys checked only the palette and the help (R7.3, R7.4).
- Choice:
  - R7.1: a session with status `ended` and `last_error.code = start_failed` gets an error banner in the app frame: "Session <title> did not start: <first line>", with Open and Dismiss. One banner for each session (by station and session id). A failure older than 10 minutes is not announced (a page that opens later); the card shows "Failed to start: <first line>" always. The text is shown as the station sent it.
  - R7.2: the board marks the plan `executed` only after the spawn start returned ok (`markOnStart`); a failed start keeps it `proposed` and the spawn form shows the error. A proposed plan whose proposer session is still live shows "The proposer session <callsign> is still running; this starts a new session." The assign path marks after a successful mail only (as before).
  - R7.3, R7.4: the rules are in `UI-DESIGN.md` §3.5. One hook `useOverlay()` marks an overlay as open; `useGlobalKeys` ignores every key but `Mod+K` and `Esc` while one is open. The drawer, the help, the palette and the two dialogs call it.
  - R6.3: when no session matches, the palette shows "Start a session: “<query>”", which opens the spawn form with the query as the first prompt.
- Tests: `web/src/app/feedback.test.tsx` (banner and card text, dedupe and window, plan mark on start, proposer note, palette row, key gating, the source scan for `useOverlay`).

## DL-161 — Usage and accounts: tightest window, 80% chip, limits in the picker, quota tie-break, inferred Codex cost (R4)

- Context: `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R4 asks for limits that govern a busy day. The data was on the board, but the meters were sorted by key, the 80% color lived only in the Usage tab, and Codex reported tokens only.
- Choice:
  - R4.1: the meters sort by use (highest first, unknown last), each with "resets in 2 h 10 min" from `resets_at` (and "resets at 14:30" local time when more than a day away, "reset due" when past). The panel re-renders every 30 s (`useNow`).
  - R4.2: a `fd-badge` chip "Limit 85% · five_hour" (`data-tone="act"` from 80%, "Limit reached · scope" in `hazard` at 100%) on the fleet home header (tightest window of the whole fleet) and in the session header badges (windows that the session itself reports).
  - R4.3: the spawn form's provider options show the tightest limit of each instance of the chosen station ("claude · claude · 2.1.999 · 42% of five_hour, resets in 1 h"). "(most room)" marks the unique instance with the lowest use, when at least two instances report a limit.
  - R4.4: `pick_session` (`assign auto`) sorts by (status rank, buried mail, quota, recency, session id), so among equal fits the provider instance with the lowest tightest `used_percent` wins before recency (latest window per scope over the sessions of the same station and instance; unknown = 50).
  - R4.5 (research, no code): a Claude CLI session sends `rate_limit_event` mostly near a limit, so Claude meters are often empty. The candidates are the `/usage` slash command (human-readable text, no stable schema) and the OAuth usage endpoint of the CLI (private API, can change). Neither is wired yet. Next step: record a `/usage` reply with `claude -p` and decide whether to parse it.
  - R4.6: `crates/fleetdeck-providers/src/codex/prices.rs` holds a versioned price table (`PRICE_TABLE_VERSION`, USD per Mtok: input, cached input, output). The Codex adapter prices each `thread/tokenUsage/updated` (cached tokens are part of the input tokens) and sets `Usage.cost_inferred = true`. A model that is not in the table, or a missing token count, gives `cost_usd = None`. The price of `gpt-5.6-luna` (the model of the recorded fixtures) is a placeholder equal to gpt-5.
