The fold keeps `cost_inferred` when any added part is inferred. The board shows "≈ $1.20 inferred" for a session and "≈ $x" for a fleet total with an inferred part. The Codex capability `usage.cost` stays false (the provider reports no cost).
- Tests: `web/src/board/usage.test.tsx`, `web/src/spawn/spawn.test.tsx`, `web/src/session/controls.test.tsx` (meters, countdown, chip, picker, inferred cost); `routing_tests.rs` (quota tie-break); `prices.rs` (price table); `fold_tests.rs` (inferred flag); the Codex board fixtures carry the inferred cost.

## DL-159 — Harvest: the session base, the diff since spawn, line notes, and checkpoint ref cleanup (R1.1, R1.2, R1.3, R1.5)

- Context: the owner wants to review the work of a session as one change since it started, to leave notes on lines, and to find no hidden refs after a worktree is deleted for good. `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R1 sets the scope; R1.4 ("compare the winner") is a later wave.
- Choice:
  - R1.1 base: a new event `session.base { git_ref?, oid, inferred }`, emitted by the station (not the provider) after the start succeeded, and folded into `SessionSummary.base`. Worktree start: the base name, else the existing branch, else the `HEAD` of the repository (with its branch), read before the worktree is made; a repeated effect (the folder exists) records nothing. Clone: the `HEAD` of the clone. Start in place: the `HEAD` of the working directory. Import and resume of a session without a base: `git merge-base HEAD origin/HEAD` (`git_ref` `origin/HEAD`), else `HEAD`; `inferred` is true. A directory outside Git, an unborn `HEAD` or a failed `git` call gives no base and no event.
  - R1.2 diff: link method `files.session_diff { session_id, max_bytes? }` (scope `read`, feature `files.session_diff.v1`) and route `GET /api/stations/{station}/sessions/{session}/diff` (scope `read`). The answer is `SessionDiff { session_id, base_oid, summary, patch, truncated }`: the parts of `TurnDiff` against the base commit (a new type, because `TurnDiff` has a turn id and a completion commit). Tracked content comes from the checkpoint snapshot (private index, no filter, no external diff), `git diff-tree -p -r -M` against the base commit, private paths left out. Each untracked file that is not ignored is read through the file service (no symlink followed) and shown as a new file; a binary or a large file (over 256 KiB) is a header line only and sets `truncated`; at most 200 untracked files. A cut tracked patch skips the untracked files. Errors: `no_base` (409 on the board; also a base commit that the repository lost), `no_diff` (not a Git worktree, or the tracked content is out of the snapshot scope), `unknown_session`, `outside_roots`, `missing_workspace`, `timeout`.
  - R1.3 notes: a click on a line number of the diff (both views) opens a one-line editor under the line. Notes are per session, in memory and in `localStorage` (`fd-notes:<station>/<session>`), listed in a "Notes (n)" bar. "Send notes" sends ONE follow-up through the existing follow-up action: `Review notes:` and one line `- <file> L<line>: <note>` for each note that was not sent yet. After a success the notes stay, marked "sent", until "Clear".
  - Board: the Changes tab has the toggle "Since spawn | By turn" (default "Since spawn" when the summary has a base). The session card shows `+a −d` from the loaded session diff only; nothing polls for it. The tab loads the diff when it opens, when a turn ends, and on "Refresh".
  - R1.5: a purge of a worktree (DL-150) deletes the checkpoint refs of the session (`refs/fleetdeck/checkpoints/<incarnation>/<worktree id>/<session>/...`) with `git update-ref -d`, one ref at a time, found by the session segment (the worktree id changes when the folder moves into the trash). A failure is logged and does not fail the purge: the folder is gone already. Untracked files in checkpoints stay out of scope (a checkpoint holds tracked content only).
- Tests: `proto` (event round trip, fold of `session.base`), `workspace::base_tests` (all start forms with real Git), `workspace::session_diff_tests` (tracked, committed, untracked, binary, ignored, private, a link, the byte limit, no base), `workspace::checkpoint_refs_tests` and the purge test in `workspace::trash_tests` (only the purged session's refs go), the deck test `board_files` (route, `read` scope), the method-table agreement test (ADR-004), web tests for `notes.ts`, `Diff` with notes, the Changes tab and the card total.

## DL-162 — Platform, quality and documents: version check, layering, test floors, load bars, release gate (FLEETDECK-V2 R8, R9)

- Context: DL-158 chose a warning for an older provider CLI and a deny list for a version that breaks the protocol. R8 and R9 of `FLEETDECK-V2.md` listed the platform and quality guards and the documents that were missing.
- Choice:
  - Provider version check (R8.2): each adapter has `TESTED_MIN_VERSION` (Claude `2.1.289`, the `cli_version` of every recorded fixture; Codex `0.160.0`) and `KNOWN_BROKEN: &[&str]` (empty). `fleetdeck-providers::version::version_status(kind, version)` compares numeric parts; a suffix after `-`, `+` or a space is ignored; no version, no minimum or an unparsable version is `unknown`. `StationProvider.version_status` (`ok`, `older_than_tested`, `broken`, `unknown`; serde default `unknown`, so an older station still decodes) is the new wire field; the Rust type is `fleetdeck_proto::link::VersionStatus` and `make ts-types` generated its binding. `SessionManager::providers()` marks a `broken` instance on the target; `SessionTarget::start` then ends the start with `start_failed` and the text "this <provider> version is known to break the protocol" and returns `NotDispatched("provider_broken")`, before any adapter call. The spawn form shows a note under the provider select for `older_than_tested` and for `broken` (`versionWarning` in `spawn.ts`, one block in `SpawnForm.tsx`). `fleetdeck doctor` prints `warn provider <id>: <version> is older than the tested <min>` and `fail provider <id>: <version> is known to break the protocol`. `make codex-schema-diff` (`tools/codex-schema-diff.sh`) runs `codex app-server generate-json-schema --experimental` into a temporary directory and diffs it against `spikes/s2-codex/schema/0.160.0` (the pinned schema was made with `--experimental`); exit 2 and a how-to when the CLI or the command is missing.
  - Crate layering (R8.3): `crates/fleetdeck-cli/tests/layering.rs` uses `cargo metadata --no-deps`: `fleetdeck-proto` declares no Fleet Deck crate; the normal-dependency closure of `fleetdeck-providers` has no `fleetdeck-deck` and no `fleetdeck-station`; a scan of the non-test sources of `crates/fleetdeck-deck/src` finds no line that names `ProviderKind::Claude` or `ProviderKind::Codex` together with `=>`, `matches!`, `if let`, `==`, `!=` or a `|` arm. The allow-list is empty (the deck has none today).
  - Zero-test guard (R8.4): `tools/check.sh` fails when the Rust lane ran fewer than 1000 tests (nextest `N tests run`, or the sum of `cargo test` results) or `bun test` ran fewer than 300. The floors are constants at the top of the script.
  - Bars (R8.5, R8.6): `tests/perf.rs` has a second `#[ignore]` test (run by `make perf`, writes `target/perf/report-r8.md`): the median turn in a Git workspace (checkpoints) must be at most 10 ms above the median turn in a plain directory, 20 turns each, interleaved; with 15 live sessions, the p95 of `GET /api/sessions` is under 250 ms and the p95 from a follow-up to its card update is under 500 ms.
  - Reproducible board (R8.7): `make board-repro` (`tools/board-repro.sh`) builds `web/` twice into two temporary directories and compares the SHA-256 of every file.
  - Documents (R8.1, R9): `TESTING.md` gets "macOS before a release" and "Rules for tests" (green or revert; never edit, skip or ignore a test to pass; eight fail-open behaviors that must not get stricter). New: `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` (every surface since DL-143 with file, risk and proof, plus the designed surfaces that still need a design review), `DAY-ON-THE-DECK.md`. `PARITY.md` gets a proof column (fail-open and exposure tests) for the night-2 rows, rows for DL-151 and this entry, and the provider version gate moves from deferred to done. `internals.md` gets the board route map (generated by `tools/route-map.sh` from `http/methods.rs`), two Mermaid diagrams (card lanes and effect states) and the tables by migration. `PLAN.md` §10 is reconciled with the 20 station and 12 deck migrations. `FLEETDECK-V2.md` §9 gets the one-line release gate.
- Invalidates: the "per-provider minimum version deferred (DL-131)" row of `PARITY.md` and its deferred item 3 (corrected here); the first-sketch data model of `PLAN.md` §10 (replaced by the real tables). The same commit corrects `cutover.md` §1 and the doctor description in `operations.md`.
- Tests: `fleetdeck-providers` `version::tests` (equal, newer, older, numeric not textual order, deny list wins, unparsable, pre-release suffix, the shipped constants); `fleetdeck-proto` `link.rs` (version status names, unknown fallback, a station before this field decodes as `unknown`); `fleetdeck-station` `manager/version_tests.rs` (an older CLI is `older_than_tested`, the tested one is `ok`, a start on a broken instance gets the refusal text); `fleetdeck-cli` `tests/doctor.rs` (an older provider is a warning and exit 0), `tests/layering.rs`; `web/src/spawn/spawn.test.tsx` (the warning shows for an older CLI and not for an ok one).

## DL-160 — Orchestration for an unattended fleet: `blocked` and `question`, dead-worker synthesis, escalation, wait for any, group mail (R2.1–R2.5)

- Context: a fleet without a human at the keyboard needs workers that can say "blocked" or ask a question, a coordinator that never waits for a dead worker, a wait over several tasks, and mail to a group of sessions (`FLEETDECK-V2.md` R2).
- Choice:
  - Completion kinds (R2.1): `TaskState` is `open`, `done`, `failed`, `blocked`, `question`. `TaskCompletion` is `{ state, result?, reason?, question? }`; `TaskCompletion::check` is the one rule set (HTTP, store and coordinator use it): `blocked` carries a non-empty `reason`, `question` a `reason` and a `question`, `done` and `failed` carry neither, no text is longer than `MAX_RESULT_CHARS`, `open` is no completion. `TaskView` gains `completion_reason` and `question` (`reason` stays the delivery reason). A task is open while its state is `open` or `question` (`TaskState::is_open`): a `question` task takes a new completion (also a new question), and `done`, `failed` and `blocked` end it for good. The same completion again is a duplicate; any other completion of an ended task is `409 task_ended`. HTTP answers 400 `invalid_state` for a completion that breaks the rules and 400 `invalid_text` for a text that is too long. Deck migration V13 rebuilds `tasks` with the new state check and the columns `completion_reason`, `question`, `coordinator_station` and `coordinator_session` (the coordinator session that assigned the task; both are NULL for a task of the owner). `wait_task` returns as soon as the state is not `open`, so a question ends a wait.
  - Coordinator API: `CoordinatorOp::CompleteTask` gets `state?` (`done` when absent), `reason?` and `question?`; the MCP tool `complete_task` takes the same arguments.
  - Dead-worker synthesis (R2.2): in the read-model transaction (the event fold and the snapshot batch), a session whose state is `exited` or `lost` ends its tasks that are `open` or in `question` as `blocked` with the reason `the worker session ended without a completion (<exited|lost|removed>)`. A session that a snapshot batch does not name is `removed`; revoking a station does the same for all its sessions. Only a task without a final completion changes, so a replay or a later event changes nothing.
  - Worker escalation (R2.3): `CoordinatorOp::AskCoordinator { question }` and the MCP tool `ask_coordinator { question }` (class `mail`). The deck stores a `question` completion on the caller's newest open task (reason `the worker asks its coordinator`) and mails `[FLEETDECK QUESTION from <callsign>] <question>` to the coordinator session that assigned the task, as a follow-up through the outbox (mail row sender `worker <callsign>`). No open task: `invalid_state`. A task of the owner, or whose coordinator session is gone, keeps the question for the board and sends no mail. Today only a session with a coordinator grant has an MCP endpoint, so `ask_coordinator` exists only for such sessions (a sub-coordinator); the artifacts wave adds a session MCP for plain workers. The answer is mail from the coordinator, and the worker completes the task later.
  - Wait for any (R2.4): `GET /api/tasks/wait?ids=a,b,c&timeout_ms=` (`tasks.wait_any`, read; 1 to 50 ids, 400 `invalid_ids`, 404 `unknown_task`) and `CoordinatorQuery::WaitTasks { task_ids }` with the MCP tool `wait_tasks { task_ids }` (class `read`, the grant's stations only). The answer is `{ "task": TaskView | null }`: the first task, in the order of the ids, that is not `open` (a task that is done, blocked or in question already returns at once), or `null` at the deadline. The cap is the one of `wait_task` (60 s on the deck, 55 s on the station).
  - Group mail (R2.5): the group addresses are `@idle` (live idle sessions), `@claude` and `@codex` (live sessions of that provider; ended, lost and unreachable sessions are not live). The deck resolves the group at send time (`store::fleet::group_members`) into one mail and one outbox item per session; the mail ids are `<mail_id>-<n>` (`n` from 1, in station and session order). Board: `POST /api/mail/group { mail_id, group, text, delivery? }` (`mail.send_group`, drive, audited; `mail_id` at most 56 characters) answers the list of mails (empty when nobody is in the group); a resend returns the stored mails and sends nothing new, also for a changed group. Coordinator: the MCP tool `send_mail` takes `to` as a group address string; it becomes `CoordinatorOp::SendGroup`, limited to the grant's stations, without the caller. No live session in the group is `invalid_state`.
  - The control skill (version 2) tells coordinators about `wait_tasks`, group addresses, the new states and `ask_coordinator`.
  - Board: the Mail & tasks panel shows `Blocked: <reason>` and `Asks: <question>`.
- Tests: proto — `TaskCompletion::check` rules are exercised by the deck tests; deck — `store::fleet::tests` (a question leaves the task open and `blocked` ends it, an exit or a loss ends the open tasks as `blocked` once, a snapshot that drops or ends a session, a removed station, group members, a group send stored once, `tasks_by_ids`), `store::coordinator::tests::fleet_ops` (group mail for the grant's stations without the caller, class and empty group, `ask_coordinator` stores the question and mails the coordinator once, `complete_task` with `blocked` and `question`, a task of the owner, `wait_tasks` on the first task that is not open), `tests/board_fleet.rs` (group mail over HTTP, blocked and question completions, `GET /api/tasks/wait`, synthesis on an exit event); station — `coordinator::tools::tests` and `forward::tests` (the new tools and `wait_tasks` arguments), `ingress_tests` (the catalog); web — `fleet.test.ts` (`taskStatus`).
- Open items: a plain worker has no MCP endpoint yet (see above). A wait that names a task in `question` returns at once, so a coordinator leaves answered tasks out of its next `wait_tasks`.

## DL-165 — Artifacts backend (R12) and the session's own MCP tools (`publish_artifact`, `set_focus`, `ask_coordinator`)

- Context: DL-155 and DL-156 make artifacts first-class: immutable, versioned files that a session or a person publishes, shown natively, kept in a sandbox and shared by link, with no size cap and no CDN allowlist. This entry is the backend (proto, station, deck). The board is another worker. The same MCP endpoint also carries the R6 focus line and the R2 escalation tool, so every session needs an MCP endpoint, not only coordinator sessions.
- Choice:
  - **Types** (`fleetdeck-proto::artifacts`): `ArtifactKind { html, markdown, image, svg, pdf, other }`, `Artifact`, `ArtifactVersion` (the first entry of `files` is the main file), the link params and answers, and the deck's `DeckArtifact`, share and view types. Events `artifact.published`, `artifact.suggested` and `session.focus`; the fold counts `summary.artifacts_count` (new artifacts only, not new versions) and sets `summary.focus` (empty text clears it). The tag field of `artifact.published` is `artifact_kind` because `kind` is the event tag. Feature `artifacts.v1`.
  - **Station store (V21):** tables `artifacts`, `artifact_versions` and `artifact_suggestions`; bytes under `<station dir>/artifacts/<artifact_id>/<version>/`. A version commits in one transaction with its `artifact.published` event; the staged bytes move into place before the commit. No size cap. A publish of content that equals the latest version (a hash of the paths and file hashes) makes no new version (`created: false`), so a retry is safe.
  - **Bundling:** an HTML page is published with every local file that its `src`, `href` and CSS `url()` values name: one level, below the page's own directory, no `..`, no absolute path, no scheme, no backslash, no hidden directory. The copy opens every component with `O_NOFOLLOW` from a root descriptor, so a symlink is never read. The page keeps its relative links.
  - **Link methods:** `artifacts.publish` (drive), `artifacts.list`, `artifacts.get` and `artifacts.file` (read), none a receipt mutation (ADR-004). A path must lie inside the session workspace or an allowed root; `artifacts.file` returns chunks of at most 700 KiB (`offset`, `length`, `eof`).
  - **Suggestion:** when `file.changed` or `tool.completed` names a `*.html` or `*.htm` path outside `node_modules` and `target`, the station appends `artifact.suggested { path }` once for each path and session (a table keeps the once).
  - **The session's own MCP tools:** a new tool class `Own`. Every session whose provider can take the credential handoff gets the loopback MCP endpoint and a credential in the environment variable that already exists (the same launch handoff). A coordinator session keeps its tools and gets the three more. A session without a grant gets a fleet-only binding (empty grant id): it is not in the coordinator projection, makes no `coordinator.bound` or `coordinator.ended` event, and never reaches the deck. The station answers the tools itself: `publish_artifact { path, title? }` (author `agent`; the path is relative to the session workspace or absolute inside it or an allowed root), `set_focus { text }` (at most 120 characters, no control character; the event `session.focus`) and `ask_coordinator { question }`. A resume, a shim-hosted start, an unverified provider version or a missing endpoint start the session without the tools (not an error). An adapter that refuses the handoff for a session without a grant falls back to a plain start.
  - **Deck (V14):** tables `artifacts` (the index, fed by the folded events), `artifact_versions`, `artifact_cache` (bytes, filled when an artifact is viewed or shared) and `artifact_shares` (the SHA-256 of the token, never the token). Routes: `GET /api/artifacts?session=&station=&q=`, `GET /api/artifacts/{station}/{id}`, `GET /api/artifacts/{station}/{id}/{version}/raw/{*path}` (read scope), `POST /api/artifacts/publish` (drive, relay), `POST /api/artifacts/{station}/{id}/shares` and `DELETE .../shares/{token}` (admin scope and the owner's login), `POST .../view` and the public `GET /a/{token}/{*path}`. Every HTML or SVG answer carries `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts allow-popups allow-forms allow-modals; default-src * data: blob: 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. A share is read-only, one version or the latest, expires in 1 to 365 days, is revocable, and each opening of its main document is counted and audited (`artifacts.share_view`); a revoked, expired or unknown token is 404. The share URL is built from the request's board origin. The owner revokes by the share id or the token.
  - **Cookie and the sandbox:** the board cookie is `SameSite=Strict`, and a sandboxed frame has an opaque origin, so its sub-requests do not carry the cookie. A page with bundled files therefore cannot load them from the cookie-guarded raw route. The board viewer asks `POST .../view` for a one-hour link under `/a/{token}/` (a share row of kind `view`; not listed, not counted).
- Tests: Rust unit tests for the bundle scan, the path walls, versions, chunks, the suggestion and the three tools; an ingress test for a fleet-only binding; a start test for the fleet-only handoff; deck integration tests over a real link (publish, raw, sandbox headers, share, revoke, expiry, owner-only, share while the station is off). The Rust tests run at the end of the goal.
- Open: `ask_coordinator` returns "no coordinator" until the R2 escalation path (DL-160) is wired; the Codex handoff for a session without a grant follows the existing verified-version rule.
- Merge note: `ask_coordinator` is the one DL-160 tool (class `Mail`); `Tool::granted` also gives it to a session's own binding (`Own`), where the station answers it (`no_coordinator` until a plain worker has a deck path). The board CSP gains `frame-src 'self'` so that the viewer iframe can load `/a/<token>/`; the artifact answers keep their own sandbox CSP and send no `X-Frame-Options`.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-170 — Compare the winner of an `Nx` batch (R1.4)

- Context: the owner starts a batch (`3x task`, or several lines) to see which session does the job best. After the batch, the board shows the siblings side by side, and the owner keeps one. `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R1.4 sets the scope; the choice stays with the owner, never a model.
- Choice:
  - Batch identity: `SessionCommand::Start` gains `batch: Option<BatchRef { batch_id, index, size }>` (serde default, skipped when absent; boxed in the command to keep the enum small, the wire is the same). The board makes one ULID for each press of "Start N sessions" and sends `index` from 1 to N. The station checks the reference (`batch_id` 1 to 64 characters of `[0-9A-Za-z_-]`, `size` 1 to 100, `index` 1 to `size`) and refuses a bad one with `invalid_command` before it creates anything. The station emits `session.batch { batch }` in the same decision as `session.named` (not in the start effect), so a refused or failed start still has its batch and a redelivered command adds nothing. The fold sets `SessionSummary.batch`. An import never carries a batch. The ticket batch of R3 may send the same field later.
  - Compare page `#/compare/<batch_id>`: one column for each sibling (the sessions with that `batch_id`, by `index`; horizontal scroll). Each column shows the title, the status, the model, the session diff summary and the changed files (`GET .../sessions/{id}/diff`, DL-159), the last agent message (from the timeline, which the page opens for every sibling), the usage and cost, and the digest line when the summary has one (read defensively: another worker adds `digest`). "Show diff" opens the patch in a dialog. The entry points are a "Compare N" chip on each card of a batch with two or more sessions (a link beside the card link, never inside it) and "Compare batch" in the session menu.
  - "Keep this one": an inline confirm "Keep <title>? The other N−1 sessions are stopped and their worktrees move to the trash; nothing is deleted." Then, for each other sibling, side by side: a live session gets the graceful stop command, the board waits up to 30 s until the session is `exited` or `lost`, then the worktree trash route (DL-150). A session without a worktree of its own is only stopped. Each sibling shows its own progress and failure; one failure does not stop the others. The kept session gets a local note "Kept from batch" (`localStorage` `fd-batch-kept`, no backend). The button is off without the `drive` scope, without the owner's `admin` scope (the trash route is owner only), on a stale board, and when one session is left.
  - Race summary slot (R10.5): a "Summary" section with a "Summarize the race" button that calls `POST /api/assist/race { sessions: [{ station_id, session_id }] }`. A 404 (or a non-JSON answer) means the deck has no such route, and the slot hides. The text is labeled as generated and picks no winner.
- Tests: `proto` (event sample and round trip, fold of `session.batch`, `BatchRef::problem` limits), station `manager::tests::batch` (a start records the batch, a start without one has none, bad references are refused and create nothing), web tests for the siblings and counts, the ULID, `keepOne` (stop then trash, ended, no worktree, refused stop, refused trash, timeout, stopping, throw, gone), the race summary (text, 404, error), the compare page and the chip markup, the route, the batch field of the spawn command, and the session menu item.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-168 — Automations: schedules owned by the deck, precheck on the station (R5)

- Context: `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R5 asks for scheduled runs (daily triage, nightly test fixes, review sweeps) with a cron expression or a preset, a prompt, a repository, a station and a provider instance. The deck is always on, so it owns the schedule. A shell precheck must run on the station, never on the deck.
- Choice:
  - Store (deck schema V16): `schedules` (the fields of the brief, `last_station`/`last_session` for the session to reuse, `grace_ms` default 1 h, and `armed_at`) and `schedule_runs` (`run_id`, `schedule_id`, `due_at`, `started_at`, `state` `started|skipped|failed|missed`, `reason`, `station_id`, `session_id`, `manual`). `UNIQUE (schedule_id, due_at)` makes a run idempotent. `armed_at` is the time since which due times count: it is set at creation, and again when the owner turns a schedule on or changes its cron or time zone, so the time before does not run. A manual run ("run now") has `manual = 1` and never moves the cursor. The cursor is the later of `armed_at` and the newest scheduled `due_at`. The deck keeps the newest 500 runs of a schedule. The migration is numbered V16 as the brief says; its list position decides at the merge with the branches that add V13 to V15.
  - Cron (`scheduler/cron.rs`, no new dependency): five fields with lists, ranges, steps and `*`; day of week 0 to 7; day of month and day of week both set match either (classic cron); names (`mon`, `jan`) are not supported. Presets: `@hourly` = `0 * * * *`, `@daily` = `0 0 * * *`, `@weekdays` = `0 9 * * 1-5`, `@weekly` = `0 0 * * 0` (Sunday). The time zone is `UTC` or a fixed offset `+HH:MM` / `-HH:MM` (at most 14:00); the deck has no time zone database, so a fixed offset does not follow daylight saving time. The title date of a new session uses the same offset.
  - Scheduler (`scheduler/`): a deck task wakes every 30 s (and once at start, so a restart evaluates again). For each enabled schedule it walks the due times after the cursor up to now (at most 20,000 for each look) and looks only at the newest one; older ones are overtaken. Newer than the grace period: the run is claimed (the `started` row is the claim, so a second tick or a restart does nothing) and executed. Older: one `missed` run is recorded for the newest due time, and the reason says how many due times passed. If the station has no live link while the due time is inside the grace period, nothing is recorded and the next tick tries again; after the grace period the run is `missed`. A crash between the claim and the end of the work leaves a `started` row with no session: a run happens at most once for a due time.
  - Run: (1) the precheck, when set: link method `workspace.precheck {cwd, cmd}`; exit 0 goes on; another exit code is a `skipped` run with the last non-empty line of the output (or "the precheck exited with code N"); a timeout, a signal, a refusal or a link error is a `failed` run. (2) `reuse_session` and the session of the last run is idle on the board: the prompt goes to it as `session.follow_up`. (3) Else `session.start` with the prompt, a new worktree of `path` (`WorkspaceRequest::Worktree`, default branch name) and the title `<name> <yyyy-mm-dd>`, with origin `board`. The command id is `sch-<schedule id>-<due time>`, so a repeated send of the same due time returns the stored command. A receipt that is accepted gives `started` with the session id (which is also stored as the session to reuse); a rejected one gives `failed` with the station's message; no receipt within 30 s gives `started` without a session id and the reason "the station has not confirmed the start yet". The end of each run is audited as `schedules.result` (actor `scheduler`, or the owner for a run now).
  - `workspace.precheck` (link method, `admin`, feature `workspace.precheck.v1`, ADR-004 §9.6): the station runs `/bin/sh -c cmd` in `cwd` (inside the allowed roots) with the scrubbed station environment, no input, its own process group and a limit of 60 s; the group is stopped (TERM, then KILL) when the command ends or times out. It returns `{exit_code, timed_out, output_tail}` (the last 4 KiB, scrubbed of URL credentials). It needs `allow_setup_cmd = true` (else `unsupported`). The deck refuses to save a precheck for a station that reports `allow_setup_cmd` other than `true`; an unknown report is accepted and the run fails with the station's own refusal.
  - Routes (owner only, `admin` scope, audited): `GET`/`POST /api/schedules`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/schedules/{id}`, `POST /api/schedules/{id}/run` (claims a manual run and answers with it; the work goes on in a task), `GET /api/schedules/{id}/runs` (at most 100, default 30). Because the board client sends only GET and POST, `POST /api/schedules/{id}/update` and `POST /api/schedules/{id}/delete` do the same as `PUT` and `DELETE`. Errors: `invalid_body`, `invalid_schedule`, `invalid_cron`, `invalid_timezone`, `unknown_station` (404), `setup_not_supported` (409), `unknown_schedule` (404), `station_unreachable` (409, run now), `owner_only` (403).
  - Board: an Automations page (sidebar and More): the list with name, schedule, station, folder, next run and last run; a form (name, prompt, reuse, station, folder, provider, mode, model, preset or cron, time zone, grace period, enabled, precheck); run history per automation; Run now, Edit, Turn off/on and Delete (with a confirm). The page reads the list again every 20 s and after a run now. The folder is a text field (no project exists before R3; `project_id` is stored for it and not used).
- Tests: `scheduler/cron.rs` (next-run computation: steps, lists, ranges, weekdays, leap day, fixed offsets, refusals), `scheduler/mod.rs` (the plan of due times: run, missed, overtaken, once), `scheduler/run.rs` (a fake backend: one run for a due time, skipped, missed, grace wait, run now), `store/schedules.rs` (CRUD, idempotent claim, re-arming, keep limit), `workspace/precheck.rs` (exit code, folder, timeout with a background child, tail), `tests/board_schedules.rs` and `tests/board_schedule_runs.rs` (routes over HTTPS; the precheck and the start over a fake station link), `web/src/automations/automations.test.tsx`.
- Owner review: pending.

## DL-169 — Views at fifteen or more sessions: unread, watch wall, focus line, activity stream, hibernation (R6.1, R6.2, R6.4, R6.5, R6.6)

- Context: `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R6 asks for ways to follow 15 or more sessions. DL-157 fixed the scope: no terminal wall, explore another form first; the activity stream and hibernation were "later, owner decision" and are built here behind a clear seam.
- Choice:
  - R6.1 unread: deck migration V17 adds `read_cursors(principal, station_id, session_id, seq, at)`; `principal` is the audit actor of the caller (`owner`, `token:<name>`; sharing adds `member:<id>`). `seq` is a `session_seq`, and only moves up. `POST /api/sessions/{station}/{session}/read { seq }` (scope `read`, CSRF like any mutating call, not audited: it is a UI cursor) answers `{ seq }`, 404 `unknown_station` or `unknown_session`, 400 `invalid_body`. The cards that the deck sends (`GET /api/sessions` and the WebSocket snapshot and delta) carry `DeckSession.unread` for the caller: the timeline rows after the cursor of kind `message.completed`, `approval.requested` or `plan.proposed` (a caller without a cursor row has the whole capped timeline unread; inputs of the owner are never counted). A new station incarnation drops the cursors of that station (the sequence restarts). V17 starts the owner with every stored session read, so an upgrade shows no unread marks. The board marks a session read one second after its chat shows a timeline end that is live and visible (`views/markRead.ts`); the sidebar rows (also the phone list) and the fleet cards show a pill (9+ above nine).
  - R6.2 watch (`#/watch`, the first explored form): a wall of compact tiles, one per live session (at most 24, one timeline subscription each; the socket holds 32): title, status dot, station and repository, the focus line, the last 4 chat lines (agent text and tool lines, one line each, 140 characters), the running tool, and the answer buttons of the first open request when it is a plain approval and the login holds `approve` (a question or a login without `approve` shows "Open the session to answer."). Filters: station, repository, "Needs you only". Focus: every 20 s it moves to the next session that needs you (oldest request first), stays on a session while it is the only one, shows "Nothing needs you right now" otherwise. Everything is in `web/src/watch/`; no other screen imports it, so the owner can replace the form. Links: sidebar, More page; the phone bottom bar keeps four items (the page is under More).
  - R6.4 focus: `SessionSummary.focus` (event `session.focus { text }`, fold: empty clears) shows as one muted line on fleet cards, session rows and watch tiles. The field and event are declared here with the same shape as the artifacts worker (DL-165) so the branches agree.
  - R6.5 activity (`#/activity`): `GET /api/activity?since=&repo=&session=&limit=` (scope `read`; `since` is Unix ms and returns rows after it; `repo` matches a session's repository origin or root; `limit` 1 to 500, default 100; 400 `invalid_query`) returns `{ items: ActivityItem[] }`, newest first, merged from the stored timelines (`message.completed`, `plan.proposed`, `tool.completed` with the title of its `tool.started`, approval requested/resolved/expired, `session.started`, `session.ended`, `session.hibernated`) and from the `mail` table. V17 also adds the generated column `timeline.at` and an index for the order. The board lists channels on the left (All, one per repository, one per session), refreshes every 10 s while the page is open, and posts to a channel as mail with the existing `POST /api/mail`: to the session, or to every live session of the repository (All has no form). The stream reaches back as far as the capped timelines do (500 rows per session).
  - R6.6 hibernation: `station.toml` `hibernate_after_min` (0 = off, up to 10080). `manager::hibernate` sweeps once a minute: a session is a candidate when it is `running`, `idle` (no run, turn, request, background work or tool), not held by a terminal, not blocked by a revert, able to resume, and its last event is that old. The decision (command id `hibernate:<session>:<session_seq>`, origin local) re-checks the rules, the native resume identity and the executions, appends `session.hibernated { after_min }`, then queues the ordinary graceful `stop`. `SessionSummary.hibernated` stays set through the exit and clears at the next `session.started`. The board shows "Hibernated" on the badge, the card and the row, lists the session with the live ones (after the idle ones), and "Clear" does not archive it. A prompt typed in the composer of a hibernated session sends `session.resume`, then the prompt, repeated while the station refuses it (up to 20 tries, 500 ms apart); the Resume button is the same resume. Any other event of the session before the sweep counts as activity.
- Tests: `proto` (event round trip, folds of `session.focus` and `session.hibernated`), `deck` store (`read_cursors`), `tests/board_views.rs` (cursor, unread in `GET /api/sessions` and in the WebSocket delta, input checks, CSRF, the stream with filters and the limit), `core` config (`hibernate_after_min`), `station` `manager::hibernate_tests` (candidate rules, one sweep, resume), web `watch.test.tsx`, `activity.test.tsx`, `views.test.tsx`, the `App` routes, the timeline notice.

- Board: a session **Artifacts** tab (list, in-place viewer), a global page `#/artifacts` (title search, kind, station and session filters) and a viewer page `#/artifact/<station>/<id>[/<version>]`. The viewer asks `POST .../view` and loads the link in `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts allow-popups allow-forms allow-modals" referrerpolicy="no-referrer">` (never `allow-same-origin`); Markdown renders with the chat Markdown component from the raw route text. A version picker, "Open in a new tab", "Download" and the owner's "Share…" dialog (URL with Copy, QR, the list with Revoke; expiry 1, 7, 30 days or 1 year, because the deck allows 1 to 365 days and has no "never"). The chat shows "Artifact: <title> v<n>" chips and "Keep <file> as artifact" chips (a kept suggestion hides the later event chip of the same version); the Files panel has "Publish as artifact" for HTML, Markdown, images, SVG and PDF; a card shows `summary.focus` and an artifact count chip. The calls reach the views through one React context. `DELETE` and the text read use `fetch` with the login's CSRF token, because `Connection` has `get` and `post` only. The board CSP needs `frame-src 'self'` for the viewer frame (deck change, not part of this entry).

## DL-164 — Integrations backend: envelope, station service, projects, writes (R3 backend)

- Context: DL-152 and DL-156 put GitHub, GitLab, Jira and Linear in the board. Issue, PR and comment text can carry instructions for a model, and a tracker token is a credential. This entry is the backend half of `FLEETDECK-V2.md` R3 (R3.1, R3.2, the projects, the data of R3.3 to R3.8, and the write verbs of R3.9). The board UI is another entry.
- Choice:
  - **Envelope (R3.1).** `fleetdeck_proto::untrusted::fence(source, text)` wraps third-party text in `<untrusted source="...">`, a label line and a closing tag; every `</untrusted` in the text (any letter case) gets a zero-width space after its `<`. The source keeps only letters, digits and `._:/#-`. Every issue and PR body, comment and CI log tail that the station returns is inside it (`fence_cut` cuts a long text before the envelope: 100000, 20000 and 60000 characters). `SessionCommand::Start` gains `issue: Option<IssueRef>` (boxed) and `origin_text: bool`. With `origin_text` the station sets the mode of the start to the most restrictive one of its provider when the board names none (Claude `default`, Codex `untrusted/read-only`), accepts only `default` or `plan` (Claude) or an `untrusted` approval policy with a sandbox that is not full access (Codex), and rejects any other mode with `forbidden`. The deck sets `origin_text` on every start from a ticket or a PR; the board cannot change it. The station emits `session.issue { issue }` after `session.named`; the fold sets `SessionSummary.issue`.
  - **Station service.** `[[trackers]]` in `station.toml` (`id`, `kind`, `site` and `email` for Jira, `token_env` or `token_file` with mode 0600). `crates/fleetdeck-station/src/integrations/`: `gh api` and `glab api` for the forges (GraphQL for the GitHub PR views, REST for the rest), Jira REST v3 and Linear GraphQL through `curl -sS --fail-with-body -K -` with the URL, method, headers (the auth header) and body on stdin. Each call has a 20 s limit, a stdout cap and a scrubbed error text (the token, `Authorization` values, known token prefixes and URL credentials are masked). Jira ADF converts to Markdown on the station. List calls (`issues.search`, `prs.list`) have a 60 s cache that a write clears. Every user value goes into a JQL string literal, a GraphQL variable, a JSON body or an URL-encoded query; none reaches a command line as an option.
  - **Link methods (feature `integrations.v1`, ADR-004 §9.6).** Reads (scope `read`): `integrations.status`, `issues.search`, `issues.get`, `issues.asset` (a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP of at most 5 MiB, checked by its bytes, from a host of the binding; the tracker token goes only to the tracker's own host), `prs.list`, `prs.get`, `prs.checks` (failing checks with the last 200 log lines each). Writes (scope `drive`): `prs.create`, `prs.comment`, `prs.review`, `issues.comment`, `issues.transition`. The five writes are not receipt mutations (the owner chose this): `command_id` is in the params, and the station keeps the answers of the last 500 writes for one hour in memory, so a repeated id with the same request returns the stored `{url, actor}` and a changed request with the same id is `invalid_params`. A failed write is not stored. One write runs at a time. Each write records one `tracing::info!` line (method, target, actor, result URL; never the text). `prs.create` needs a path inside the allowed roots, and a repository with one remote must have the bound repository as its origin. GitLab has no request-changes verb in its REST API, so `prs.review` with `request_changes` on GitLab is `unavailable`.
  - **Deck.** Table `projects` (V18): bindings and prompts as JSON text, no token. Owner-only routes `GET/POST /api/projects`, `PUT/DELETE /api/projects/{id}`, `POST /api/projects/{id}/preflight` (`integrations.status`, a test search per binding, scope `admin`). Relay routes (scope `drive`): `GET .../issues`, `.../issues/{key}`, `.../assets`, `.../prs`, `.../prs/{n}`, `.../prs/{n}/checks`. Write routes (scope `admin`, owner only, CSRF, one write for each request, no retry): `POST .../prs`, `.../prs/{n}/comments`, `.../prs/{n}/reviews`, `.../issues/{key}/comments`, `.../issues/{key}/transition`. Each needs `confirm: true` (the board sets it after the owner's confirm) and a `command_id`; the deck takes the repository, forge and path from the project, never from the body. The audit row holds the action, the target (project and object) and the result URL, never the text. `POST .../sessions { batch_id, issues | review_of_pr, provider_instance, mode?, model?, count_per_issue }` reads each ticket through the station and starts a session: a worktree, the branch `<KEY>-<slug of the title, at most 40 characters>` (`issue-<n>-<slug>` for a forge issue; the n-th session for one issue gets `-<n>`; a review uses the existing head branch), the title `<KEY> <title>` (`Review #<n> <title>`), the project's prompt template (default "Work on this ticket. When done, summarize what you changed.") and the fenced ticket text; a review has the review prompt (default "Review this pull request. Report problems with file and line."), the fenced description and a fenced list of the changed files. The command ids are `<batch_id>-<n>`, so a resend after a lost answer starts nothing new.
- Tests (written, not run by the worker; the owner runs the Rust tests once at the end):
  - proto: `untrusted::tests` (the hostile fixture stays in the fence; a closing tag in any case is inert; the source cannot leave its attribute), `integrations::tests` (binding, key, branch and write checks), the `session.issue` fold and the event kind list.
  - core: `config_tests` (trackers parse, bad entries, the token file mode and content).
  - station: `manager::tests::origin_text_tests` (the restrictive mode for Claude and Codex, refused modes, the issue in the summary), `integrations::*::tests` (ADF, JQL quoting, GraphQL variables, time stamps, the PR grouping, mappings), `integrations::tests` and `tests_more` with fake `gh`, `glab` and `curl` programs (arguments, the token on stdin only, fences, cache, write log, asset checks, status).
  - deck: `store::projects::tests`, `http::projects_routes::tests`, `http::project_writes::tests` (slug, branch, the flagged and fenced starts), and `tests/board_projects.rs` over a real link with a fake `gh` (CRUD, owner-only, the relay, the confirm, the audit row, a review start).
  - link/ADR: the method table test counts 59 rows.
- Not done in this entry: the board UI; a GitLab or GitHub image behind a login (the CLIs cannot fetch an arbitrary URL, so only public images are served); Linear OAuth; the adversary DESIGN REVIEW of this high-risk feature (credentials, untrusted text that starts sessions) is an architect step.
- Owner review: pending.
